# CHANGELOG

## Version 3.18 - unreleased [pending]

### New features
- **PDF files: shown on the site, previewed in the Media Manager and the editors.** pdf.js 6.4.299 (Mozilla, Apache 2.0, in `media/system/pdfjs`, with its font, character map and image decoder files; loaded only where a PDF is shown, about 480 KB compressed) draws PDFs the same way in every browser, phones included.
  - **On the site:** the new "Content - PDF" plugin shows `{pdf images/folder/file.pdf}`, written on a line of its own, as a viewer in the text: the pages drawn as they scroll into view, fitting the width, with a toolbar (previous/next and the page number, zoom, fit to width, full screen, download). Options: Height, Download Button. Inside a sentence the shortcode becomes a link to the file with its size; feeds and Smart Search's index get the link too. Only PDF files of the site are shown (a path inside the site ending in `.pdf`, no `..`, the file must exist); anything else is left out. Without JavaScript the viewer's place shows the link.
  - **The editors' PDF button** ("Button - PDF", next to Image) opens the Media Manager's picker showing PDF files, each with its first page, and inserts the chosen one as the shortcode, in every editor. TinyMCE shows a PDF in the text as a block with its first page and name, and the Blocks editor (Editor.js) as a PDF block; both save the shortcode, so the content stays the same whichever editor opens it. EasyMDE, CodeMirror and None show the shortcode itself. The button's icon is a Bootstrap Icons SVG: editor buttons may now name one (`$button->svg`).
  - **In the Media Manager** a PDF's tile shows its first page, and a click opens it in the viewer (thumbnail and details views).
  - Both plugins are enabled on new sites and once on updated ones, and can be uninstalled (System > Extensions). Hammond, Finch and Rookwood's standfirsts and excerpts (made from the raw intro text) leave out the core plugins' shortcodes (`{pdf}`, `{loadposition}`, `{loadmodule}`, `{loadmoduleid}`, `{emailcloak}`), which showed there as text. Tested in Chrome on desktop and phone sizes: the site's viewer (5 pages, a landscape page, non-embedded standard fonts, Greek text), the Media Manager's tiles and preview, the picker, TinyMCE and the Blocks editor (open, insert, save), EasyMDE's round trip.
- The installer asks which administrator template the site gets, on its last step (Finalisation), under the sample data: Prothero (chosen by default, as before) or Isis, each shown as a card with its thumbnail (the installer's own WebP copies in `installation/template/images`, 13 to 15 KB, as the administrator's folder may be behind HTTP authentication), two side by side at every width. The choice is made the administrator's default template style when the core data is installed; only `prothero` and `isis` are accepted. `core:install` has the same choice: `--admin-template=isis` (`prothero` by default, anything else refused; shown in `--dry-run` and in the JSON as `adminTemplate`). The section's title and description are translated in all 77 installation languages. Tested with installs through the browser (each choice, SQLite) and from the command line.
- **No jQuery or Bootstrap script on administrator pages that don't need them.** `global.js` now does Bootstrap 2.3's dropdowns, collapse (accordions), tabs, modals and alerts on Bootstrap's own markup and data attributes (`CMS.bootstrap`), with Bootstrap's events through jQuery when it's on the page (the same names, `show.bs.modal` handlers included) and `cms:*` events natively; when an extension loads Bootstrap's script, Bootstrap does it all and these stand aside. Isis and Prothero no longer load jQuery and Bootstrap's script on every page: the Control Panel, lists and most forms run on `global.js` alone, about 140 KB of scripts less. Pages whose scripts are jQuery code ask for it themselves (the image field, the Media Manager, Joomla Update). **Extensions that use jQuery or Bootstrap's plugins in the administrator without asking for them (`JHtml::_('jquery.framework')`, `JHtml::_('bootstrap.framework')`) must now ask**; where jQuery is on the page without Bootstrap's script, `jQuery.fn.modal`, `tab`, `collapse`, `dropdown`, `alert`, `tooltip` and `popover` work on the CMS's code. The tab helpers write no scripts (the tab list is built from the panes), `renderModal`'s modal opens its page in a new iframe each time without an inline script, and modals' footer buttons click buttons in their frame with `data-frame-click`. Isis' `template.js` is plain JavaScript now (Prothero's, without its own parts); the error pages load `global.js` instead of jQuery and Bootstrap. Core's remaining inline jQuery (Menus, Smart Search's filters, the toolbar's Popup and Batch buttons, the module, plugin and template style forms' Description links, Templates' Menus Assignment, Banners' export, Messages' options, Associations, the menu type picker) is gone. Checked with a crawl of the administrator (no script errors; searchable lists, validators and tooltips as before) and by clicking menus, tabs, modals, accordions and messages in Isis and Prothero, and front-end editing with Hammond and Protostar (Bootstrap's script loaded). Documented for developers in API-GUIDE.md.
- **Font stacks instead of web fonts.** A new form field (type `fontstack`) lists the 15 stacks of [Modern Font Stacks](https://modernfontstacks.com) (CC0): fonts already on visitors' devices, so nothing is downloaded. Each option shows the stack's name and fonts, written in the stack itself, so the list previews them. Protostar's Google Font options (a font name loaded from Google's servers, for headings only) are replaced by a Font option for the whole page, System UI by default; Hammond and Finch get a Main Font and a Text Font, Rookwood a Font, all "Template's own" by default (their design as before).
- **Scripts at the end of the page: `<jdoc:include type="footer" />`.** Templates write it before `</body>` (Isis, Prothero, Hammond, Finch, Rookwood, Protostar and the core system pages): script files marked `'position' => 'footer'` go there (without `defer`), with the code added by the new `$doc->addReadyScript()` (run when the page is ready). The shared script (`global.js` in the administrator, `frontend.js` on the site) joins them only on pages where nothing else could use it while loading: no script without the mark besides jQuery, jQuery UI, Bootstrap and MooTools, no script declaration and no inline script in the component's or modules' HTML; otherwise it stays in the head. Framework files and every unmarked script stay in the head, so extensions work as before; a template without the tag keeps everything in the head. Core marks its own scripts that start when the page is ready (the components', modules' and plugins' files of the inline-script work, the templates' scripts, the quick icons' checks, CodeMirror's start-up), and the `JHtml` helpers' start-up code (tooltips, modal, tree, tabs, sliders) is ready code. With the cleanup so far, the Control Panel, lists like User Groups, the site's pages (Hammond, Finch, Rookwood) and Smart Search's page load every script of the CMS at the end of the page; pages using Bootstrap's tabs and modals keep `global.js` in the head until Bootstrap's scripts are replaced. Documented for developers in API-GUIDE.md.
- **Behaviour from markup.** Validated forms, tooltips, popovers, searchable lists and keep-alive no longer need a call in the view: when the head is written, the component's and modules' HTML is read and what it asks for is loaded (`HtmlDocument::loadMarkupBehaviours()`). New markup uses data attributes (`<form data-validate>`, `data-tooltip`, `data-popover`, `<select data-searchable>`, `<form data-keepalive>`), which show which elements have behaviour; Joomla's classes (`form-validate`, `hasTooltip`, `hasPopover`, `advancedSelect`) work too. The validator now attaches to every such form, and tooltips and popovers of these selectors work on every page with the shared script, without being registered. In the administrator, every page of a logged-in user has the shared script, every list searchable (`data-searchable="false"` or Joomla's `data-chosen="true"` keeps one as it is; a page's own selectors and options for lists still come first) and keep-alive outside modal frames. Core's views dropped the calls their markup made unnecessary (about 280 in 164 files); the calls still work for extensions and for other selectors or options. Checked page by page, before and after, in Chrome: the administrator's menu pages, edit forms and modals (searchable lists, validators, tooltips identical; keep-alive now everywhere) and the site's forms (identical). Documented for developers in API-GUIDE.md.
- **Email addresses cloaked without a script per address:** `JHtml::_('email.cloak')` (the Email Cloaking plugin, contacts) writes the address in parts in data attributes, put together by `CMS.cloak` (`global.js`, `frontend.js`) into the same link as before; no address readable in the HTML, as before. A page with addresses no longer carries an inline script for each.
- **Audio previews in the Media Manager:** MP3, M4A, AAC, OGG, OGA, Opus, WAV and FLAC files are listed as audio (they were plain documents, without a preview) and open in a preview window with the browser's own player, as videos do. An image with the same name next to an audio file (`song.mp3` and `song.jpg`, `.webp`, `.png`, `.avif` or `.gif`) is its cover: the file's thumbnail in the list and the picture above the player. Without one, the preview shows a small waveform, drawn in SVG and moving while the audio plays (still for visitors who prefer reduced motion). The preview stops when its window closes. Videos take an image of the same name the same way: it's their thumbnail in the list and their poster (the picture shown before they play).
- **Drag and drop uploads in the Media Manager:** files dragged onto the list of files (the Media Manager's thumbnails and details views, and the image picker the editors' Image button and image fields open) are uploaded into the folder shown, two at a time, each with its progress, and the list reloads when they're in; a hint shows where they'll go while dragging. Uploads go through the JSON upload with the same checks as every upload (permission to create, allowed types, size, MIME, executable names refused) and the Media Manager's own options, as its upload form: optimised (upright, WebP, without metadata, resized and with a small-screen copy as set) when "Optimise Media Manager uploads" is on. Files that fail say why ("This file type is not supported.", too large) and stay listed until closed. In the image picker, a single dropped image is selected once the list shows it, ready to insert. Plain JavaScript (the drop upload part of `media/com_media/js/media.js`); without it, the upload form works as before.
- **Cropping in the Media Manager:** a Crop button in the toolbar, enabled while exactly one JPEG, PNG, WebP or GIF image is selected in the list, opens the image in a window as large as the screen allows. Above it, Free (draw, move and resize the area as in the template editor) and the shapes 21:9, 16:9, 14:9, 4:3, 3:2 and 1:1: choosing one selects the largest area of that shape in the middle of the image, which can then be moved and resized from its corners and edges keeping the shape, always inside the image. Below it, the area's size in the image's real pixels, then Cancel, Save & Replace (asks first; the image is overwritten in its name and format) and Save As New (a file next to it with the size added to its name, `harbour_1920x823.jpg`, or the time too when that's taken, as uploads do). Nothing covers the image but the selection. The image is cropped on the server (`MediaHelper::cropImage()`, GD): upright as browsers show phone photos stored sideways, written once at the optimiser's quality without metadata, transparency kept (PNG and WebP; a GIF keeps its palette and transparent colour, and only its first frame), through a temporary file; images too large for PHP's memory are refused. Copies made by image optimisation next to an image (`-600`) are left as they are. The list reloads and shows the new picture (the browser is asked for a fresh copy of a replaced image). The button is shown to users who may upload and delete in the Media Manager (choosing images needs the list's checkboxes, which only they have); on the server, Save As New needs the right to upload and Save & Replace also the right to delete, with the form token, a POST, a file of the media folder (no folders in its name), an area inside the image, and content plugins' `onContentBeforeSave` (which may refuse it) and `onContentAfterSave`. Global.js' cropper gained an aspect ratio, `maximise()` and `setOptions()` (see API-GUIDE.md), and Escape in it now clears only the selection, not a window around it. Tested: the cropping on PHP 7.4 and 8.5 (each format and its pixels, a sideways photo, transparency, refused areas and files, replacing in place, the memory limit); the window with real mouse input in Prothero (light, dark, phone, landscape phone) and Isis (every shape, corners and edges against the image's edges, drawing backwards, Free after a shape, Escape, both saves, refusals of every kind, a user without the right to delete); the template editor's image tool as before.
- **Renaming in the Media Manager:** a pencil next to the name of every file and folder in the list (thumbnails and details) turns the name into a field in place, as wide as the tile, with a check mark to save and a cross to cancel (Enter and Escape too); a file's extension stays as it is and isn't part of the field. The new name is made safe for addresses (letters, digits, hyphens and underscores; spaces become underscores; words with international letters in punycode, `xn--caf-dma` for "Café", rather than losing those letters) and must have a letter or digit; a name already taken by a file or folder is refused, keeping the field open. The list then reloads with the new name and a confirmation; a renamed folder is renamed in the folder tree too, with the links of its subfolders. Links to the item in content aren't changed (the pencil's tooltip says so), and copies or covers of the same name keep theirs. Shown to users who may upload and delete (the list's checkboxes); the server (`file.rename`) checks both rights, the form token, a POST, and that the item is inside the media folder and named without a path. Icons: Bootstrap Icons `pencil`, `check-lg` and `x-lg` in `media/com_media/images/icons`. Tested with real input in Prothero (light, dark) and Isis: thumbnails of every kind (images have the pencil in their name bar) and details, Escape and the cross, an unchanged name, a taken name, a folder with subfolders (the tree's links lead to the moved subfolder), and refusals (no token, GET, `..`, a name with a path, the media folder itself, a file sent as a folder, a name of symbols only).
- **Thumbnail sizes in the Media Manager:** the list's tiles are 20% larger (163 pixels instead of 136); next to the Thumbnail View and Detail View buttons, a magnifier minus and plus (Bootstrap Icons `zoom-out`, `zoom-in`) and Reset Size change their size in 14-pixel steps, four smaller and six larger than the default (107 to 247 pixels), kept for the browser session (`sessionStorage`; the list's frame sets it before drawing, so it doesn't jump) and shown with the thumbnails only. Pictures, folder and document icons and the up tile follow the size (one CSS variable, `--media-tile`).  While renaming in a tile, the save and cancel buttons sit above the name at its end, on a dark semi-transparent plate, so the name's field has the tile's width (it had about a third of it).
- **The Media Manager's list, reworked:** every tile is square and is its picture or icon, with the name on a see-through dark bar over its foot and the checkbox and delete button floating over its top corners: image tiles show the picture across the whole tile, cropped to it (`object-fit: cover`; the preview shows it whole; it was centred in a square the tile's width, leaving a band above wide pictures and cutting tall ones), a video's or audio file's cover fills its tile likewise, and folders and file types have a larger icon (Bootstrap Icons for file types) centred in the space above the name (grid, `place-content: center`; a document's link had an inline `display:block` and its icon sat at the top); the rename pencil sits on the name bar's edge, flush with the tile's side, and shows over the tile under the pointer or with the focus as the delete button does (always on touch screens; saving and cancelling then take its place), and clicking anywhere else on a folder's or media file's tile opens it as before. The tile with the arrow for the folder above is a Back button above the list, next to the folder's path (`thumbs_up.php` removed). Check All Items (it was in the list) is at the left of one row above the list, the size buttons (zoom out, zoom in, and Reset Size as a small link) and the Thumbnail View and Detail View buttons at its right; the page's Check All Items ticks the list's boxes and follows them (all, some, none), and goes with the thumbnails (the details table has its own). The list's frame is as tall as the window allows (it was 500 pixels high whatever the screen), less Prothero's fixed status bar, and follows the window's size. Images open for preview in the CMS's modal, as large as the window allows (Bootstrap's slide-down window showed them at most 300 pixels high), with a bar under them: the file's name at the left, its place in the folder ("3 of 20") in the middle, its pixel size and file size at the right; buttons at the image's sides and the left and right arrow keys go through the folder's images, in the list's order and round (mirrored right to left), the neighbours loaded ahead; the modal's close button takes the focus, so Escape closes it. Image names are no longer preceded by a magnifier icon. Tested with real clicks in Prothero (light, dark) and Isis: tile and bar geometry, pencils, checkboxes and delete buttons hit-testable over pictures, the tile opening its item, icons centred, the Back button, the row and Check All Items (all, none, one ticked by hand: partial; hidden in the details view), the frame's height at two window sizes, renaming in every kind of tile.
- **Redirects, reworked into a general redirects manager** (Components → Redirects and "System - Redirect"):
  - **Any address can be redirected**, not only pages that give a 404: the plugin now sends visitors on in `onAfterInitialise` (site only, GET and HEAD), ordered right after Little WAF (-95) and before the Language Filter and Page Cache, so cached pages are redirected too. Codes are 301 or 302: the component's new "Default HTTP Code" option (301), which each redirect may override ("Use default", 301, 302; the field is always shown, as Advanced Mode's other codes and error-code entries are gone).
  - **Matching** is exact and ignores case, a leading or trailing slash and `index.php/`. An old address without a query redirects that address with any query and passes the query on; one with a query matches only that query (the more specific entry wins). Addresses are stored relative to the site, and the form shows the site's address before the field (a whole address on the site may be pasted). The new address is "On this site" (the same way) or an "External URL" (`http`/`https` only; `javascript:`, `mailto:` and other schemes are refused).
  - **Old and new addresses are handled differently:** the old address is something to recognise, so it's stored in one form that every way of writing it matches: `something` catches `/something`, `/index.php/something`, `/Something/` and `https://www.example.com/something` alike (the request is reduced the same way before the lookup). The new address is where visitors are sent, so it's kept exactly as written, `index.php/` included, apart from the site's own address and folder: whether `/something-else` works without `index.php/` depends on the server (URL rewriting in Global Configuration and a rewrite rule), which the component can't know. The checks still see both forms as one page: `something` → `index.php/something` is refused as the same address, and loops and chains through `index.php/` addresses are found.
  - **Checks when saving** (form, Errors and Import alike): the old address must be on this site and unique; a new address leading back to the old one, directly or through other redirects, is refused ("would send visitors round in a loop"), and one which is itself redirected is saved with a warning (visitors would be redirected twice).
  - **Fast:** entries carry an indexed hash of their normalised old address, so a page asks the database one indexed question (it compared twelve variants of the address); the site remembers in its cache whether any redirect is enabled at all (cleaned whenever redirects change), so a site without redirects asks nothing.
  - **Errors**, a list of its own (`#__redirect_errors`): the addresses which gave an error, 404 and any other code from 400 up (500 for errors without one), each with its hits, last referring page, first and last hit, filterable by code. One row per address (counted up, never duplicated). Off by default ("Collect Errors" in Options; every recorded error writes to the database). "Create Redirect" on a row opens a small form (`CMS.modal`) for the new address and code; the error is removed once the redirect is saved. Old errors are deleted by the "Errors to Keep" (10000) and "Delete Errors After (days)" (30, counted from the last hit, so an address still being hit is kept) options, now and then while collecting and with the "Delete Old Errors" button.
  - **"Do not collect URLs containing these strings or patterns"** (Options): addresses never recorded, one per line: plain text matching anywhere, `*` as a wildcard, `/.../` a regular expression (checked when saving). It replaces the plugin's exclusion list and the two filters hardcoded in the plugin (`mosConfig_` and `=http://`, attacks of Joomla 1.x's time). Its default lines leave out what browsers, devices and scanners ask for that a Joomla site never has: `.well-known/` (Chrome's `traffic-advice`, `change-password`, `security.txt`, app links), `apple-touch-icon`, source maps (`.js.map`, `.css.map`) and other software's admin and debug paths at the site's root (`/vendor/`, `/actuator/`, `/_ignition/`, `/telescope/`, `/autodiscover/`, `/owa/`, `/boaform/`, `/HNAP1`, `/server-status`). Sites updating get the old plugin's exclusions instead, when it had any. The option's description offers a line for scanners trying `.php`, `.asp`, `.jsp` and `.cgi` addresses, not a default, as a site that replaced an old one wants those errors to redirect them. Which requests reach Joomla at all depends on the server: with Joomla's `.htaccess` on Apache, a missing image, map or text file gets the server's own 404.
  - **Import**, a page of its own (Super Users of the component; it was a modal anyone who could create redirects saw): one `old|new` per line with the separator from Options, instructions and examples above the text area, and whether the imported redirects are enabled chosen for each import (Enabled by default; it was an option, Disabled by default); every line is checked as the form checks a redirect (it inserted lines unchecked, duplicates and empty addresses included), and the page reports "N imported, M skipped" with each skipped line's reason, leaving those lines in the text area to fix.
  - **Options in one place:** the plugin has no options left; the component's are on one tab under headings (Redirects, Errors, Import), Permissions on its own. The Bootstrap modal for the plugin's settings and the "Update selected links to" form are gone; a notice links to the plugin when it's disabled.
  - **The plugin is enabled by default** (new sites, and once on update). On update, what a site had is kept where it worked: with the plugin enabled, the redirects that have a new address keep their state and get their addresses relative to the site (duplicates removed), while collected 404s (no new address) and entries with an error code go; with the plugin disabled, nothing was redirected, so the table is emptied. The plugin's exclusions move to the list of what isn't collected (regular expressions as `/.../i`), collecting errors starts off, and the old Advanced Mode option is removed.
- **The debug console as a panel.** With Debug System (or Debug Language) on, "System - Debug" no longer adds its console to the end of every page, where it had to be scrolled to: a small button fixed at the bottom right (above the administrator's status bar) shows the page's time, peak memory and number of queries, with a red count of errors and warnings (errors raised, log messages of warning level or worse, language files with parsing errors), and opens the console as a panel docked to the bottom of the window. Its sections are tabs with their counts (queries, log messages, untranslated strings...), one shown at a time; the panel is resized by dragging its top edge and closed with its button or Esc; whether it's open, the tab and its height are remembered (in the browser), and the arrow keys move between the tabs. A new design, light or dark as the page (`data-scheme`, Rookwood's `data-theme`) or the device says, scoped to the console so site templates without Bootstrap (Hammond, Finch, Rookwood) show its labels, tables and bars properly and the console doesn't change the page. Sections extensions add (`addDisplayCallback()`) become tabs too; without JavaScript the console stays at the end of the page as before. `media/cms/js/debug.js` (plain JavaScript), `media/cms/css/debug.css` rewritten. Verified on the site (Protostar, Hammond, Finch, Rookwood) and the administrator (Prothero, Isis), desktop and phone, light and dark, with language debugging, and with real input in Chrome: open, tabs, arrow keys, resizing, links from the profile bars, remembering, Esc, the list sidebar's toggle, no JavaScript.
- **Prothero**, a new administrator template, shipped alongside Isis: the default administrator template of new sites; existing sites get it installed and keep Isis as their default (switch in System > Templates > Administrator Templates). It has Isis' markup, element positions, overrides, module chrome and scripts, so extensions made for Isis work with it unchanged (only code checking for the template's name `isis` sees a different template); what changes is the look: minimal, black and white, after shadcn/ui's and Vercel's designs. Colours are CSS custom properties with a light and a dark set: the **Colour Scheme** option (Automatic, the default, follows the device; or Light, or Dark) is written as `data-scheme` on every page, the error page and modal windows included. Buttons are outlined, with Save and New (Bootstrap's primary and success classes) filled in the text colour; tabs are underlined; menus, popovers and modals are bordered panels; Chosen's select boxes, the Yes/No switches and the media field match. Colour is kept for meaning only: errors and destructive actions in red, success in green, warnings in amber, published/unpublished states and their selects tinted. The header shows the page title on a plain bar with Joomla's logo in its colours, as in the installer (its wordmark in the text colour, for dark mode), or the Logo option's image, the toolbar and status bar are plain bars. The top menu bar is black with white text, or one of eight other dark colours (the **Menu Bar Colour** option: graphite, navy, blue, indigo, purple, wine, teal, forest green), in light mode only: in dark mode it takes the page's colours, as a coloured bar would glare; its menus keep the page's colours. Light grey surfaces set the frame apart from the content, as in shadcn/ui's sidebars: the toolbar, the status bar, the sidebars (the Control Panel's quick icons included), the title band of dashboard cards, table headers and the settings column of edit forms; the row under the pointer is shaded in every list (articles, modules, users...). The search tools have space below them (they touched the list). On phones the toolbar is always shown as one row of buttons scrolling sideways, with the same fades (Isis hid it behind a "Toolbar" button that opened a column of full-width buttons). The editor's buttons (Article, Image, Page Break, Read More...) stay on one line too, scrolling sideways with fades, next to "Toggle editor" when there's room. Tab rows (edit forms, options) stay on one line and scroll sideways when they don't fit, with a fade on the side that has more tabs; the active tab is scrolled into view (vertical tabs, e.g. permissions, unchanged). The lists' sidebar runs the list's full height and stays in view while the list scrolls, under the pinned toolbar; hiding and showing it works as before, and its toggle stays in reach. On phones the top bar stays fixed at the top as on larger screens (Bootstrap let it scroll away) and the status bar fixed at the bottom (Isis hides it there; the version, copyright and visitor counts are left out so it fits one row), the top bar centres the logo, the site's name and the menu button, and the menu opens over the page (which stays where it was), at once rather than with Bootstrap's sliding animation, with every section already expanded, in two columns under small section headings (without Help, whose pages are made for large screens), with the user's links in a row at the end (instead of opening each section in turn); the status bar's items and the lists' pagination are centred. Lists that can be reordered get a Reorder button in the search bar (the sort options are hidden on phones): it sorts the list by ordering, which shows the drag handles (otherwise hidden on phones), and Done returns to the previous sort. On phones the login page shows the building blocks behind the whole page. Checked on 153 administrator pages (lists, edit and add forms, options, modals) at desktop and phone widths against Isis: no page runs wider than in Isis. The Control Panel's post-installation messages notice is tinted with the accent (left neutral with a black or graphite menu bar), and file fields' buttons look like the template's buttons. One accent colour, a pinch: blue, or the one that goes with the Menu Bar Colour (indigo, purple, rose, teal or green), on focus rings, checked boxes and the active sidebar item's marker; lighter in dark mode. Isis' four colour options are not offered, nor its Status Module Position: the status bar is always fixed at the bottom of the window. The login page has Isis' layout, form and keyboard order, with a panel of light grey building blocks (an isometric pattern with a few pastel blocks, in three colourways shown one after another on each visit, remembered by a cookie: blue/lilac/peach, mint/sky/lemon, rose/coral/violet; dark versions in dark mode) instead of the gradient, the site's name and link on a translucent plate over it, and the form on the page's colour; on phones the form is a bordered card. One script for every page, the login page's included, in plain JavaScript (`js/template.js`: Isis' four files were its jQuery script, the login page's, and two unused leftovers); it hears the events some core scripts send only through jQuery (Chosen, subforms) through jQuery when the page has it. Plain CSS (`css/template.css`, Bootstrap 2.3.2's rules restyled, then the template's own sections; `css/template-rtl.css` with only the right-to-left overrides) and system fonts. Removable like Isis (once another administrator template is the default); existing sites get it through `3.18.0-2026-10-12.sql` (for sites updating from before 3.18), not as their default. TinyMCE 8's sticky toolbar now stops under the administrator's fixed top bar and pinned toolbar (Isis and Prothero) instead of sliding under them (`toolbar_sticky_offset` from their heights). The editors follow the colour scheme: "Editor - TinyMCE" (TinyMCE 8) switches to the dark version of its skin (`oxide-dark`, `tinymce-5-dark`) on a page shown dark (it reads `data-scheme` on `<html>`, so it also follows Finch's scheme on the site), and the legacy TinyMCE 4 editor's toolbar, menus and dialogs take Prothero's colours, light and dark (its only skin has no dark version); the editing area keeps the site template's `editor.css` in both, so it still shows the content as the site will. Stylesheets core code adds with fixed light colours (the TinyMCE options' toolbar builder and headings, the colour picker, the old modal) follow its colours; the Sample Data module's separators and descriptions no longer use fixed greys either. Verified with screenshots of 32 administrator pages and the login page, at 1280px and 390px, light and dark, left to right and right to left, plus open menus, Chosen lists, modals and popovers; an administrator crawl (210 pages) without errors, warnings or deprecations; installing from the migration twice (one row each, Isis still the default) and uninstalling it, on SQLite, PostgreSQL 18 and SQL Server 2022; the install SQL loaded into empty PostgreSQL and SQL Server databases (Prothero the default, the next style ID 13); administrator crawls with Prothero on both, without errors or warnings.
- **MySQL-style identifiers on PostgreSQL.** Both PostgreSQL drivers turn backtick-quoted identifiers (`` `group` ``, `` c.`itemID` ``, `` `#__table` ``) into PostgreSQL's double-quoted ones before running a query, so extensions whose SQL quotes names the MySQL way (third-party extensions often write raw SQL like that) work on PostgreSQL without a second copy of their queries: reserved words as column names, mixed-case columns (`itemID`, which PostgreSQL would otherwise read as `itemid`) and mixed-case aliases (`AS numOfItems` came back as `numofitems`, so `$row->numOfItems` was empty). Only identifiers change: string literals (with backslash escapes, as the drivers run with `standard_conforming_strings` off), dollar-quoted strings, double-quoted identifiers and comments are left as they are; a doubled backtick inside an identifier stands for one, a double quote in it is doubled. Queries without a backtick are untouched (`JDatabaseDriverPostgresql::translateBackticks()`, called by both drivers' `replacePrefix()`). MySQL-only functions and syntax (`GROUP_CONCAT()`, `INSERT IGNORE`, `FORCE INDEX`...) still need the extension's own care.
- **Structured data, Open Graph and X cards: "System - Structured Data & Social Tags"**, a new core plugin, enabled on new sites and on every site the 3.18 update reaches (uninstall it, and later updates leave it uninstalled). It describes the site's pages to search engines and social networks in their `<head>`, with any template:
  - **Google structured data** as schema.org JSON-LD: on every page the site's publisher (an organization, of a type such as `NewsMediaOrganization` or `LocalBusiness`, or a person: name, alternate name, description, logo, profiles as `sameAs`, address and contact point) and the `WebSite`, and the page's breadcrumbs (`BreadcrumbList`, from the site's pathway). Articles are described as their type: `Article` by default, or `NewsArticle`, `BlogPosting`, `OpinionNewsArticle`, `AnalysisNewsArticle`, `ReportageNewsArticle`, `TechArticle`, `ScholarlyArticle`, `Report`, `Review`, `Product`, `Service`, `Course`, `Recipe`, `SoftwareApplication` or `WebPage`, with what each takes from the article (headline, description, its text (`articleBody`, or `reviewBody` for reviews: the plain text the visitor may read, so only the intro text when the visitor's access level can't read the whole article), images with their sizes (the full text image, then the intro image, both when they differ), publishing and modification dates, author, publisher, section, keywords from its tags and meta keywords, word count, language; the reviewed item, provider, category; "Content - Vote" ratings for products, courses, recipes and applications). Categories, tags, the categories and tags lists and the archive are `CollectionPage`s, search results (Search and Smart Search) `SearchResultsPage`s.
  - **Open Graph** (Facebook, LinkedIn, WhatsApp, Telegram, Slack...): type, title, description, URL, image with its size and description, site name, locale; for articles their dates, section, tags and the site's Facebook page; the Facebook app ID.
  - **Large image previews:** `max-image-preview:large` is added to the page's robots meta tag (or the tag is added), which Google Discover needs to show a page's image large; a tag which already says how large previews may be is left as it is. An option turns it off.
  - **X (Twitter) cards**: large or small image, the site's and the articles' author account (given as `@username` or a profile URL), title, description, image and its description.
  - **Where it comes from:** articles from their own title, meta description (else the start of their text), images (full text, then intro, then the first in the text), dates, author or author alias, category and tags; categories and tags from their title, meta description or description, and image; every other page, e.g. a home page showing featured articles, a contact or a login page, from the site's settings (name, description, preview image, else logo). Titles leave out the site's name Global Configuration adds to page titles.
  - **Types per category and tag:** Articles' categories and tags get a Structured Data tab with a type; an article takes its category's (or the nearest parent category's), else its first tag's with one, else the plugin's. There are no per-article fields.
  - **Other extensions' tags are kept:** each family (Open Graph, X, JSON-LD) is only added when the page has none of it yet, so extensions setting their own (e.g. K2) keep theirs. Extensions can add or change properties through the `onStructuredDataBeforeRender` event (the page's context and an object with the page, `opengraph`, `x` and `graph` arrays), which runs before anything is written. Nothing it does can make a page fail: an error leaves the page without its tags.
  - Verified on a News sample site with Hammond, Finch, Rookwood and Protostar, on PHP 8.5 and 7.4 with `error_reporting=-1`: every kind of page (home, article, category, tag, tags, archive, Search, Smart Search, another component) with valid JSON-LD and no PHP messages, the type taken from the category, then the tag, then the plugin, `</script>` and quotes in titles escaped in both tags and JSON, ratings, another extension's Open Graph and JSON-LD left alone (X still added), an extension adding properties through the event, and the plugin's, a category's and a tag's settings saved through their forms; the install row and the migration (run twice) on MySQL 8.4, PostgreSQL 18 and SQLite.
- Resource hints for developers: the HTML document has `addPreconnect($href, $crossOrigin = false, $dnsPrefetch = true)` (a preconnect for the address's origin, and a dns-prefetch for browsers without preconnect), `addDnsPrefetch($href)` and `addPreload($href, $as, $attribs)` (fonts get `crossorigin="anonymous"`, which browsers require). The head writes every preconnect, then every dns-prefetch, then the preloads, right after the title and before every stylesheet and script, whichever extension added them and when. These hints (also when added with `addHeadLink()`) are sent as HTTP `Link:` headers too, which reach the browser before the page and which some servers and CDNs turn into 103 Early Hints. At most 2,000 bytes of them, as servers and proxies refuse large headers (nginx answers 502); `setLinkHeaders(false)` turns them off. Documented, with the head's order of output, in the new [API-GUIDE.md](API-GUIDE.md), the guide to this distribution's additions to the developer API.
- **"Editor - EasyMDE (Markdown)"**: a Markdown editor ([EasyMDE](https://github.com/Ionaru/easy-markdown-editor) 2.21.0, MIT), next to TinyMCE: enabled, not the default (choose it in Global Configuration or a user's profile), removable. A toolbar (Bootstrap Icons), preview, side-by-side view (Markdown and the rendered page in columns of the same height which scroll together; where the screen is at least 1024 pixels wide the editor opens in it, which the "Start in Side-by-Side View" option turns off), full screen, keyboard shortcuts (F9 side by side, F11 full screen, Ctrl/⌘-P preview), word and line counts. Content stays HTML in the database, so the site, modules, feeds, search, Read More and the structured data plugin read it as before, and articles can move between editors: the editor turns the HTML into Markdown when it opens (Turndown 7.2.4 with its GitHub-flavoured plugin) and back on every change (marked 18.1.2, also the preview), and keeps whatever Markdown can't express as HTML inside the Markdown, as it was: elements Markdown has no syntax for (figures, iframes, `<div>`s, `<cite>`, scripts...), attributes it can't write (classes, styles, IDs, image sizes, link targets), tables it can't express (captions, merged cells, classes), comments, Joomla's Read More and page break markers. Content opened and saved without changes is saved exactly as it was. Files dropped, pasted or chosen with the upload button go to the Media Manager (folder `images/uploads` by default) and are inserted as GitHub does: a placeholder while uploading, then images as images, videos and audio as players, other files as links. The editor buttons below it (Article, Image, Read More, Page Break, Module...) work as with the other editors. Prothero's colours, light and dark, and full screen above the administrator's bars. Spell checking is the browser's (EasyMDE's own downloads dictionaries from a CDN, so it's off), and nothing else is loaded from outside the site. Verified: every article of the News, Blog and Studio sets through the conversion in Chrome (313 documents with test cases: same elements, attributes, text and comments, stable on a second pass; only `<b>`, `<i>`, `<s>` come back as `<strong>`, `<em>`, `<del>`, Markdown's one syntax for each); saving articles with YouTube and X embeds unchanged leaves the database identical; typing, uploads (an image twice, a PDF), preview, full screen and the editor buttons in Prothero and Isis and in the front-end article form (Hammond), without errors.
- **Image optimisation** (Media Manager's options, a new Image Optimisation section at the top): images uploaded in the editors (TinyMCE 8 and 4, EasyMDE: whatever uploads through the Media Manager's JSON upload) are turned upright (phone photos are often stored sideways with an orientation flag), saved as WebP (Save as WebP, on by default; transparency kept) and without their metadata (EXIF, which on phone photos includes where they were taken), at quality 90. The same for the Media Manager's own upload form when "Optimise in the Media Manager" is on (off by default: what you upload there is kept as it is). Their size only changes when chosen under Resizing, two switches off by default: Limit the Width makes wider images 900 pixels wide (Maximum Width; narrower images aren't enlarged), and Small Screen Copies saves a 600 pixels wide copy next to every wider image (Small Screen Width, `photo-600.webp`). GIFs (often animated), SVGs, images PHP can't read and images too large for PHP's memory limit are left as they are.
- **Editors write the `srcset`** of an uploaded image which got a small screen copy (TinyMCE 8 and 4, EasyMDE), with `sizes` up to the image's own width (`(max-width: 900px) 100vw, 900px`), so phones load the copy with any template, not only Hammond, Finch and Rookwood (which leave such a `srcset` as it is). The upload's answer now has the stored image's `width` and `height` and its `srcset` sources. TinyMCE 8 drops a `srcset` written this way when another image is chosen for it (it would keep showing the old one). EasyMDE keeps the Markdown plain (`![photo](images/uploads/.../photo.webp)`): Markdown has no syntax for a `srcset`, so the editor remembers it for the image's address and writes it into the HTML again, also for images opened with one; its preview shows the copies too.
- **Editor uploads organised by date:** files uploaded in the editors go into `images/uploads/YYYY/MM/DD/` (Organise Editor Uploads: also by month, by year, or straight into `images/uploads/`; inside the editor's own upload folder when it names one), created as needed, each level with an `index.html`. TinyMCE 8, which uploaded into the images folder itself unless its Upload Path was set, now uses `images/uploads` too.
- **`media:optimize <folder>`** (also `media:optimise`): optimises the images already in a folder of the Media Manager (e.g. `images/uploads`, with its folders) with the same options. Files keep their names and formats (also with Save as WebP on), as articles, modules and settings link to them; GIFs, images already optimised and the small screen copies are skipped, so running it again changes nothing. `--dry-run` lists what it would change; folders outside the Media Manager's images folder are refused.
- Media Manager's options reorganised under headings: Image Optimisation (new, first, with Resizing as a heading of its own), Uploads (types, sizes and checks) and Folders (with its warning as a short notice instead of a bold paragraph posing as a heading); the long lists are wider.
- Verified on PHP 7.4 and 8.5: a 3000×2000 photo stored sideways with GPS data came out upright (900×1350 with Limit the Width on), as WebP or JPEG, without GPS, with its 600 pixels copy (with Small Screen Copies on; with both off, a 1920 pixels photo stayed 1920 pixels wide, without a copy); transparent PNGs (also palette ones) kept their transparency; an animated GIF and a small JPEG were left alone (no copy for an image narrower than the copy); AVIF converted on PHP 8.1+ and left as it is before; uploads as TinyMCE and EasyMDE send them landed in `images/uploads/YYYY/MM/DD/` as WebP; the Media Manager form kept the original with its option off and optimised it with it on; `media:optimize` resized three images keeping their names, skipped the rest, and found nothing on a second run.
- **"Custom Code"**, a new module for the site and the administrator: code of your own (HTML, CSS, JavaScript, embeds, tracking or verification snippets) for a module position, written in CodeMirror and output as it is, with no other options. It's stored as the module's text, like Custom HTML's, but content plugins don't run on it. The text filters of Global Configuration apply as they do to Custom HTML: scripts, iframes and other tags they remove are kept only for users with No Filtering (Super Users, by default). Removable; existing sites get both modules when they update. Module manifests can now ask for the editor of their text (`<customContent editor="codemirror|none" syntax="html" buttons="false" />`, also `hide` and `height`), in the administrator's module form and in front-end module editing (`JModuleHelper::prepareContentField()`); the text filter always stays. Verified in Chrome on PHP 8.5: a site and an administrator module created through the module form (CodeMirror, the dark theme, HTML/CSS/JavaScript colouring, no editor buttons), the code on the site and the Control Panel exactly as written; saved as a Manager (`module:create --as`), the script and iframe were removed.
- **"Editor - Blocks (Editor.js)"**, a block editor ([Editor.js](https://editorjs.io) 2.31.7, Apache 2.0, with its official tools, MIT): content is written as blocks (paragraphs, headings, lists with nesting and checklists, images, embedded YouTube/Vimeo/X/Instagram/CodePen, quotes, tables, code, notes, raw HTML, dividers, and Joomla's Read more), with inline bold, italic, links, underline, marker and inline code. Enabled, not the default; choose it in Global Configuration or a user's profile, for articles and anything with an editor field (e.g. K2 items).
  - **Templates decide the HTML:** content is still saved as HTML (the site, modules, feeds and search read it as before). The blocks are rendered on the server when the content is saved, one layout per block type in `layouts/joomla/blocks/`, which the site's default template overrides in `html/layouts/joomla/blocks/<type>.php`. `Joomla\CMS\Blocks\BlockRenderer` renders blocks for other code too (API-GUIDE.md).
  - **Blocks open as blocks again:** the editor saves them with the HTML, base64 JSON in a hidden element signed with the site's secret (only blocks the site rendered open as blocks; others are made again from the HTML). Raw HTML blocks go through the saving user's text filter, as the HTML does, so a user whose filter removes scripts can't keep one in the blocks for someone else to save later. Content written with another editor (or changed in one since) is turned into blocks when it opens: what the tools express becomes their blocks, anything else (classes, styles, comments, other embeds) a Raw HTML block holding it as it was. Content opened and saved without changes is saved exactly as it was.
  - **Templates' own blocks:** a template adds blocks with a field definition and a layout each (`html/layouts/joomla/blocks/<template>/<block>.xml` and `.php`): text, formatted text, multi-line text, images (uploaded through the Media Manager, with alt text), links, lists, Yes/No, and groups of entries (from a smallest to a largest count, shown in columns), so blocks can be as generic as a grid of cells or as specific as a template's hero. The block menu offers the site template's blocks, with the template's name as a note; blocks of other installed templates still open. Content stores the template's and the block's names, never a path: a block is rendered with the site template's override of it, else its own template's layout (also when that template isn't the default), else a generic layout which writes its values plainly. Their values go through the saving user's text filter by field type. **Rookwood** comes with three: Hero (image, eyebrow, a title whose marked words get its gradient, lead, two buttons, large or compact), Testimonials (two to four quotes in a two-column grid, with names, roles and photos) and Services (two to eight numbered cards in Rookwood's colours).
  - **Preview** (a button above the editor): the blocks as they are, saved or not, rendered with the site template, in a dialog at desktop, tablet or phone width (a frame with the site's origin, so embedded players such as YouTube's play there too; the sandbox keeps it from navigating the administrator away). Its page is a layout too (`joomla.blocks.preview`), which Hammond, Finch and Rookwood override with their stylesheets, theme and article text classes. **Full Screen** (beside it, Escape to leave): the editor over the whole window.
  - **Content Width** (an option, 800px by default): the content in the editor stays at most that wide, centred, also in full screen, so large images and blocks don't fill the window.
  - **Read more:** a Read more block (one per content, as Joomla splits the text there), and the Read More button works as with other editors; so do the other editor buttons (links at the caret, images as image blocks), except Page Break, which the editor leaves out (page breaks already in content stay, as Raw HTML blocks).
  - **Images** dropped, pasted or chosen in an image block are uploaded to the Media Manager and optimised as its options say, with the `srcset` of the small screen copy, and their size.
  - Works next to MooTools, which core pages may load: MooTools 1.4 replaces `Array.from` with one that knows no iterables, which stopped Editor.js entirely; the plugin gives iterables the browser's behaviour back when the editors start (everything else as MooTools expects). Light and dark (Prothero's colour scheme), and Bootstrap 2's input rules, which broke its block menu, are overridden for its own fields.
- **"Custom Blocks"**, a new module for the site and the administrator: a module position's content built from blocks in the Blocks editor (Read more and Page Break left out), rendered with the site template's block layouts. Removable; existing sites get the editor and both modules when they update.
- Verified in Chrome on PHP 8.5 (the renderer also on 7.4): an article of mixed HTML turned into the right blocks (and its classed box, styled paragraph and comment into raw HTML blocks); saved, it was split at the Read more into intro and full text, every block through its layout, and opened again as the same blocks; saved unchanged, the text stayed byte for byte; a dropped image came back optimised with its `srcset`; the Read More button refused a second Read more, as did the block list; an article saved by a Manager with a script in a raw HTML block kept neither in the HTML nor in the blocks a Super User then opened; blocks with a forged signature were rebuilt from the HTML; a Custom Blocks module rendered on the site.

- **Third-party Software in System Information:** a new tab (also in the text and JSON downloads) listing the software from other projects that Joomla 3.x UTD includes, grouped as JavaScript libraries, CSS and JavaScript frameworks, editors, fonts and icons, and PHP libraries: 44 entries, each with its version, the release or build date it states (YYYY.MM.DD), its licence, its home page, where it lives and whether Joomla patched it; groups (headings in title case) and entries in alphabetical order. Only the primary software is listed (Editor.js, not each of its tools), as its version leads to the rest. The list is generated by `docs/thirdparty/build.php`, which checks every version against the files themselves (or Composer's `installed.json`) and stops when one doesn't match, so the page can't drift from what's shipped.
- **Little WAF: hide the administrator's login behind a secret key** (new "Administrator Login" section, off by default): with "Require a secret key" on, visitors who aren't logged in and open the administrator without the key are sent to the site's home page, so bots and attackers never see its login form. The key is a word (`/administrator/?word`) or a name and value (`/administrator/?name=value`, compared in constant time). Once given, it's remembered for the browser's session, so the login form's own requests (and logging in again after logging out) need no key. Logged-in users are never affected. A key PHP couldn't read back from the address (characters other than letters, digits, `-` and `_` in its name) leaves the protection off rather than locking everyone out; a lost key is recovered with `php cli/joomla.php extension:disable plg_system_littlewaf`. Tested: no key, wrong values, the name alone, an array, the right key, the login and logout, a fresh session posting the login form, a keyword with and without a value, invalid and empty keys, and the option or plugin turned off.
- **"CAPTCHA - Google reCAPTCHA": one plugin for Google's current reCAPTCHA types**, replacing "CAPTCHA - reCAPTCHA" and "CAPTCHA - reCAPTCHA Invisible". A Type option chooses the checkbox (v2), invisible (v2) or score (v3, new: no challenge at all; Google scores each submission and a Minimum Score option, 0.5 by default, decides what passes; the token's action is checked too). Each type shows only its own options; the developer options (tabindex and the three JavaScript callbacks, which Google still supports for both v2 types) are grouped last under "Advanced Settings". reCAPTCHA v1, shut down by Google in March 2018, is gone with its themes and its end-of-life post-installation message.
  - Answers are verified with Google's siteverify API over cURL (with Global Configuration's proxy), or PHP's streams where the cURL extension is missing, instead of Google's PHP library (which stays in `libraries/vendor` for extensions using it). Google's error codes become readable messages (expired or reused token, wrong secret key, incorrect answer, unreachable server).
  - Plain JavaScript (`plugins/captcha/recaptcha/js/recaptcha.js`): the invisible and score checks run when the form is sent (forms sent through `Joomla.submitform()` included), after the form's own validation, and the form is sent on with the button that sent it. If Google's check fails to load or errors, the form still goes and the site says the CAPTCHA is missing, instead of a button that does nothing.
  - On update: the old plugin's options become the new ones (v1 and v2 become the checkbox type, keeping v2's theme and size). Sites using "reCAPTCHA Invisible" get its keys and options moved into the new plugin as the invisible type when it's the one in use (the global choice, or the only one with keys), and every choice of it (Global Configuration, the Users, Contacts and Articles options, menu items, contacts) switched to the new plugin; when both had keys and the checkbox one was the global choice, the checkbox settings stay and everything uses them. The old plugin is then removed with its files. A site which had uninstalled "CAPTCHA - reCAPTCHA" but used the invisible one keeps its protection: that plugin becomes the new one. Tested: each type in Chrome on the registration form with Google's test keys (the checkbox rendered and refused unticked, the invisible type registered the user, the score type fetched and verified a token and applied the minimum score), verification with and without the cURL extension (PHP 7.4 and 8.5), and five update scenarios, each run twice.
- **Four new CAPTCHA plugins**, next to Google reCAPTCHA, each chosen like any CAPTCHA (Global Configuration, or the options of Users, Contacts and Articles), and removable. IconCaptcha, which needs no keys, is enabled on new and updated sites (an enabled CAPTCHA plugin does nothing until a form uses it); the others need keys and are installed disabled:
  - **"CAPTCHA - Cloudflare Turnstile":** Cloudflare's free CAPTCHA, which most visitors pass without doing anything. Options: theme (auto, light, dark), size (normal, flexible, compact) and appearance (always, or only when Cloudflare asks for a click). A form sent before its token is ready waits for it; the widget's language follows the site's.
  - **"CAPTCHA - hCaptcha":** the checkbox (normal or compact) or the invisible check run when the form is sent; light or dark theme.
  - **"CAPTCHA - Akismet":** no challenge. What a form sends (name, email address, subject and message of the contact, registration, article and "email this" forms) is checked with Automattic's Akismet and spam is refused, with the kind of form, the visitor's IP address, browser and referring page as Akismet asks; Super Users are never judged spam. Options: refuse all spam or only what Akismet is sure about (its "discard" advice); when Akismet can't answer (no or invalid key, an outage), let the form through (default) or refuse it, the reason logged to `akismet.php`; and a test mode. Akismet is free for personal sites; commercial sites need a paid plan.
  - **"CAPTCHA - IconCaptcha" (self-hosted):** visitors click the icon shown the fewest times. Challenges are made and checked on the site itself through `com_ajax` and kept in the visitor's session: nothing goes to another service and no keys are needed. Based on IconCaptcha 4.0.6 by Fabian Wennink (MIT, `plugins/captcha/iconcaptcha/lib`, its widget in the plugin's `css` and `js` folders). Needs PHP 7.4 and GD (on older PHP it says so instead of loading). Its token is Joomla's form token, so pages served from the Page Cache work. Options: theme (auto follows the page's colour scheme or the device), wrong answers allowed before a one-minute pause, credits link. Its description says plainly that the challenge must be seen and clicked: visitors using a screen reader or only a keyboard can't complete it.
  - Each CAPTCHA plugin (Google reCAPTCHA too) shows its service's logo beside its description (bundled in the plugin's `images/` folder: reCAPTCHA's and hCaptcha's from the CC0 logo collection behind svglogos.dev, Turnstile's and Akismet's public-domain files from Wikimedia Commons, IconCaptcha's wordmark drawn from its website's in Poppins Bold), and the descriptions are short enough to show whole. IconCaptcha is listed as "CAPTCHA - IconCaptcha (no API key required)", its description leading with its being self-hosted.
  - System Information's Third-party Software tab lists the four online services in a group of their own, External Services (Google reCAPTCHA, Cloudflare Turnstile, hCaptcha, Akismet), with the API version used, their terms and the plugin using them; IconCaptcha is among the PHP libraries.
  - The services' answers are verified over cURL (with Global Configuration's proxy, or PHP's streams where cURL is missing) by a shared helper, `Joomla\CMS\Captcha\CaptchaHttp`, which Google reCAPTCHA now uses too; their scripts are plain JavaScript, and a form whose CAPTCHA script fails to load still goes, with the site saying what's missing.
  - Tested with each service's test keys on PHP 8.5 and 7.4 (with and without cURL): Turnstile and hCaptcha (invisible) registered users in Chrome and refused failing or reused tokens and bad secret keys; Akismet's unanswered cases let forms through or refused them as set; IconCaptcha was solved in Chrome on PHP 8.5 and 7.4, from a cached page too, and refused when unsolved.
- Editor switcher (administrator): an editor that follows the user's choice gets a menu above it, "Editor: TinyMCE", to switch to TinyMCE, TinyMCE (legacy), Markdown (EasyMDE) or Blocks (Editor.js), whichever are enabled and allowed to the user. The choice lasts until the session ends, without changing the user's Editor setting. Core components' edit forms (articles, categories, modules, contacts and the rest) switch through their reload task, so nothing typed is lost and nothing is saved; other forms say that unsaved changes will be lost, ask, and load the page again. Editors a field or manifest names stay as they are, without the menu: Custom Code's CodeMirror, Custom Blocks' Editor.js and now Custom HTML's (JCE when installed, else TinyMCE, else TinyMCE (legacy), else no editor), so a site's Custom HTML modules are all edited the same way, whatever editor an administrator prefers. All four editors store HTML; Blocks' hidden block data survives a round trip through the others, so template blocks come back intact. Extensions can use the reload too (`data-editor-reload="item.reload"` on the form; see API-GUIDE.md). The menu is the layout `joomla.editors.switcher`.
- Global Configuration: "Default Editor (Site)", the editor everyone uses on the site (front-end editing), whatever their own Editor setting. "Use Default" (the default) keeps the site as before: each user's Editor setting, else the Default Editor.
- `global.js`, one shared script for the CMS, with the namespace `CMS` (`media/system/js/global.js`): what `core.js` was, plus vanilla JavaScript versions of everything the core used MooTools for: the modal (SqueezeBox), tooltips, tabs, sliders, the tree and the password strength meter. No core page loads MooTools any more: the editors' buttons (Article, Image, Module and the rest, in every editor), the front-end image, user and Versions fields, the Privacy Consent and Terms links, Extensions: Install and the deprecated behaviours all work without it, and `behavior.modal` and `behavior.tooltip` no longer load jQuery either (about 160 KB of MooTools less on those pages; `global.min.js` is 26 KB). The old names keep working and call the new code (`Joomla.submitform()`, `Joomla.getOptions()`, `Joomla.JText._()`, `SqueezeBox`, `jModalClose()`, `JTabs`, `MooTreeControl`...), and the modal keeps SqueezeBox's markup, `rel` options and stylesheet, so extensions' modal links and pickers work as before. MooTools stays bundled for extensions that ask for it (`behavior.framework`). `core.js` and `core-uncompressed.js` were removed (`JHtml` requests for them load `global.js`; pages linking the file directly must ask for `behavior.core` instead). Its styles are one file too, `global.css`, loaded with it. Merged and removed: SqueezeBox (`modal.js`, `modal.css`), `tabs.js`, MooTree (`mootree.js`, `mootree.css`, `mootree_rtl.css`), `passwordstrength.js`, `keepalive.js` (started from the page's options now) and the editor switcher's script and stylesheet; asking for any of them (or `core.js`) through `JHtml` loads `global.js` or `global.css`. Smart Search's old MooTools suggester (`autocompleter.js`, unused since Joomla 3.3) was removed. Also merged, in vanilla JavaScript started from the page's script options (no inline scripts): the lists' shift-click ranges (`multiselect.js`; the toolbar's count of checked rows now follows a range, which it didn't), image captions (`caption.js`), search highlighting (`highlighter.js`; words with characters such as `+` or `(` no longer stop it with an error), the help site field's refresh button (`helpsite.js`) and the site's media field (`mediafield-mootools.js`, which wasn't MooTools). Associations (the edit forms' tab), the Permissions field (`permissions.js`) and front-end editing (`frontediting.js`, `frontediting.css`) were merged too; front-end editing no longer needs jQuery or Bootstrap (its edit buttons and menu item links are drawn by `global.js`, with an SVG icon, so they look right in templates without Bootstrap or its icon font). Global Configuration's "Send Test Mail" script is part of the component now (`administrator/components/com_config/js/config.js`), in vanilla JavaScript. On the site, `behavior.core` loads `frontend.js`, a 10 KB subset of `global.js` (the old `Joomla.*` functions, keepalive, captions, highlighting): visitors' pages no longer get the whole file or its stylesheet, and `JHtml::_('behavior.cms')` loads `global.js` where more is needed (modals, tooltips, front-end editing), in `frontend.js`' place. Polyfills and fallbacks for browsers no longer supported were removed (`polyfill.classlist`, `.event`, `.filter`, `.map`, `.xpath`, `html5fallback.js`, and the unused `progressbar.js`): `JHtml::_('behavior.polyfill')` loads nothing, edit pages with tabs no longer load 30 KB of XPath and filter polyfills, and form fields and the Search module no longer load jQuery for an Internet Explorer fallback. The map of old to new names is in API-GUIDE.md.

### Improvements
- Libraries updated: Symfony Yaml 2.8.52 to 4.4.45 (the latest release supporting PHP 7.1; the Registry's YAML format, tested reading and writing on PHP 7.4 and 8.5; patched for PHP 8.4's deprecation of parameters implicitly nullable, as 4.4 ended before 8.4), the TYPO3 PHAR Stream Wrapper 2.2.2 to 4.0.0 (its latest release, for PHP 7.1 to 8.x, unchanged: our two patches are part of it; `.phar` files are read, other names refused, as before), the two-factor authentication's QR Code Generator 1.4.3 to 2.0.4 (its latest release; the same classic script, which also escapes the image's text now). Symfony's ctype polyfill is removed: PHP's ctype extension, which Joomla's code calls, is part of every common PHP build, and the installer's and Joomla Update's PHP checks now list "Ctype Support" among the requirements, so a server without it is told before installing or updating.
- System Information's Third-party Software list marks software with newer releases as "Outdated" (a red label beside its name) instead of colouring current software green, as most of it is current now. Current: the latest release, the latest of a series still maintained (CodeMirror 5, sodium_compat 1), or the latest release supporting the PHP versions Joomla 3.x UTD runs on (Symfony Yaml 4.4, PSR Log 1.1; the version's tooltip says so). diff-match-patch is no longer listed (Content History's copy is Joomla's, maintained with it), nor the removed ctype polyfill.
- Third-party Software list: Chosen is no longer listed (only its stylesheet and sprite are bundled, for the markup templates style; the searchable lists are Joomla 3.x UTD's own code, derived from Chosen's, credited in `global.js`' header); the QR Code Generator of "Two Factor Authentication - Google Authenticator" (Kazuhiko Arase, MIT), which wasn't, is: version 1.4.3, identified by comparing the file with every npm release (identical to 1.4.3; the file states none). Rows at the latest release of a maintained series (CodeMirror 5.x, sodium_compat 1.x) are green like rows at the latest release, not only their version.
- Isis and Prothero: form fields are wider and line up. Text fields, text areas and lists were 206 to 228 pixels wide by default (Bootstrap 2's sizes), narrow on today's screens; they're now 550 pixels wide on every page that uses the default size (Global Configuration and the components', modules', plugins' and templates' options, edit forms), and never wider than the column they're in (e.g. Global Configuration's two columns, an edit form's settings column). In Prothero, text fields came out 8 pixels wider than lists (its larger padding was added to Bootstrap's width); inputs and lists now have the same width in both templates, as do `span` fields in fluid rows (an article's Note fields were shorter than the lists above them). Fields with a size of their own (`input-mini` to `input-xxlarge`, `span*`), Yes/No buttons and phones (full width) are unchanged; Chosen's search boxes keep the dropdown's width. Isis' fieldsets no longer grow wider than the screen on phones (`min-width:0`, as Prothero's). Checked on Global Configuration and the edit forms of articles, modules, plugins, menu items, template styles and users, at 1440, 1024 and 390 pixels in both templates: no field wider than its column and no page wider than the window. Global Configuration's Text Filters table, whose fields sit in table cells, fills its column instead (its fields at the full width of their cells).
- Prothero becomes the administrator's template of sites updating from Isis, once (update step `prothero-default`): only when the default administrator style is an Isis style and Prothero is installed and enabled. A third-party administrator template is kept; Isis stays installed, users who chose it in their own settings keep it, and an administrator who makes Isis the default again keeps it on later updates. New sites never run the step (the installer's choice stays).
- The Media Manager, the image picker and the image field, and Joomla Update, are plain JavaScript (no jQuery on their pages): `media.js`' three jQuery parts and Joomla Update's update run (its requests are `fetch()` calls) and pre-update check rewritten, with the same names for extensions (`MediaManager`, `ImageManager`, `jQuery.fn.fieldMedia` and `jQuery.fieldMedia` where jQuery is on the page, `jInsertFieldValue()`, `PreUpdateChecker`). Checked without jQuery: browsing the Media Manager (upload form and folder field following the list, views), picking an image in the image field (Insert, the preview popover and path tooltip, Clear), the editor's Image button inserting through the editor's API, and a whole update through Upload & Update (extraction and finalising).
- A plugin's options opened from another page return there on Save & Close and Close (they went to the plugin list): the notices of Redirects, Smart Search, Fields and Field Groups, Multilingual Associations and Privacy asking for their plugin, Action Logs' options, Update Sites, the Page Cache post-installation message and TinyMCE's legacy-options notice. The plugin form takes Joomla's `return` (base64 of an address on this site; anything else, `javascript:` included, is ignored).
- Protostar keeps its code in `includes/helper.php` (`ProtostarHelper`): every page file starts with one include, as in the other core templates, and its markup reads `$page` (the `<body>` classes too, which the page files built in a block of PHP each).
- Protostar's, Hammond's, Finch's and Rookwood's options are on one tab, grouped under headings (Site, Appearance, Footer, Social), as the administrator templates' and plugins' are.
- Hammond, Finch and Rookwood keep their helper in `includes/helper.php`, as Isis and Prothero do (the old `helper.php` is removed on update; template copies made from them keep their own).
- Scripts at the end of the page come in groups: core's shared script, then plugins', modules', components' and the template's, each in the order they were added (the Control Panel's quick icon checks, modules and template script no longer interleave).
- Our stylesheets write a list of selectors one per line (`.a,` / `.b {...}`); `docs/format/` holds the formatters used for stylesheets (`css.py`, `css_ie_hacks.py`) and template files (`template.py`), for reuse.
- Templates manager: the previews of Isis, Prothero, Hammond, Finch and Rookwood show the desktop (1440px wide) and phone views side by side, and their thumbnails the desktop view, square; a preview opens in the CMS's own modal, as wide as the window allows and scrolling when it's taller, instead of Bootstrap's modal with a title bar and a Close button. Thumbnails are shown 200px wide (only a width, so other templates' landscape thumbnails keep their shape).
- Bootstrap Icons and JalaliDatePicker are on System Information's Third-party Software list, instead of licence files beside them (`license.txt` in each icon folder, `jalalidatepicker-license.txt`; removed on update).
- **Page scripts moved into their extensions' own files, in plain JavaScript** (the step after forms' validation; jQuery left out wherever the code used it, except where it drives a Bootstrap plugin, which comes with replacing Bootstrap's scripts): pages carry data (script options, data attributes) instead of code.
  - Menu item form (`com_menus/js/menus.js`: parent items by menu with `fetch()`, the type picker, the Login Form's redirects, item pickers marked invalid with their value); module form (`com_modules/js/modules.js`: from a menu item's Modules tab a save also updates the module's row there); Fields (`media/com_fields/js/fields.js`: the category reloads the form of any component with custom fields, a field's type reloads its form, the label follows the title); Languages (the flag preview, now also when the form starts without one); User Groups (deleting asks first only when a chosen group has users); User Actions Log (export); Content History (the comparison's buttons, `data-history-show`); Templates (`com_templates/js/templates.js`: file trees, the open file marked, the image crop); Extensions: Install (`com_installer/js/installer.js`, keeping `JoomlaInstaller` and `Joomla.submitbutton4()` for install plugins) and the upload, URL and folder install plugins (the upload's drop area with progress through `XMLHttpRequest`, the form token from `form.csrf` instead of jQuery's AJAX setup); Joomla Update (the pages' settings as a script option read by `joomlaupdate.js`; tested with a whole update through Upload & Update); the Media Manager's list (checkboxes, previews, the Delete button, the page's settings: `media.js`; the list's frame loads no jQuery itself); CodeMirror (`js/codemirror.js`: an editor created hidden, in a tab or a slider, redraws when it's shown, whatever shows it); Global Configuration's text filters; Smart Search's search page and module (`media/com_finder/js/finder.js`); Associations' list; the System Information and Sample Data modules; Debug's sections.
  - The batch dialogs' Copy/Move choice (Articles and every list with categories, Menu Items, Modules, Fields) shows through `data-showon` (it was a jQuery script in the language layout, run even where no batch dialog was shown).
  - `global.js` starts what the core layouts carried as page scripts: the password field's strength meter (`data-password-meter`) and lock (`data-password-lock`), and the list sidebar (the template's `toggleSidebar()`).
  - New `js` folders are listed in their extensions' manifests, as are the install plugins' `tmpl` folders and Smart Search's `images` (stock Joomla 3 left them out).
- **Forms validate without page scripts:** 30 administrator and front-end forms (articles, categories, contacts, news feeds, tags, fields, menus, modules' options and the rest, Global Configuration, Mass Mail, the site's article form and configuration pages) had a script of their own replacing `Joomla.submitbutton()` to validate before saving. The default one does it now from two attributes on the form (`data-submit-validate`: validate before any task but a cancel; `data-submit-disable`: fields left out of a save, such as the permissions saved as they change), so those scripts are gone, jQuery with them where they used it. Editors' `save()` code they carried isn't needed: every core editor updates its field on the form's `submit` event. Documented for developers in API-GUIDE.md.
  - The two-factor method's settings (user edit, the administrator's and the site's profile) are shown by `data-showon` instead of a jQuery script; the banner type's image or custom code fields too.
  - Smart Search (delete in Index, Content Maps and Search Filters; Clear Index) and Multilingual Associations (Purge) ask for confirmation through the toolbar's own Confirm button instead of a page script.
  - Mass Mail checks the subject and message as other forms do (marked fields instead of `alert()`s; both are required in its form), and Global Configuration's component options open on their first tab without a script.
  - "Two Factor Authentication - Google Authenticator" draws its QR code from a `data-totp-qrcode` attribute with a script file of its own (`js/totp.js`) instead of a script in the page.
- Templates: `<jdoc:include type="header" />` is the name of the tag that writes the document head; every core template (site, administrator and the system pages) uses it. `type="head"` stays and does the same, so third-party templates need no change (`HeaderRenderer` extends `HeadRenderer`).
- Wrapper: the `load` query parameter is part of the view cache's key (with `Itemid` and the platform's `option`, `view`, `layout`, `format`, `tpl` and `id`), so template overrides and plugins reading it get the page for their own value from the cache; it's reduced to letters, digits, `_` and `-` before anything reads it. Tested with view caching on: one cached copy per value (case kept), other parameters not making new ones, hostile values cleaned (`alpha<script>.x` became `alphax`).
- **Cropping is the CMS's own** (`CMS.cropper` in `global.js`, styles in `global.css`) instead of Jcrop 2.0.4 (a 38 KB jQuery plugin, removed with its stylesheet, whose `Jcrop.gif` stock Joomla never shipped; off the Third-party Software list). The template editor's image tool looks and works as before (a dashed selection with handles, the rest dimmed; coordinates in the image's own pixels for the Crop button), and now also with touch and pen, the arrow keys (Shift: 10 pixels) and Escape. Clearing the selection no longer empties the form's token (stock emptied every input of the form, so a crop after clearing failed). `CMS.cropper.attach(image, {trueSize, onChange, onSelect, onRelease})` is there for any page.
- **Administrator menu:** Media is a top-level item right after Content (it was the last item of the Content menu), and the Help menu was removed (the toolbars' Help buttons stay), in both menu presets (Joomla and Modern); administrator menus built from them keep their items. In Prothero on phones, the opened menu's sections flow in two balanced columns, each following the one above it, without the gaps the grid left beside shorter sections; Media reads as a section heading there, and stays a link.
- **Inline Editing is off by default** (Global Configuration → Site, "Inline Editing": None instead of Modules): new sites write it (the installer didn't write the setting at all), and sites that never saved Global Configuration, which have no such line in `configuration.php`, get None too.
