A value saved in Global Configuration is kept.
- **PHP code style is PSR-12** across the distribution's own code (2,142 files, converted with PHP CS Fixer's `@PSR12` rules; `docs/php-cs-fixer.dist.php` is the configuration, run from the root). FOF and Joomla Update's extraction script (Akeeba Kickstart) are converted too, as Joomla's own code, and are no longer on the Third-party Software list; third-party code keeps its upstream style (`libraries/vendor`, IDNA Convert, phpass, php-encryption, IconCaptcha's library). Formatting only: the token streams of every file are equal apart from whitespace, comments and the rule set's equivalent rewrites, and files with added braces compile to identical opcodes.
- **Date fields use the browser's date input** (`<input type="datetime-local">`, or `date`, `time` or `month` as the field's format asks; with seconds when the format has them) instead of Joomla's calendar script, which was removed with its 35 language files, the Jalali date helper and its stylesheets, and with the older calendar of `JHtml::_('behavior.calendar')` (`calendar.js`, `calendar-setup.js`, `calendar-jos.css`): about 140 KB less, and the browser's picker on phones. The field adds a Now button (Today for dates) and a Clear button; read-only and disabled fields have neither. Now is the time in the time zone the field shows (the user's for `filter="user_utc"`, the site's for `server_utc`), not the browser's clock. The form still posts the date as before, in the field's format (`DATE_FORMAT_CALENDAR_DATETIME` or the field's `format`), from a hidden field kept in step before any `onchange` runs, so saving, time zones and the search filters that submit on change work as they did; `minyear`/`maxyear` become the input's limits. Dates are shown in the browser's format: a browser formats its date inputs by its own language (Chrome on Linux by `LANG`, ignoring the system's regional format and the page's language), which no page can change. So Global Configuration has a new **UI Settings** section (Site tab, after Metadata Settings) with **Date and Time Fields**: *Browser's format* (the default, also for updated sites) or *Site's language format*, where the field is a text box showing the date as the site's language writes it (`DATE_FORMAT_CALENDAR_*` or the field's format; a placeholder such as `YYYY-MM-DD hh:mm:ss` spells it out and the form validator checks it), with a button opening the browser's own calendar (`showPicker()`, on the browser's input lying invisible under the field) besides Now and Clear. Users can choose for themselves in their profile's Basic Settings (also on the site's profile form), with Use Default. The popup is the browser's in both modes (its colours, its language). **Jalali dates are back** for languages using the Jalali calendar (the language's metadata `<calendar>jalali</calendar>`, as the Persian packs set it; the page's language, i.e. the user's or the site's default): date fields show and accept Jalali dates (`1405/08/07 21:23:48`) with [JalaliDatePicker](https://github.com/majidh1/JalaliDatePicker) 1.0.0 (MIT, no dependencies, loaded only on those pages), whatever the option says, and still post the Gregorian date in the field's format; Now gives today's Jalali date in the field's time zone; Prothero gives the picker its own colours, light and dark. The conversion (Borkowski's algorithm, `Joomla\CMS\Date\Jalali` in PHP and in the script) matches ICU's Persian calendar on every day from 1900 to 2100. Week numbers and the calendar's layout options (`weeknumbers`, `filltable`, `singleheader`, `timeformat`) no longer apply. `JHtml::_('calendar')` writes the same field; the old calendar's `Calendar.setup()` turns an extension's text field into one (keeping its name and format), `JoomlaCalendar.init()` does nothing, and asking for the old scripts and stylesheets loads the new code.
- **New error and offline pages** for templates without their own (core's layouts): responsive, light or dark as the device is, in the system font. The error page says what went wrong in plain words by kind (page not found, access denied, something went wrong), with the status code large, a search form when Smart Search or Search is enabled, the way home (and a Log in button for guests denied access), the error's message small at the bottom, and the technical detail (file, line, backtrace, earlier errors) in a Technical details section only in debug mode; it doesn't rely on the database or the language (built-in English when the strings can't be read), as it may be shown because one of them failed, and asks not to be indexed. The offline page is a login page like Prothero's: the site's name, its offline image and message on a gradient panel beside the login card (stacked on narrow screens), messages inside the card; the form keeps its names and ids, with the browser's autocomplete hints.
- **The system templates' pages are core's own layouts.** The pages a template may leave out and the module chromes every template uses were the "system" templates' (`templates/system`, `administrator/templates/system`), folders that weren't extensions: deleting one broke every page with a module. They are now `layouts/joomla/system/` (site: `error.php`, `offline.php`, `component.php`, `index.php`, `html/modules.php` with the chromes `none`, `html5`, `table`, `horz`, `xhtml`, `rounded`, `outline`) and `layouts/joomla/system/admin/` (`error.php`, `component.php`, `index.php`, `html/modules.php`: `none`, `html5`, `xhtml`, `sliders`, `tabs`). A template's own `error.php`, `offline.php` or `component.php` still comes first, then its override of the layout (`html/layouts/joomla/system/...`), then core's; the module form's Style list reads the chromes from there. The pages carry new body classes beside the old (`contentpane tmpl-component`, `tmpl-error`, `tmpl-offline`), which their styles are scoped to, and the viewport tag; the offline page no longer loads Bootstrap. The site's styles are one file, `media/system/css/frontend.css` (+ `.min`): the system messages (what `media/system/css/system.css` was, which now imports it), unpublished items, search highlighting, form validation, the error and offline pages (right to left included), and module preview mode's outlines (`?tp=1`, the `outline` chrome, which wrote them inline and now loads `frontend.css`); the dead rules of `general.css` (Joomla 1.5's editor buttons, the old calendar's icon, Joomla 2.5's image classes, the old caption script's) and its yellow `div.tooltip`, which would have restyled the new tooltips, were dropped with their images. `templates/system` keeps only its stylesheets, each importing `frontend.css` (`editor.css` imports the new `media/system/css/editor.css`), for templates linking them; the administrator's system template was removed (its error page's styles are in `global.css`). The editors' fallback stylesheet for the editing area, `media/system/css/editor.css`, was rewritten: the system font, readable sizes and line length, tables, quotes, code, figures, visible Read More and page break markers, light or dark as the device is.
- **Tooltips and popovers are the CMS's own** (`CMS.tip` in `global.js` and the site's `frontend.js`) instead of Bootstrap's jQuery plugins, looking the same: they're written in Bootstrap 2's markup (`.tooltip`, `.popover`), so every template's styles apply. They stay inside the window: the side asked for (`data-placement` or the `placement` option) flips to the opposite one when the element is near an edge, else to the side with the most room, and the bubble slides along the edge with its arrow still pointing at the element; kept in place on scroll and resize. Shown on hover and keyboard focus (or click, `trigger: 'click'`), hidden on leaving, blur or Escape; `aria-describedby` while shown; the browser's own tooltip doesn't show as well (`title` moves to `data-original-title`, as Bootstrap did). Started once for the whole page, so content loaded later (subform rows) needs no setup. `JHtml::_('bootstrap.tooltip')` and `JHtml::_('bootstrap.popover')` keep their signatures (placement, html, title, content, trigger, delay) and still load the Bootstrap framework; calls with JavaScript callbacks, a template or a delegated selector keep Bootstrap's plugin, as do elements an extension starts with `jQuery(...).tooltip()`. Isis and Prothero give `[rel="tooltip"]` the same tooltips. Tooltips in Bootstrap modals (`.modalTooltip`: the Batch dialogs, the template editor's file dialogs) are ours too, shown above the modal: Bootstrap Tooltip Extended (its script and stylesheet, which only added corner placements for them) was removed, and `JHtml::_('bootstrap.tooltipExtended')` loads nothing.
- **The user field picks users in the CMS's modal** (the one the image, article and menu pickers use, at their size) instead of a Bootstrap modal: `fielduser.js` (a jQuery plugin) was removed with the Bootstrap version of the field's layout in Isis, Prothero and Protostar, and the field's script is part of `global.js` (`CMS.userField`). The list of users' links call `jSelectUser()`, now plain JavaScript, which fills the field, fires its change and its `data-onchange`, and closes the modal; new subform rows work without setup. Fields written for `fielduser.js` (templates' own copies of the layout) open the same modal; `jQuery.fn.fieldUser()` remains as a no-op, asking for `fielduser.js` loads `global.js`.
- **The item pickers open in the CMS's modal** (the Article, Category, Contact, Menu Item and News Feed fields: menu items' options, Menu Item Alias, the Associations tabs, the Login module's redirects, front-end module editing) instead of three Bootstrap modals written into the page for every field (an article form with three languages carried nine). Select opens the list at the pickers' size; Create and Edit open the item's form with Close, Save & Close and Save in a row below it, which the CMS's modal can now show (`buttons` option, also a `title` above the content; both fit inside the box, phones included). The form closes only through its buttons, so the item is always checked in again: Close now waits for the check-in before closing, and Save & Close keeps the form open when saving fails (its message shows). The five fields share one layout (`joomla.form.field.modal`, overridable) instead of five copies of the markup, and their script is part of `global.js` (`CMS.modalField`): `modal-fields.js` (jQuery) was removed, and `processModalParent()`, `processModalSelect()` and `processModalEdit()` remain for extensions' fields, also with their own Bootstrap modals. The fields no longer write a script into the page for each field.
- **The module ordering field is part of `global.js`** (`CMS.moduleOrder`): the Ordering list of module forms (administrator and front-end module editing), filled with the modules of the position chosen and refreshed when it changes, is plain JavaScript with `fetch()` instead of `moduleorder.js` (jQuery, removed). It no longer goes through the deprecated `writeDynaList()`, which wrote a deprecation warning to the browser's console on every module form.
- **`Joomla.request()` uses `fetch()`** (`CMS.request()`, in `global.js` and the site's `frontend.js`) instead of XMLHttpRequest, with the same options and callbacks, which still get the status, response text and headers as before (see API-GUIDE.md); `abort()` and `perform: false` keep working. An error thrown in a success callback no longer counts as a failed request.
- **Subforms and the Repeatable field are part of `global.js`** (`CMS.subform`, `CMS.repeatable`) instead of `subform-repeatable.js` and `repeatable.js` (jQuery, jQuery UI's sortable, Bootstrap's modal; about 28 KB with their uncompressed copies), so repeatable subforms (custom fields' options, the Repeatable custom field, extensions' forms) no longer load jQuery UI. Same markup, buttons, limits and stored values, nested subforms included. Rows are moved by dragging the move button with a mouse, a finger or a pen (jQuery UI only knew the mouse), with a line showing where the row will go, or with the arrow keys on the focused button, which now has a tab stop; a row with an editor in it moves once, when released, instead of reloading the editor at every row it passed. Events keep their names and arguments for scripts listening through jQuery (Chosen, tooltips, the editors, the media field and extensions' fields start in new rows as before), and also go out as native `cms:subform-*` events. The deprecated Repeatable field (`type="repeatable"`, used by no core form) opens its table in the CMS's modal, with Cancel and Apply below it. The jQuery names (`jQuery.fn.subformRepeatable()`, `jQuery.fn.JRepeatable()`) still work.
- **Trees of checkboxes are part of `global.js`** (`CMS.treeSelect`): a module's Menu Assignment and a Menu Item Container's list of items to hide, instead of `treeselectmenu.jquery.js` and the two pages' inline jQuery scripts. The same tree, filter, links and node menus; the +/- toggles can be reached with the keyboard (Enter or Space) and tell screen readers whether they're open; the filter also reacts to pasting.
- **System Information's Configuration File tab lists the settings alphabetically** (also in the downloaded text and JSON), instead of in the order `configuration.php` happens to have them.
- **Unused libraries removed:** Symfony's PHP 7.3 polyfill (nothing calls the functions it adds for PHP 7.1 and 7.2), and the Joomla Framework's DI container with the PSR-11 interfaces it needed (no core code uses the container). They're off the Third-party Software list, and updates delete them. sodium_compat is listed as used by extensions too ("shared"), as the functions it adds for PHP 7.1 are global.
- **FOF's LESS compiler updated** from lessphp 0.3.9 (2012) to 0.5.0, the version Joomla's own `JLess` uses, with its fixes: both now compile the same LESS to the same CSS (0.3.9 dropped at-rules such as Bootstrap's `@-ms-viewport`).
- **Lists are reordered without jQuery UI** (`CMS.sortableList` in `global.js`, instead of `sortablelist.js` and jQuery UI's sortable): Articles, Categories, Menu Items, Modules, Plugins and the other lists sorted by ordering. Rows are dragged by their handle with a mouse, a finger or a pen (touch went through a translation layer before), a line shows where the row will go, the moving row is outlined, and a focused handle moves its row with the arrow keys. Rows stay within their group (an article within its category) and categories and menu items take their sub-items along, as before; the order is saved as soon as a row is dropped. No core page loads jQuery UI any more.
- **The Control Panel's checks need no jQuery:** the Joomla Update, Extension Updates and Privacy Requests quick icons and the statistics message are plain JavaScript with `fetch()`, reading their settings from script options instead of global variables. Their messages' buttons are links, and the statistics message's close button works without Bootstrap's script.
- **Language overrides' search needs no jQuery** and shows and copies strings exactly as the language files have them (markup and entities included, as text), instead of rendering them as HTML and copying back the browser's version; results can be chosen with the keyboard too.
- **Smart Search's indexer and the Multilingual Associations editor need no jQuery of their own** (`com_finder`'s and `com_associations`' scripts, plain JavaScript with `fetch()`). The association editor keeps both items' association fields in step as before; Chosen lists in its frames are redrawn through their own page instead of being rebuilt by the outer page, and new list entries are created as elements (the item's title was pasted into HTML). The associations lists no longer ask for jQuery either.
- **Content History needs no jQuery or Bootstrap:** the comparison of two versions is Joomla's own code around Google's diff-match-patch instead of the jQuery.PrettyTextDiff plugin (off the Third-party Software list; diff-match-patch has its own entry), and the list of versions' Load, Preview and Compare buttons are plain JavaScript in the same file instead of an inline jQuery script (also for front-end editing).
- **Fewer pages ask for jQuery:** fields with show-on conditions (forms, list filters, options, also the site's contact and profile forms) no longer load it, as show-on is plain JavaScript; the list sidebar, the password field's Modify/Cancel lock, Global Configuration's text filter warning, Component Options' first tab, the banner type switch, the multilingual status and System Information modules, and `JHtml::_('behavior.noframes')` are plain JavaScript; the menu type field no longer asks for jQuery it didn't use. The password lock no longer depends on Bootstrap's button plugin and says whether it's pressed (`aria-pressed`).
- **Tooltips and popovers no longer load Bootstrap's script or jQuery** (`JHtml::_('bootstrap.tooltip')` and `bootstrap.popover` handled by the CMS's own code): pages of Hammond, Finch and Rookwood showing the Login Form, and the site's other forms using tooltips, no longer load either. Callbacks, a custom template or a delegated selector still use Bootstrap's plugin, as before. On the site the bubbles take their look from `media/system/css/frontend.css` (Bootstrap 2's, as a base templates' own styles override); its other base rules (system messages, unpublished items, search highlights, invalid fields) give way to templates' own too.
- **The Login Form loads nothing:** its icons' tooltips repeated the placeholders and hidden labels, so they're gone (the icons are decorative, hidden from screen readers); the two-factor secret key's help, a tooltip on an element the keyboard couldn't reach, is visible text under the field, tied to it with `aria-describedby`. A guest page showing the module now loads no script or stylesheet beyond the template's (it loaded jQuery, jQuery Migrate and Bootstrap's script, then the CMS's tooltip code).
- **Keep-alive only for editorial work** (maintainer's decision): forms used before logging in or for a one-off request no longer ping the server to keep the session alive: the Login Form module (also its logout form), the login, registration, username reminder and password reset pages, the contact form, "E-mail this link to a friend" and the privacy request, reminder and confirmation forms. Article editing, profile editing and front-end module and template options keep it. A form left open longer than the session lasts asks to be sent again (invalid token), as any form did before keep-alive.
- **Searchable lists are the CMS's own** (`CMS.chosen` in `global.js`) instead of the jQuery plugins Chosen and Ajax Chosen, rewritten in plain JavaScript (no third-party library, after comparing Tom Select, Choices, Slim Select and two autocompletes against core's fields). They look and behave as before: the same markup and class names (so Isis's, Prothero's, Protostar's and other templates' styles apply unchanged), options, typed custom values (a module's position, the Tags field's new tags by Enter or comma) and the Tags field's server lookup, and the same events and `jQuery(...).chosen()` for extensions. Screen readers now hear them as comboboxes with a list of options. Lists no longer need jQuery: the site's forms (profile, front-end module and template options) load none.
- **Lists' search tools are the CMS's own** (`CMS.searchtools` in `global.js`, its styles in `global.css`) instead of the jQuery plugin `jquery.searchtools.js` and its stylesheet, which were removed: Filter Options, Clear, sorting by a column's heading, the number shown and the marking of active filters work as before, started from the page's script options instead of an inline script. `jQuery(...).searchtools()` still works for extensions.
- **Smart Search's suggestions are the CMS's own** (`CMS.suggest`, in both `global.js` and the site's `frontend.js`) instead of jQuery Autocomplete: the search page and the Smart Search module suggest terms as before, with the same look (`finder.css` styles the same class names), and now answer known texts without asking the server again, skip texts whose start had no suggestions, keep the list inside the window, and are announced by screen readers. Both no longer load jQuery: the search page and the module's own scripts (leaving out empty filters when searching) are plain JavaScript, and the module loads its searchable lists and tooltips only when it shows advanced filters or a button. Ajax Autocomplete for jQuery (`jquery.autocomplete.js`) was removed; extensions calling it get a stand-in (see `jquery.compat.js`).
- **TinyMCE (legacy)'s toolbar builder** (its plugin options) is plain JavaScript: menus and buttons are dragged with the browser's own drag and drop instead of jQuery UI's sortable, and the sets' tabs no longer need Bootstrap's script. It works as before: copy from the source panel, reorder within a bar, drag out of a bar to remove, presets and Clear.
- **`jquery.compat.js`: stand-ins for jQuery plugins core no longer ships**, on the CMS's own code, for extensions that still call them (core never loads it). jQuery UI's sortable (`$(list).sortable(options)`) runs on the new `CMS.sortable`, the drag-to-reorder of subforms made available to any code: items in a column or side by side (grids, floats), dragged by a handle or by the whole item (links and form controls in it still work), a placeholder class or the CMS's own line, mouse, touch, pen and the arrow keys; the item follows the pointer while a placeholder of its size holds its place, as with jQuery UI. Its options `items`, `handle`, `cancel`, `axis`, `placeholder`, `forcePlaceholderSize` and `disabled`, the events `start`, `change`, `beforeStop`, `update` and `stop` (callbacks and jQuery events `sortstart`...) and the methods `option`, `disable`, `enable`, `destroy`, `toArray`, `serialize`, `refresh`, `instance` and `widget` work as before; dragging between lists (`connectWith`), helpers, containment and revert animations don't, and options it doesn't know are ignored, so an extension's script keeps running. Ajax Autocomplete for jQuery (`$(field).autocomplete(options)`, `devbridgeAutocomplete()`) runs on `CMS.suggest`, which gains a page's own suggestions (`lookup`, a list or a function), more request values (`params`), POST, `transformResult`, `formatResult`, `onSelect`, the search callbacks, `autoSelectFirst` and the methods `setOptions`, `disable`, `enable`, `clear`, `hide` and `dispose`. Both plugins' files were removed (`jquery.ui.sortable.js`, `jquery.autocomplete.js`, deleted on update); `JHtml::_('jquery.ui', array('sortable'))` and requests for either file by any of its names (`jui/...`, `system/...`, `media/jui/js/...`) load jQuery, `global.js` and the stand-ins. Tested with an extension-style page run against the original plugins and against the stand-ins with real mouse and keyboard input: the same order after every drag (a list by its handles, a grid of floated boxes, table rows by a cell, drags from a link or an input ignored), the same events in the same order, the same `toArray()`/`serialize()`, options, disabling and destroying, and for autocomplete the same suggestions, choices, callbacks and requests (GET with extra values, POST). Subforms, which now use `CMS.sortable` too, were tested again (mouse, touch, arrow keys, `sortstop`).
- **Reordering lists reports failures:** when a list's new order can't be saved (an expired form token, an error), a message says so; before, the rows stayed where they were dropped as if saved. Saves after quick successive drags are sent one after another, so an earlier one can't overwrite a later one.
- **Show-on is part of `global.js`** (and the site's `frontend.js`): the conditions that show form fields only for given values of other fields (`showon`) run in plain JavaScript as `CMS.showon` instead of `cms.js` (removed, with its Internet Explorer 8 polyfill). The same conditions (`=`, `!=`, AND, OR, checkboxes and radios when checked, multiple lists, list options disabled and Chosen redrawn, subform rows), changes heard through jQuery where it is loaded (Chosen and subforms announce theirs only that way). `Joomla.Showon.initialise()` and `Joomla.setcollapse()` remain; asking for `system/cms.js` loads `global.js`.
- **Site or Administrator is a row of tabs** above the search tools, instead of a small list beside the search box, which was easily missed (a default language set for the site while meaning the administrator, for one): System: Clear Cache, Modules, Languages: Installed, Menus: Manage, Menu Items, Templates: Styles and Templates: Templates. Tab-style links in each template's own tab look (Bootstrap 2's `nav-tabs` classes, without the tab script; Prothero's tab rows scroll on phones), working by keyboard, the current side marked with `aria-current`. Switching does what the list did: some lists clear their other filters (module positions and menus differ per side) and the list reloads. Menu Items' list of menus stays a list. Language Overrides keeps its combined language and side list (it grows with every language).
- **Colour fields use the browser's colour picker** (`<input type="color">`, with its eyedropper where the browser has one) instead of the MiniColors and Simple Colors jQuery plugins, which were removed with their two start-up scripts (about 30 KB of scripts and styles; colour fields no longer load jQuery). The field shows the picker's swatch, the value as text (type or paste a colour, `#rgb` or `#rrggbb`; the swatch follows) and a Clear button (an empty or unknown value shows a crossed-out swatch). Fields with `format="rgb"`/`"rgba"` write `rgb()`/`rgba()` (the opacity is kept as typed; browsers supporting it get the picker's own alpha), keywords are typed. `control="simple"` shows a row of swatches (radio buttons; `split` starts new rows). Read-only and disabled fields have no Clear button. The field's `onchange` and `required` are now written as real attributes (the old layout printed them bare). `JHtml::_('behavior.colorpicker')` gives text fields with class `minicolors` the same picker, `jQuery.fn.minicolors()` (and its `'value'` method) still works for extensions' own fields, and asking for the old scripts and stylesheets loads the new code. As before, an option with a default saved empty gets its default back (Joomla's form handling).
- **Core icons are SVG** (Bootstrap Icons, one small file per icon, written into the page so they're drawn in the text colour: sharp at any size, following the template's colours and dark mode): the Media Manager's file types (PDF, Word, Excel, PowerPoint, text, spreadsheet, archive, audio, video, image, code, else a plain file), Contacts' markers (address, email, telephone, fax, mobile, other information), the Breadcrumbs module's default separator, the content icons' legacy images (edit, new, email, print, checked out), the vote stars (gold and grey), the feed icon (orange), the e-mail form's close icon, the tooltip's default icon, and the administrator menu's language item (menu presets may now name icons as `svg:<name>`). Colours that mean something are kept; an unpublished item's edit pencil is faded. A template's own copy of an old image (`images/system/rating_star.png`, as Protostar has) is still used. Hammond, Finch and Rookwood draw their icons from single files too (`images/icons/<name>.svg`, written in place) instead of a sprite repeated at the top of every page; they look the same (screenshots compared). Every SVG is optimised with svgo. The modal's close button and loading spinner, the system messages' icons and Smart Search's PDF result icon are SVG files too; the image picker's "working" dots are animated in CSS. `JHtml::_('icons.svg', $name, $label, $options)` writes an icon. The old PNG/GIF images were removed, with the unused ones (`media/system/images`, the Media Manager's old uploader and folder icons, the templates' unused sort arrows). Template overrides of the Media Manager's lists that still print `$file->icon_16`/`icon_32` get no image: call `MediaHelper::typeIcon($file->icon, $size, $file->name)`.
- **Language flags redrawn** (the Language Switcher module, language columns, associations, the language manager): sharp WebP images at twice the old size (36x24, shown at 18x12, so they stay crisp on high density screens), from current renders of each country's flag (svg-country-flags), regional flags from flag-icons (Catalonia, the Basque Country, Galicia, the Arab League, the East African Community) and public domain flags from Wikimedia Commons (Esperanto, Kurdistan, Quebec, the Assyrian flag); Brittany and the Sinhala, Tamil and Uyghur script names redrawn. Each flag keeps its own proportions, centred in the same box; North Macedonia shows its current flag. 66 KB for all 162 (lossless WebP was smaller than PNG). The GIFs were removed: asking for a flag as `.gif` (template overrides of the switcher, language packs) loads the WebP, a language pack's own GIF is still used when there's no WebP of that name, names are taken in lower case (`en_GB` finds `en_gb`), and flags are given their 18x12 size unless the caller gives one. The flags moved to `media/system/images/flags/` (relative name `system/flags/<name>.webp`), as both the site and the administrator show them: old names (`mod_languages/<name>.gif`, `media/mod_languages/images/...`) load them from there, and flags language packs installed into `media/mod_languages/images/` are still found when the new folder has none of that name. The switcher's stylesheet (`media/mod_languages/css/languages.css`, six rules) is part of `global.css` now, which the module loads; asking for the old file loads `global.css`. Sources and licences: `media/system/images/flags/credits.txt`; the build script: `docs/flags/build.py`.
- Form validation (`behavior.formvalidator`: required fields, email, username, password, numeric, patterns, custom handlers) without jQuery: `validate.js` is part of `global.js` and of the site's `frontend.js` now (`CMS.formValidator`, still `document.formvalidator` with the same methods), so forms for visitors (contact, login, registration) no longer load jQuery for it; `validate.js` was removed (asking for it starts the behaviour). `document.formvalidator` exists as soon as the page's scripts have run, so extensions can add handlers at any time; their handlers still get the field as a jQuery object where jQuery is loaded.
- The switcher (`behavior.switcher`) and the combo box (`behavior.combobox`, the `combo` form field) without jQuery: both are part of `global.js` now (`CMS.switcher`, `CMS.combobox`), and `switcher.js` and `combobox.js` were removed (asking for them starts the behaviours; `JSwitcher` and `jQuery.fn.ComboTransform` still work). The switcher's `onShow` and `onHide` callbacks are now called the right way round (stock Joomla called `onShow` when hiding a page) and get the page element.
- Edit forms remember their open tab (`behavior.tabstate`) without jQuery: the code is part of `global.js` now (`CMS.tabState`), and `tabs-state.js` was removed (asking for it starts the behaviour). Tab sets for which nothing was remembered now open their first tab: when one set was remembered, or the address named a tab that isn't on the page, the others showed no open tab (stock Joomla 3).
- Restricted content tells guests to "Log in to access content ..." instead of "Register to read more ...", as registration is closed on new sites: the read-more link of articles visitors may see but not read (all templates, category lists) and the Articles - Category and Articles - Newsflash modules.
- `LICENSE.md` removed: `LICENSE.txt` (identical) is the licence file, and the only one the release package ever carried (the package leaves out Markdown files). Updates remove a `LICENSE.md` older packages may have left.
- Post-installation Messages no longer shows joomla.org's release news (stock Joomla's announcements, through the administrator Feed module) beside the messages: the messages are one centred column (wider; full width on phones). The "Showing messages for" selector appears only when another extension has added messages of its own (as some do), not on sites where only Joomla has any.
- **SimplePie 1.9.0** (from Joomla's 1.3.3 fork): the RSS/Atom library extensions get through the deprecated `JFactory::getFeedParser()` (`JSimplepieFactory`) or use directly; core's own feeds (the Feed modules, News Feeds) use Joomla's parser and don't need it. SimplePie 1.9 needs PHP 7.2: on PHP 7.1, where its constructor would end the request, `getFeedParser()` returns `false` (its failure result) and logs why. The old class names (`SimplePie`, `SimplePie_Item`, ...) remain as aliases. Two fixes upstream made after 1.9.0 for PHP 8.5 are backported. Tested reading RSS 2.0 and Atom on PHP 8.5, 8.4 and 7.4 (titles, links, dates, authors, enclosures, sanitised descriptions), through `getFeedParser()` and directly. SimplePie, TinyMCE (legacy) and other leftovers are planned for removal in a later version.
- **Updates within their series:** sodium_compat 1.24.2 (from 1.17.1; Joomla 3.x UTD's PHP 8 casts reapplied, the PHP 8.4 fixes now upstream's own).
- **SQLite driver: MySQL on SQLite 3.1.1** (from 3.0.2), with Joomla's translator patches reapplied (`libraries/vendor/wordpress/mysql-on-sqlite/PATCHES.md`). Upstream fixed `CREATE TEMPORARY TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` and `DROP TEMPORARY TABLE` as a connection's first temporary table (3.0.2 failed with "table `_wp_sqlite_mysql_information_schema_tmp_tables` doesn't exist"), `ROLLBACK` as the first transaction, `FOUND_ROWS()`, foreign keys and `IF()` (now translated to `CASE WHEN`), and rewrote date arithmetic so negative intervals work and `DATE_SUB()` of a date returns a date; Joomla's own patch for negative intervals was dropped. Tested by comparing 3.0.2 and 3.1.1 on every patched case and the changed areas (PHP 8.5 and 7.4), a fresh install with sample data, 41 site pages, the administrator's lists, the schema check, Smart Search indexing and a database export.
- **Three unused libraries removed:** HTML5 Shiv (only Internet Explorer 8 and older needed it; the system templates no longer load it, and templates still asking for `jui/html5.js` get nothing, without errors), Google's reCAPTCHA PHP client (with Joomla's bridge class for it; the reCAPTCHA plugin verifies over cURL since 3.18) and the "phputf8" library (`libraries/phputf8`, a copy nothing used: Joomla's string functions bring their own, which stays; legacy `JApplication::redirect()` checks for non-ASCII addresses with `preg_match()` instead). Their files are deleted on update, and the library's entry is removed from Extensions.
- Unused files removed from `media/system`: IcoMoon's design sources (`IcoMoon.dev.svg`, `IcoMoon.dev.commented.svg`), its Internet Explorer 7 script (`icomoon-lte-ie7.js`) and its EOT and SVG font files, which only Internet Explorer and very old Safari read (the templates' and `icomoon.css`' font rules now name WOFF and TrueType only), the old colour picker's `hue.png` and `saturation.png`, and Chosen's `chosen-sprite@2x.png`, which its stylesheet never used. About 315 KB.
- **Third-party Software: versions at a glance.** Every version is in bold, and current ones are green, the whole row: the latest release, or the latest release of a series still maintained (a release within a year), such as CodeMirror 5.65.21 next to CodeMirror 6, whose tooltip says so. Other versions' tooltips add the latest release of their own series where it differs (e.g. sodium_compat 1.17.1: "Latest 1.x: 1.24.2"), which shows the updates possible without a new major version. Libraries bundled for one of Joomla 3.x UTD's own extensions are listed as its sub-packages (marked and Turndown: "Sub-package of EasyMDE"), and icons bundled as images or SVG are no longer listed (Bootstrap Icons, used as a few SVG masks by EasyMDE); icon fonts are.
- **Third-party Software: what extensions may rely on.** A "Used by" column says whether only Joomla 3.x UTD's own code uses each item ("Core only": e.g. phpass, the Symfony Yaml and PSR classes, the SQLite package, the CAPTCHA services) or extensions may use it too ("Core and extensions": the front-end libraries loaded through `JHtml` such as jQuery, Bootstrap, MooTools and SqueezeBox, the editors, the IcoMoon icon classes, FOF, lessphp, PHPMailer, SimplePie and the PHP polyfills), so it's clear which replacements and updates need more care. A judgement rather than an audit: 26 of 45 are shared. On phones the column replaces Licence.
- **Third-party Software: who patched what.** Notes credit stock Joomla's changes to "Joomla" and this distribution's to "Joomla 3.x UTD" (e.g. PHP 8 fixes in phpass, sodium_compat, Symfony Yaml, SimplePie and IDNA Convert; the SQLite package's translator patches; PHPMailer's `php_uname()` guard), as found by comparing each library with the Joomla 3.10.20 files this distribution started from. marked, Turndown and Bootstrap Icons are noted as part of the EasyMDE editor (Joomla 3.x UTD bundles them for it; they're separate projects with their own releases).
- **Third-party Software: what's at its latest release.** Rows at their project's latest release are green (the online services too, as the plugins use their current APIs); for the others, pointing at the version shows the latest release and when that was checked. The latest versions come from npm, Packagist and GitHub (releases, else tags), asked by `php docs/thirdparty/build.php --latest`, which keeps them in `docs/thirdparty/latest.json` for the regular build (no network needed). Copies Joomla adapted and kept, and FOF (whose upstream repository is gone), have nothing to compare with. At this check, 12 of 46 are at their latest release; marked (18.1.2), TinyMCE (8.10.0) and MySQL on SQLite (3.1.1) have just had releases newer than the bundled ones.
- **Third-party Software: a release date for everything bundled.** Where the software states no date, it was looked up: the project's GitHub releases and tags (annotated tags' own dates), npm and Packagist (cross-checked), release announcements (Bootstrap 2.3.2's blog post; its GitHub release record dates from two months later), SourceForge (PHP UTF-8) and Openwall (phpass); for copies Joomla adapted and kept (the IcoMoon font, SqueezeBox, Simple Colors, Akeeba Restore), Joomla's last change to them. Files without a version were identified by comparing them with every upstream release: jQuery MiniColors is 2.2.4 (identical), PHP Encryption 1.1 (with PHP 8 casts added), Ajax Chosen an untagged upstream commit of January 2013. Pointing at a date shows its source. The oldest (MooTools More 1.4.0.1 of 2011, PHP UTF-8 0.5 of 2007, MooTools Core 1.4.5, Ajax Chosen, Bootstrap 2.3.2, IcoMoon) are where updating or replacing is most due.
- **PHPMailer 7.1.1** (May 2026) replaces 5.2.28, the last of a series that ended in 2020 (Joomla 3 used a backports fork of it). PHPMailer 7 needs PHP 5.5, so it runs on every PHP version Joomla 3.x UTD supports; it brings years of fixes, including 7.1's hardening of headers and properties against injection. Joomla's mailer (`JMail`) works as before, and so do extensions using PHPMailer's old global class names: `PHPMailer`, `phpmailerException`, `SMTP` and `POP3` are aliases of the namespaced classes (`libraries/src/Mail/phpmailer-aliases.php`, through Composer's class map), so `new PHPMailer`, `extends PHPMailer` and `catch (phpmailerException $e)` keep working. Mail errors stay in the site's language: PHPMailer 7 reads language files as plain text, so Joomla's mailer now sets its `PHPMAILER_*` strings directly instead of through a language file. Left out: 5.2's OAuth classes (they needed a Google OAuth library Joomla never bundled), the `EasyPeasyICS` and NTLM extras, and 7's `get_oauth_token.php` (a web page). SMTP's NTLM login, which 5.2 chose only when a server offered none of CRAM-MD5, LOGIN and PLAIN, is gone. One local patch, a guard on `php_uname()` (often disabled by hosts; fatal on PHP 8). Tested on PHP 8.5 and 7.4: sending through PHP's `mail()` (UTF-8 subject, HTML) and SMTP with authentication, CC and an attachment, refused credentials and a closed port (Joomla's messages), an invalid address caught as `phpmailerException`, and extension-style code using the old class names.
- Statistics modules (site and administrator, "System Information" in the Control Panel): on SQLite sites the database version shows on two lines, the SQLite version and then "(MySQL ... emulation)" (language string `MOD_STATS_SQLITE_VERSION`), instead of wrapping mid-phrase in narrow columns.
- The CAPTCHA selectors (Global Configuration, and the options of Users, Contacts and Articles) list every installed CAPTCHA plugin, alphabetically by name: disabled ones follow the enabled ones, marked "(disabled: enable it in Plugins)" and not selectable, instead of being missing from a list that was empty on new sites. A disabled plugin already chosen stays selected, so saving keeps it. Opt-in for other plugin lists: `showdisabled="true"` on a `plugins` field.
- `core:install` reports its progress more clearly: the title says "Installing Joomla 3.x UTD... Hold tight!", the steps are numbered (3 or 4, with sample data), and the result says how long the installation took ("Joomla installation completed in 3.4 seconds for "My Site", using the SQLite database in database/joomla-....sqlite."), with blank lines between the steps, the result, the administrator's details and the closing note. JSON returns the time as `seconds`. A dry run's title stays plain ("Install Joomla 3.x UTD"). A blank line first keeps it apart from whatever the shell showed before. In a terminal, every command's messages (`[OK]`, `[WARNING]`, `[ERROR]` and notes) now wrap at spaces to the terminal's width (`COLUMNS`, else `stty size`) instead of the terminal breaking words in two; piped or redirected output, JSON and the MCP server are unchanged. Measured on SQLite: about 1.5 seconds without sample data, 3.5 with the News set.
- System Information: the PHP Settings tab sits next to PHP Information (the tabs run System Information, Configuration File, Folder Permissions, Third-party Software, PHP Settings, PHP Information), and Folder Permissions lists the folders alphabetically instead of by kind. The Log, Temp and Cache folders set in Global Configuration are shown relative to the site (`administrator/logs`, `tmp`) when they're inside it, as the other folders are; only folders outside the site keep their full path.
- **Larger modals for picking images and content:** the image picker (the editors' Image button and image fields) and the editors' Article, Module, Menu, Contact and Fields buttons opened 800 by 500 pixels on any screen; they now fill most of the window (up to 1400 by 900; in Isis, Prothero and Protostar the image field's picker is as tall as the window allows, up to 900 pixels). Page Break's small form keeps its size. On phones and small tablets they take the whole screen, 20 pixels from its edges (see Bug fixes).
- **Page Cache: times of its own, stale pages and browser caching kept apart.** Its options, all on one tab under Page Cache (on the server), Browser Caching and Exclusions ("Exclude URLs" was alone on an Advanced tab):
  - **Cache times for the home page and for other pages** (seconds; 60 and 180 by default, also on existing sites, whose pages were kept for Global Configuration's Cache Time, 15 minutes by default; 0: that Cache Time), separate from the time the caches of components and modules keep. Each page is stored with the moment it stops being fresh (decided when it's rendered, when the plugin knows whether it's the home page: the home menu item's own link, also each language's, not every page Joomla shows with the home item active), so every cache handler applies it the same way.
  - **Serve Stale Pages For** (seconds, empty by default: off): a page past its cache time can still be sent for that long. While one request renders the fresh copy (marked with a short-lived refresh entry, holding the page's lock), every other visitor gets the previous copy at once instead of waiting for it or rendering it too, so a busy page doesn't slow down when its time ends. When rendering it fails with a server error, the previous copy is sent instead of the error page (with a 200; the error still goes to PHP's error log; 4xx errors such as a deleted article's 404 are never hidden). Measured with a page taking 2 s to render: the refreshing request took 2.16 s, three visitors arriving with it got the previous copy in 21 to 30 ms; with rendering failing, every visitor got the previous copy in about 40 ms. A refresh that never finishes stops counting after 30 seconds. A database outage isn't covered: plugins need the database to load.
  - **Browser caching is its own choice:** "Let Browsers Keep Pages" (off by default) sends a cache time with the pages the page cache sends (`Cache-Control: max-age`, with a matching `Expires`)., one for the home page and one for other pages (seconds, 60 and 180 by default), also on cache hits. Off, browsers ask for every page, so they always show the latest one: a news site can keep pages on the server while browsers never show an old copy (with a time, e.g. mobile Chrome shows its copy when a tab becomes active again). The plugin never let browsers keep a page before: even with "Use Browser Caching" (now "Not Modified Replies (304)", which only answers a browser's question with a 304), cached pages went out as `no-cache` with an `Expires` of 2005.
  - **Advanced Caching (Varnish, CDNs):** shared cache times for the home page and for other pages (`s-maxage`, empty by default: not sent), apart from the browser times. With browsers not keeping pages, pages then go out as `max-age=0, s-maxage=N`: browsers always fetch the latest copy, from the cache in front of the site. With any cache time (browser or shared), `stale-while-revalidate` (as long as the longest time sent by default, so each kind of page gets its own; 0 leaves it out) and `stale-if-error` (3600 seconds by default) let those caches keep serving a page while they refresh it or when the site fails, e.g. `max-age=60, s-maxage=300, stale-while-revalidate=300, stale-if-error=3600` for a home page.
  - **Ages don't add up:** a page served from the page cache with a cache time carries `Age` (seconds since Joomla stored it), and its `Expires` counts from then too, so Varnish, CDNs and browsers subtract the time it already spent in Joomla's cache instead of keeping it for their whole time again (a page stored for 150 of its 180 seconds was kept another full `s-maxage` before). E.g. a hit 4 seconds after storing: `Age: 4`, `Expires` unchanged.
  - **Logged-in header** (Advanced Caching, on by default): every page of the site carries `X-Logged-In: True` or `False`, so a cache in front of it can store guests' pages (dropping the session cookie Joomla sends with each of them, which otherwise keeps Varnish from storing anything) and never a logged-in user's. On requests it tells logged-in visitors apart by Joomla's `joomla_user_state=logged_in` cookie, set at login; the header also covers logins that cookie doesn't announce (Remember Me during the request). Set first thing (so cached pages, redirects and error pages carry it) and again after rendering; site only, never stored with cached pages. In Varnish, e.g. `vcl_recv`: `if (req.http.Cookie ~ "joomla_user_state=logged_in") { return (pass); }`; `vcl_backend_response`: `if (beresp.http.X-Logged-In == "False") { unset beresp.http.Set-Cookie; } else { set beresp.uncacheable = true; }`.
  - Saving the plugin empties the page cache, so new times apply at once.
  - Its description explains what the page cache does and what each group of options is for ("Provides page caching." before), with links to MDN's and web.dev's explanations of HTTP caching.
  - Sites updating to 3.18 with the plugin enabled get a post-installation message ("System - Page Cache has new options") asking to review them, with a button to its options; it's shown only while the plugin is enabled, and never on new sites.
  - The plugin's "cachetime" (its own page cache lifetime in old Joomla versions, a field Joomla 3 removed but kept writing "15" into every site's options, read by nothing) is gone from new sites and removed from existing ones on update, with its two unused language strings.
- **Pages marked cachable follow the Cache Time:** when a view or an extension lets browsers keep a page (`allowCache(true)`, e.g. K2's items) without a cache time of its own, Joomla sent `Expires` 15 minutes ahead, hardcoded since Joomla 1.5 (`WebApplication::respond()`); it's now Global Configuration's Cache Time (15 minutes by default, so nothing changes on sites that kept it), through the new `$app->getBrowserCacheTime()`. And a view served from the view cache counts it from when the view was stored, not from now: a view cached 10 minutes ago with a 15-minute Cache Time gets 5 more, not 15.
- **The page cache ignores tracking parameters** (Global Configuration → System → Cache Settings → "Ignored Query Parameters"): links from analytics, advertising, e-mail and social services carry parameters which never change a page (`utm_*`, `fbclid`, `gclid`, `msclkid`, `mc_*`, ...; the default list holds 58, `*` for any text), and the page cache stored one copy per link, so campaign and social visitors almost never got a cached page. A visit with them now gets the stored page of the address without them. Its own page is never stored, as it may hold those parameters in links built from the current address, which other visitors would then get. The address isn't changed, so analytics still see the parameters. New sites have the list in `configuration.php` (`cache_ignored_params`); existing sites use the same default until Global Configuration is saved. Empty: nothing is ignored.
- **Little WAF: "Fix HTML-escaped ampersands"** (a new URL Normalization, on by default): `?start=20&amp;limit=10` (a link copied out of a page's HTML, which PHP reads as a parameter named `amp;limit`) is redirected to `?start=20&limit=10`, also when escaped twice. "Remove doubled question marks" also removes an empty query (`/page?` → `/page`).
- **The file cache reads faster:** a cache hit checked its file six times before reading it (the folder twice per lookup, the file's existence twice, its date, its size); now once, its date, and folders found in a request are remembered. About 30% less time per hit (20,000 hits: 442 → 306 ms), which counts on pages with many cached modules.
- **A second request for a page being cached now waits for it:** while one request renders a page or module to cache it, another one for the same entry was meant to wait and use the result, but gave up after about 15 ms (0.1 ms between tries) and rendered it too. The file, APCu and Memcached handlers now try every 10 ms, for up to a tenth of the lock time (1.5 s by default): measured, a second request took the first one's page after 410 ms instead of rendering it again. The generic lock (used by Redis) keeps its short wait, as an interrupted request leaves its lock behind for the whole cache time.
- **Long-running processes see cache entries expire:** the cache's clock was set once per process, so in the command line's MCP server or a long indexing run entries never expired; it's now read each time.
- **Platform Specific Caching is cheaper:** every cache read and write (each cached module, the page) parsed the visitor's user agent again to tell mobile from desktop; it's now worked out once per user agent and request.
- **The core system plugins run in a better order** (new sites, and once on update for plugins still at Joomla's ordering): Little WAF (-100), Language Filter (-90), Remember Me (-80) and Page Cache (-70) first, then the others (1 to 14, in Joomla's old relative order) and Debug last (1000). Page Cache serves a stored page in `onAfterInitialise` and ends the request there; as the last core plugin (9) it came after every third-party system plugin (ordering 0 by default), whose `onAfterInitialise` work ran for every cached page. Now only what must come before it does: Little WAF (so cached pages are filtered and get their headers), the Language Filter (language detection and redirects) and Remember Me (its login; otherwise returning users got cached guest pages). Debug, last, also reports the work of the plugins after it. "Restore uninstalled core extensions" puts a restored system plugin back in its place, and restores Little WAF enabled (it came back disabled).
- **Little WAF: generic protections** (each its own option, all on by default, under a "Generic Protections" heading), which catch whole classes of attack whatever extensions a site has:
  - **Probes for other platforms' files** (WordPress's `wp-login.php`, `xmlrpc.php`, `wp-admin`, `wp-json`; `.git`, `.svn`; `.env`; phpMyAdmin, Adminer, `phpinfo.php`, PHPUnit's `eval-stdin.php`; backups and dumps ending in `.sql`, `.bak`, `.old`, `~`, ...) get a plain 404 before Joomla builds its error page for them: with the shipped `.htaccess` every missing file reaches Joomla, so scans cost the server a full page each. Matched by whole path segments (a page called "environment" or "old" isn't one); not logged.
  - **Executable uploads:** front-end uploads with a script extension anywhere in the name (Joomla's own forbidden list from `InputFilter::isSafeFile()` plus ASP, JSP and SHTML: `shell.php`, `shell.php.jpg`, `x.php.`), server configuration files (`.htaccess`, `.user.ini`, `web.config`) or a NUL byte get a 403, also in nested upload fields. Joomla checks its own uploads, but extensions reading `$_FILES` themselves often didn't.
  - **PHP-serialized objects** (`O:8:"stdClass":...`, `C:...`, the old `O:+8:` form) in the address, form data, cookies or headers, also percent-encoded, get a 403: PHP object injection, as in the 2015 attack on Joomla's sessions through the User-Agent header. Serialized arrays and strings pass; the editor's text of users who may write content isn't checked.
  - **Attack tools:** user agents of sqlmap, Nikto, Nuclei, WPScan, Acunetix, Nessus, gobuster, ffuf, the Nmap scripting engine and others get a 403.
- **Little WAF: security headers** (under a "Security Headers" heading), on every front-end response: `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `X-XSS-Protection: 0` and `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin` by default (safe for every site); `X-Frame-Options` (`SAMEORIGIN` or `DENY`), `Strict-Transport-Security` (a year, optionally with subdomains; only sent over HTTPS) and `Permissions-Policy` (any value without control characters) are off until chosen, as they can change how a site works. Each can be set to "Don't send" (for headers the web server already adds). A header the response already has is left alone, and pages setting their own later still win (Users' password reset pages keep `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`). Listed values only: a stored value that isn't one of them is ignored.
- **Little WAF: URL Normalizations** (new options, all on by default, under their own heading below the protections: every option of the plugin is on one page, grouped by headings): malformed addresses of front-end pages, which crawlers make up while crawling large sites (Bing is known for appending Joomla's `start`, `limitstart` or `format` again and again), are sent to their clean address with one permanent (301) redirect, so search engines index one address per page:
  - **Remove duplicate query parameters:** every repeat of a parameter goes, the first stays where it is (`/politics?start=100&format=feed&dev=1&dev=2&start=200` → `/politics?start=100&format=feed&dev=1`); names are compared as PHP reads them (percent-encoded, `.` and space as `_`), empty parameters (`&&`, `&=value`) go too, and lists (names ending in `[]`) may repeat. Nothing is decoded or reordered;
  - **Remove doubled question marks** (`/page??start=20` → `/page?start=20`);
  - **Remove text after an ampersand in the path** (`/page&start=20` → `/page`; a real query after it stays);
  - **No feeds of search results:** `format=feed` on Search, Smart Search or another component's search (a `search` task or a `searchword`, e.g. K2's) goes to the home page, checked after routing so SEF addresses count too.
  Only GET and HEAD requests are redirected (a redirect would lose posted data); requests the filters block still get their 403 first; the redirect is always an absolute address on the site (a path starting with `//` can't send visitors to another host).
- **Little WAF is on by default**: enabled on new sites, and once on existing sites when they update (it was installed disabled in 3.16 and 3.17); a site admin who disables it afterwards keeps it disabled. Every protection stays on, and each one still only checks requests while the extension it targets is installed and enabled.
- **Control Panel defaults:** the administrator's "Latest Actions" module shows 20 actions instead of 5 (new sites, modules without a saved count, and once on update modules saved with the old 5); a "System Information" module (the Statistics module with Server Information and Site Information on) is published last in the `icon` position, beside the quick icons (new sites, and once on update for sites without an administrator Statistics module; "Restore uninstalled core extensions" also brings it back); the Statistics module's Server and Site Information options now default to Yes; new sites have the "Privacy Dashboard" module unpublished.
- More core extensions can be uninstalled entirely, at the site admin's request: the Articles, Private Messages, Privacy, Redirect, Tags and Wrapper components (each with its companion modules and plugins, e.g. Redirect with "System - Redirect", Tags with its two modules and its search plugins, Articles with its modules and its content, editor button, search, privacy and sample data plugins), the Breadcrumbs and Syndication Feeds site modules and the CodeMirror editor. New sites have them unlocked in the install SQL, existing sites get them unlocked by `3.18.0-2026-10-10.sql`; "Restore uninstalled core extensions" brings each back with its companions (Redirect's plugin disabled, as it ships). What depends on them now checks they're there:
  - the administrator's quick icons hide buttons whose component is missing or disabled (`ModQuickIconHelper::hasComponents()`), and the status bar hides the messages count without Private Messages;
  - the administrator menu leaves out the Categories and Fields items of a missing component (they showed "ACL configuration file missing");
  - tags on articles (the core layout, Hammond, Finch and Rookwood) are shown only while Tags is installed and enabled: without it, article pages failed with a 500 error (`TagsHelperRoute` not found);
  - uninstalling a component which only has site files (Wrapper) failed to find its manifest;
  - Articles lists the plugins which only serve it as companions ("Content - Confirm Consent", "System - Privacy Consent", "User - Terms and Conditions", "Sample Data") and CodeMirror's manifest lists its media folder, so nothing is left behind.
  - Contacts no longer depends on Articles: a contact's list of articles is left out without it (the page failed with a 500 error, `ContentHelperRoute` not found), and so is the category picker's article-category link, which every category picker built (choosing a Contacts or News Feeds category for a menu item, or a category for a user note, failed with a 500 error). The profile plugin's terms of service field shows plain text instead of a link, also when its article was deleted (PHP 8 warned about reading a property of null), and the category search plugin finds nothing.
  - Uninstalling any component (core or third-party) removes its entries from the Smart Search index (links to `index.php?option=<component>&...`), whose results would otherwise only lead to errors (`FinderIndexer::removeComponentLinks()`): in batches, lowering the terms' link counts and deleting the terms no other entry has, which takes about 15–30 ms an entry. The uninstall spends at most 10 seconds on it; entries left then are only taken out of the results, and a notice says to clear the index and index again to remove their words. Updates do the same for removable core components uninstalled before. Verified on SQLite and MySQL 8.4 (2,000 and 3,000 articles): the index's term counts matched its mapping tables afterwards.
  Verified by uninstalling each on a copy of a News site and crawling both sides: no errors, warnings or deprecations. Without Articles, menu items showing articles return 404 (the home page too, when it's an article view).
- Uninstalling a removable core component removes its own database tables, as Banners, Contacts, News Feeds and Smart Search already did: Articles (`#__content`, `#__content_frontpage`, `#__content_rating`), Private Messages (`#__messages`, `#__messages_cfg`), Privacy (`#__privacy_requests`, `#__privacy_consents`), Redirect (`#__redirect_links`), Tags (`#__tags`, `#__contentitem_tag_map`), Content History (`#__ucm_history`) and Fields (`#__fields`, `#__fields_categories`, `#__fields_groups`, `#__fields_values`). Tables other extensions share stay: Search's `#__core_log_searches` (Smart Search logs its searches there too), `#__ucm_base`/`#__ucm_content`, `#__content_types`, `#__associations` and `#__user_profiles`. Each of these components now has install SQL for MySQL/MariaDB/SQLite, PostgreSQL and SQL Server/Azure SQL, so "Restore uninstalled core extensions" creates its tables again; it only creates what's missing. Updates from versions before 3.18 drop the tables left by core components uninstalled then (Content History and Fields kept theirs; the others only if their uninstall SQL had failed), by running each one's uninstall SQL before the update deletes its files again (`dropTablesOfRemovedCoreComponents()`); not when "Restore uninstalled core extensions" is ticked, which brings them back instead. Verified from a 3.17 site with Fields and Content History uninstalled: their five tables dropped by a plain update, kept and the components back with the restore option, and kept on a site updating from 3.18. Updates skip the database changes meant for the tables of uninstalled components (`ChangeSet`). Core code which uses these tables now checks the component is there:
  - Tags: saving, editing, listing and showing items (`TagsHelper::isAvailable()`, which the tag observer, models and layouts go through), the Tags form field and filter (hidden), `JHtmlTag`, and the "filter by tag" of list menu items and administrator lists (ignored);
  - Content History: saving and deleting items (`ContentHistoryHelper`), even with "Enable Versions" on;
  - Private Messages: the old-message purge at administrator login, deleting a user, the notice to administrators when a registration email fails, the status bar's count and "User - Joomla!";
  - Fields: Articles' search plugin searches custom fields only while Fields is installed;
  - Articles: the multilingual status check and the Categories batch that reverses article ordering;
  - the command line: article, tag and category commands refuse to run without their component (exit code 3), and so do `--tags` and `menu:item:create --article`.
  Verified on SQLite, MySQL 8.4, PostgreSQL 18 and SQL Server 2022: each component uninstalled (exactly its tables dropped) and restored (its tables back, the root tag and Smart Search's taxonomy root and common words once each, PostgreSQL's sequence moved forward only), the install SQL run again over existing tables without changes, crawls of the site and the administrator, and saves through the command line without each component; 3.17's code failed those saves once the tables were gone.
- SQL Server and Azure SQL: Banners, Contacts, News Feeds and Smart Search had install and uninstall SQL for MySQL and PostgreSQL only, so on SQL Server their tables stayed after an uninstall and a restore couldn't create them; they have SQL Server files now, and the installer picks `sqlsrv` files on both SQL Server drivers (`Installer::parseSQLFiles()`). Component install SQL is generated from each database's `installation/sql/<database>/joomla.sql` by `docs/component-sql/build.py`.
- Removed "Authentication - GMail": it signed users in by sending their Gmail address and password to Google's mail feed, which Google stopped accepting in 2022, so it couldn't sign anyone in. Removed "System - P3P Policy": only Internet Explorer read P3P headers. Removed joomla.org's update site for the Joomla Update component ("Joomla! Update Component Update Site"), whose feed only lists versions for Joomla 3.6 and 4.0, so every update check contacted joomla.org for nothing. New sites don't have them, and updates remove them from existing sites (`3.18.0-2026-10-11.sql`, run on every update).
- Global Configuration: the Memcache options (cache and session), left for the Memcache handler removed in 3.16, are gone; saving Global Configuration (also `config:set`) removes their values from `configuration.php`.
- Removed code nothing uses: the web service packages for Facebook, Twitter, LinkedIn, Google, GitHub, MediaWiki and OpenStreetMap, OAuth 1 and 2 and the keychain (`libraries/joomla/`, with `bin/keychain.php`, deprecated since Joomla 3, removed in Joomla 4, several for APIs which no longer exist), `JSimplecrypt`, and the polyfills for PHP versions below this distribution's 7.1 minimum (Symfony's PHP 5.5, 5.6 and 7.1 polyfills and their utilities, `password_compat`, `random_compat` and the `unserialize()` polyfill; the PHAR stream wrapper uses PHP's own `unserialize()` with its `allowed_classes` option): five files fewer loaded on every request. Also Weblinks' language files and package manifest (removed only from sites without Weblinks), the "Fields - Image" plugin's language files (the plugin left in Joomla 3.7), and Composer's class map entries for the session storages removed in 3.16. Updates delete them.
- Joomla Update: the "Restore uninstalled core extensions" option's description sits next to its checkbox (and toggles it), at most 300px wide, instead of a line across the whole screen under it (Upload & Update and the reinstall form).
- Drag and drop reordering works with touch (phones, tablets): Joomla 3's list reordering (`media/jui/js/sortablelist.js`, on jQuery UI 1.9's sortable, which only listens to the mouse) now turns a touch that starts on a row's handle into the mouse events it expects, so rows can be dragged by finger in every reorderable list (articles, modules, menu items, categories, fields...) with any administrator template; a touch anywhere else still scrolls the page, and the mouse works as before. Verified in Chrome with touch input (Modules and Articles: the row moved and the new order saved) and with the mouse.
- PostgreSQL 9.5 is the minimum (was 9.0): the components' install SQL, which a restore runs on sites that may still have their tables, uses `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` (new in 9.1), and 9.5 adds `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS`, which extensions' re-runnable install scripts need (K2 3.0's does). The installer refuses older servers, and Joomla Update (sites on 3.16 or newer) won't offer this release on them. PostgreSQL 9.4's support ended in February 2020.
- Removable core extensions checked against each other: every reference from one removable extension to another which isn't its companion (classes, tables, file paths) was listed and guarded, so removing one doesn't break another. Uninstalling a component (core or third-party) also deletes its rows from the tables it shares with others: tag mappings, the content copies tagging keeps (`#__ucm_content`, `#__ucm_base`), content history, associations (and those of its deleted categories) and custom fields with their values (`ComponentAdapter::removeSharedData()`; updates do the same for core components uninstalled before 3.18). Content types stay, as reinstalling the component doesn't add them back. Its site menu items are deleted too, except the home item, as they'd only lead to "page not found" (`ComponentAdapter::removeSiteMenuItems()`): items placed under them move up to their parent, and Menu Item Aliases pointing at them and their module assignments go with them (stock Joomla 3 left them all). Without Private Messages, Privacy's request notifications, "Content - Joomla"'s new-article notifications and "System - Privacy Consent"'s expired-consent notifications are skipped (they'd fail on its missing model); without Fields, Contacts' contact form and fields layout skip custom fields; without Articles, Smart Search's category routes fall back to the category's URL, and "Content - Joomla"'s check for items in a category being deleted only looks at installed components; without Tags, the Sample Data plugin neither records nor removes tags; without the administrator Feed module, Post-installation Messages leave out the news column (PHP warned). Verified by uninstalling every removable extension at once (14 components, then the 47 modules and plugins left): the administrator and the site's remaining pages without errors, warnings or deprecations.
- Isis and Protostar no longer use Less: their `css/template.css` is the source now, edited directly, in sections named after the old Less files (Bootstrap's parts, then the template's own, e.g. "Isis: Status"), one rule per line, with the main colours as CSS custom properties. The Less sources had already gone stale (the shipped CSS had been edited directly since 3.13), so the conversion started from the shipped CSS. Prefixes and hacks only old browsers needed were removed where the standard property is in the same rule (about 430 declarations in Isis, 740 in Protostar: `-moz-`/`-webkit-`/`-o-` duplicates, prefixed gradients and keyframes, Internet Explorer filters and `*`/`\9` hacks); the CSS shrank from 8,962 to about 2,090 lines (Isis) and 7,773 to about 1,680 (Protostar). Isis' right-to-left stylesheet was a full copy of the left-to-right one with overrides at its end: it now holds only the overrides (213 lines instead of 9,920), loaded after `template.css`. The template options' colours (Isis: Template, Header, Sidebar and Link Colour; Protostar: Template and Background Colour) are set as custom properties and applied by `css/template-options.css`, loaded after `custom.css`/`user.css` when an option is set, instead of blocks of CSS written into each page. Verified with before/after screenshots of 34 administrator and 15 site pages, at 1280px and 390px, left to right and right to left, and with every colour option set: identical, apart from the error pages, which now use the options as the other pages do (Isis' tinted the sidebar and Protostar's left out its buttons). `media/jui/less` and the Less compilers (`JLess`, FOF's) stay for third-party templates. Sites which changed a template's Less files and compiled them in the Template Manager lose those changes on update, as the compiled `template.css` they produced is replaced on every update anyway; changes belong in `custom.css` (Isis) or `user.css` (Protostar), which updates don't touch.
- Isis and Protostar no longer load anything for old Internet Explorer versions: the HTML5 shiv (`jui/html5.js`) and the `filter` polyfill they loaded in conditional comments (`<!--[if lt IE 9]>`), and the `X-UA-Compatible` meta tag, are gone, and the conditional scripts core code adds (the forms' HTML5 fallback, the event, `classList` and `Map` polyfills) are left out of their pages too, as Isis' login page already did (`isisDropConditionalAssets()`/`protostarDropConditionalAssets()`). Screenshots unchanged; site and administrator crawls clean.
- Isis and Prothero tidied up: each page file (`index.php`, `component.php`, `login.php`, `error.php`) starts with one line, `$page = require __DIR__ . '/includes/helper.php';`, and its markup reads what it needs from `$page` (the page's set-up moved into `IsisHelper`/`ProtheroHelper`, `includes/helper.php`, which replaces the old `helper.php` and Isis' global `colorIsLight()`). `cpanel.php`, which only included `index.php`, is gone: a template without the page Joomla asks for (the Control Panel asks for `cpanel.php`) now gets its own `index.php` rather than core's. The `img/` folders (Bootstrap 2's glyph sprites, unused) and unused images are removed; the 32 legacy images of `images/admin/` (used by `JHtml::_('grid.published')`, its checked-out icon and extensions asking for `admin/x.png`) are SVGs from Bootstrap Icons in `media/system/images/admin/`, found when the PNG is asked for (a template's own PNG still wins). Isis' header logo is the inline Joomla SVG instead of two PNGs; the Joomla logo both templates use is `media/system/images/logo/joomla.svg`, and the status bar's icons are Bootstrap Icons from `media/system/images/icons` (one copy for every template). Each template's own SVGs are in its `images/svg/` folder (Prothero's login blocks; Hammond's, Finch's and Rookwood's icons and favicon). The template files' markup is indented by its HTML nesting, with PHP control lines at the level around them and their contents not indented further (so the HTML they write isn't indented twice), void elements closed XHTML style and `<!DOCTYPE html>` on a line of its own, in every core template and the system pages (the pages' HTML compared before and after: unchanged but for whitespace). Pages look the same (screenshots compared, desktop and phone).
- Global Configuration and `config:set` can point a site at a database of another type (MySQL/MariaDB, PostgreSQL, SQLite) when its current database can't be reached or doesn't hold the site, e.g. a copy whose settings name a database that doesn't exist (`config:set dbtype=mysqlonsqlite db=database/....sqlite host= user= password=`). They refused any switch between types, as it would leave the site's data in the old database, so such settings could only be fixed by editing `configuration.php`. The new settings must still connect (a missing SQLite file isn't created), a warning says nothing was copied, and a switch while the current database holds the site is still refused (`database:convert` moves it).
- `extension:remove`, `extension:enable`, `extension:disable` and `extension:reinstall` take an extension's name as well as its ID: its element (`com_k2`, `mod_menu`, `en-GB`, `pkg_example`) or, for plugins, templates, libraries and files, the element with its prefix (`plg_system_k2`, `tpl_hammond`), saving a lookup with `extension:list` in scripts. A name several extensions share (a module or language for both the site and the administrator) is refused with their IDs (exit code 2); an unknown one gives "not found" (3).
- PostgreSQL (both drivers, and FOF's copy): `getTableColumns($table, false)` also gives the fields of MySQL's `SHOW FULL COLUMNS` it lacked, `Key` (`PRI`, `UNI`, `MUL`), `Extra` (`auto_increment` for columns whose default is a sequence's `nextval()`), `Collation` (null), `Privileges` and `Comment`, next to its own and the `Field`, `Type` and `Null` it already had. Extensions written against MySQL's output (e.g. reading `Extra` to skip an auto-increment key, as K2 does) got "Undefined property" warnings and wrong answers. `Key` follows MySQL's rules (every column of the primary key, or without one of the first unique index whose columns are all NOT NULL; `UNI` for a single-column unique index; `MUL` for the first column of another index) and comes from a query of its own, so the column query is unchanged and a failure only leaves `Key` empty. Verified against MySQL 8.4 on Joomla's 78 core tables (711 columns): the same `Key` and `Extra` except where the two install schemas differ. The MySQL and SQLite drivers are unchanged.
- Command line: a console plugin's command can no longer take the name of a core command (or of another plugin's): it was registered over it, so a plugin's `site:info` replaced the core one in the command line and the MCP server, where the core command's declarations (read-only, dry runs, writing code) decide what an assistant may run. The first command of a name keeps it, core commands first; the other is skipped with a message on the error output (so JSON and the MCP protocol stay clean).
- `CLI-GUIDE.md`, a guide for extension developers adding commands to the command line through a console plugin (`onGetConsoleCommands`): a complete example plugin, the command API, the declarations the MCP server relies on, output, commands as MCP tools, working with Joomla inside a command, compatibility with stock Joomla 3 and Joomla 4/5, and a testing checklist. Its example was checked by installing it from the guide's own code (CLI, JSON, dry runs, exit codes, the User Actions Log and MCP, on PHP 8.5 and 7.4).
- No more microdata (schema.org `itemscope`, `itemtype` and `itemprop` attributes, and `<meta itemprop>` tags) in the site's pages: it cluttered the HTML of Articles' views (article, category blog, featured, archive), Contacts' (contact, featured), the content layouts (author, dates, category, hits, images, tags, read more, blog item title), the Latest Articles, Most Read and Breadcrumbs modules, the vote plugin's rating and the article pages of Hammond, Finch and Rookwood, and described pages only partly. Structured data now comes as JSON-LD from "System - Structured Data & Social Tags". The elements and their classes are unchanged, so templates' CSS still applies. The `JMicrodata` library class stays, for extensions which use it.
- The News sample data has a **For Subscribers** category: 20 in-depth articles only registered users may read, in the main menu. With "Show Unauthorised Links" on for them, guests see them listed and get an article's intro text with a link to log in; logged-in users read them in full (and their structured data carries only what the visitor may read). A ready-made test of restricted content. Their images are the section images no other article leads with. The Sample Data plugin now keeps an article's access level (when the site has that level), so the set is the same installed from the Control Panel as with the installer.
- Sample data (News, Blog and Studio): articles' view counts are a few dozen (3 to 48) instead of up to 9,000, which looked inflated on a new site. The order is kept (the generators scale the same random draws: `hits()` in each `generate_content.py`, also applied to the `content.json` files), so lists by popularity (Most Read, the Blog's Popular posts) show the same articles.
- Administrator dashboard: each module's box (Isis' and Prothero's `well` module style, used by the Control Panel) carries the module's name, with dashes for underscores, and its ID as classes, e.g. `<div class="well well-small mod-k2-dashboard module-id-12">`, so a module can be styled on its own (the classes it had matched every module). They're on the module's own box rather than on its `row-fluid`, which holds every module sharing the row when modules are narrower than the full width.
- New sites no longer get stock Joomla's leftover images, which nothing used since the old sample data sets were removed: the five images of `images/banners` (the folder stays, empty: the Banners component's image field opens the media picker in it when it exists), `images/headers` (5), `images/joomla_black.png` and `images/powered_by.png` (checked: no reference in the install SQL, the three sample sets, code, templates or language files). Updates leave them on existing sites, whose own content may use them. A new, empty `images/uploads` folder suggests where to upload the site's own images; the command line's help examples for article and category images point there.
