- `configuration.php` lists its settings in alphabetical order (ignoring case), so a setting is easy to find when the file is opened by hand. Every writer of the file passes the registry's PHP format a new `sort` option: Global Configuration (and `config:set`), the installer (and `core:install`), `database:convert` and the two post-installation messages that change it (eAccelerator, behind a proxy). Only the order changes: verified that a file written by `config:set` holds the same values as before, and that a new site's file is sorted and works. The file is sorted the next time it's saved.
- `addHeadLink()` keeps one link per address *and* relation (stock Joomla 3: one per address, so a second link to the same address replaced the first whatever its `rel`, e.g. a dns-prefetch replaced the preconnect for the same origin, and a canonical a feed with the same address; the language filter worked around it with custom tags). Each link now carries its address (`href`); the key stays the address for its first relation, so code reading `$_links` keeps working; the SEF plugin and the Syndication module read `href`. Verified on real pages (home, category with feeds, article, tag, search): the links and headers are identical to before.
- Hammond, Finch and Rookwood use the new resource hints, which also go out as HTTP `Link:` headers (and become 103 Early Hints behind CDNs such as Cloudflare): every page preloads the template's stylesheets (`template.css`, and `custom.css` when there is one) and `template.js`, with the exact addresses the page loads; the article view preloads the article's main image (`fetchpriority="high"`; Rookwood's with the same `imagesrcset`/`imagesizes` as its `<img>`, so phones preload the 960px copy, not the full size); an article with an X post (Hammond, Finch, whose script loads X's then) preconnects to `platform.twitter.com`, and one with a YouTube video resolves the video's host early (`dns-prefetch` only: the videos load lazily, often below the first screen, where a preconnect would go unused). Plain string checks of the article's text, no other view affected. Verified on the three sample sites (articles with and without X posts and videos, a Rookwood page with a hero image): hints and headers as described, image preloads identical to the images' attributes, Rookwood's markup unchanged, nothing logged. `Link:` headers also carry `imagesrcset` and `imagesizes` now.
- CodeMirror ("Editor - CodeMirror", also the code editor of Templates: Templates) updated from 5.60.0 (March 2021) to 5.65.21 (February 2026), the latest of CodeMirror 5, which is maintained (its author's new work is CodeMirror 6, a rewrite with a different API). 85 fixes, none of them security fixes, among them: wheel scrolling unusably slow on Chrome 94+ for Windows and stopping on Chrome 102+, content below the editor not receiving mouse clicks on Chrome 105+, text corrupted when selecting during input-method composition, macOS auto-hiding scrollbars, and fixes in many language modes; two new themes (abbott, juejin). The upstream files are unchanged; Joomla's bundles (`lib/addons.js`, `lib/addons.css`) are rebuilt from the same addons, and every `.min` file regenerated. Checked in headless Chrome, Isis and Prothero: PHP, CSS and JS template files and an article open with the right mode, no failed requests or errors.
- Media Manager: the default upload types cover today's files: images `jpg`, `jpeg`, `png`, `gif`, `webp`, `avif`, `bmp`, `ico`; video `mp4`, `webm`, `mov`, `ogv`, `m4v`; audio `mp3`, `m4a`, `aac`, `ogg`, `oga`, `opus`, `wav`, `flac` (every audio and video type the Media Manager plays); documents `pdf`, `txt`, `csv`, `rtf`, `doc`, `docx`, `xls`, `xlsx`, `ppt`, `pptx`, `odt`, `ods`, `odp`, `odg` (and GIMP's `xcf`, as before). The MIME types list holds what PHP's file detection actually reports for each (measured on PHP 7.4 and 8.5 with real files of every type). SVG stays out: an SVG file can contain scripts, which run when it's opened, and anyone allowed to upload media (Authors, by default) could add one. The type lists (allowed extensions, image extensions, ignored extensions, allowed and illegal MIME types) are textareas, still separated by commas, and are read in any case and over several lines (the capitalised duplicates are gone). New sites get these defaults, and so does every site updating to 3.18, once (also lists an administrator had changed: the new ones are a superset of the common needs, and later updates leave the lists alone). Sites already past that step (3.18 development builds) get the audio and video types added later (`ogv`, `m4v`, `aac`, `oga`, `opus`, `flac`, with `video/ogg`, `video/x-m4v`, `audio/aac`, `audio/x-aac`, `audio/x-hx-aac-adts`, `audio/flac`, `audio/x-flac`) appended once to their lists, keeping everything else (an empty list stays empty). The Media Manager lists WebP and AVIF files as images and WebM, MOV, OGV and M4V files as videos, and shows a related type's icon for types without one (`docx` the Word icon, `xlsx`, `ods` and `csv` the spreadsheet one, ...: such files had no icon at all). Banners show WebP, AVIF and SVG images (such banners rendered nothing). Uploading through the editors needs the type allowed too, and with "Restrict Uploads" on (the default) users who can't manage the Media Manager may still upload images only. With PHP before 8.1, AVIF images and (with its older file detection) `pptx` files are refused, as PHP can't identify them.
- Media Manager: an upload whose name is taken no longer fails with "File already exists": the file is saved with the time added to its name (`photo.jpg` becomes `photo-20261018-143502.jpg`, and a number after that if even that is taken), in the Media Manager's upload form, TinyMCE 8's image uploads and the EasyMDE editor (`MediaHelper::getUniqueFilePath()`). The existing file is never replaced.
- Media Manager's thumbnails work alike: an image, video, audio file or folder opens from anywhere on its tile, not only from its name (images opened only from the name bar; clicking the picture selected it); every tile has a larger checkbox (20px) in its top corner, and only that box selects, with an outline around chosen tiles; a tile that opens something is tinted on hover and keyboard focus, in light and dark mode; names are no longer cut to 10 characters but shown in full on up to two lines (the rest with an ellipsis, the whole name in the tooltip), on tiles enlarged from 120 to 136 pixels (Isis and Prothero). Image tiles had their delete button on the left and the "selected" tick on the right, the other way round from every other tile: the delete button is now top right and the checkbox top left, as on the other tiles (the checkbox was hidden, with a tick shown only once an image was chosen; mirrored right to left).
- TinyMCE 8 ("Editor - TinyMCE") becomes the default editor of sites updating to 3.18 which still use the old stock default, TinyMCE 4 ("Editor - TinyMCE (legacy)"), as it has been for new sites since 3.17. Once, on the update to 3.18, and only when TinyMCE 8 is installed and enabled; a site which chose another editor (CodeMirror, None, a third-party editor such as JCE) keeps it, and so does every user who chose an editor in their profile. `configuration.php` keeps its other settings and its permissions (a read-only file stays read-only). Also `installation/configuration.php-dist` names TinyMCE 8. Verified on PHP 7.4 and 8.5: a 3.17 site on TinyMCE 4 switches (other settings identical, permissions 444 kept, valid PHP); a 3.18 site, a JCE site and a site with TinyMCE 8 disabled are left as they were.
- Joomla Update's confirmation screens (the login after uploading a package, and after an update; stock Joomla 3): Cancel was a red button, the colour of destructive actions, beside the Install or Confirm & Continue button, and on the second screen the two buttons differed in size. Cancel is a plain button now (Prothero: white beside the black primary button; Isis: grey beside the blue one), both the same size.
- Hammond, Finch and Rookwood offer phones the small screen copies the Media Manager now saves next to uploaded images (`srcset` and `sizes`): on images in lists, at the top of an article (and in its preload, with `imagesrcset`, so the browser fetches the size it will show), and on images in article text and module content (through the templates' HTML reader, `lazyImages()`, which also adds lazy loading; images with a `srcset` of their own keep it). Only images of the site with such a copy get one; external images and the sample images (which have none, except Studio's own) are written as before. Verified: the News, Blog and Studio sites' pages are the same as before (the Blog's lists differ between any two runs, as they're sorted by hits), and an article with an optimised upload as its main image and in its text gets `srcset` in all three templates, in the article, its list and its preload header.
- Media Manager: the Maximum Size of uploads is 128 MB by default (was 10 MB; sites updating with stock Joomla 3's 10 MB get it once, a size an administrator set is kept; PHP's own `upload_max_filesize` and `post_max_size` still apply, and only PHP's configuration can raise them). A new Memory Limit (in MB) option raises PHP's memory limit for uploads, image optimisation and `media:optimize` where the server allows it (never lowers it; empty: the server's limit): resizing a 24 megapixel photo needs about 240 MB. Verified: with PHP limited to 128 MB, a 6000×4000 photo was uploaded untouched, and with the option at 512 optimised to 900×600.
- Custom HTML: the "Custom" module (site and administrator) is now called "Custom HTML", and its "Prepare Content" option "Execute Content-type Plugins", with a description saying what it does and what it was called. New site modules have it on by default (it was off; the administrator module had it on already); existing modules keep their setting.
- CodeMirror: a Height option (a number of pixels, or px, em, rem, vh or %), for every CodeMirror, the Templates code editor included. Empty (the default) keeps the height as it was: Isis and Prothero size the editor to the window (400 to 800 pixels), elsewhere 300 pixels. A height that's set also lifts the templates' minimum and maximum; full screen is unaffected.
- CodeMirror: a dark theme in the colours of Visual Studio Code's default dark theme (`vscode-dark`: Dark Modern's editor, Dark+'s code colours), the default theme of new sites and, once, of sites updating which still have the stock theme (a theme an administrator chose is kept). Its rules outrank the plugin's Active Line and Matching Tag colours, which are meant for light themes.
- English language files (site, administrator, the templates' and modules' own copies): strings sorted by key (byte order, as the installer's languages already were), section headings dropped, a string's note kept above it and the e-mail merge code legends under the header, so a string is easy to find and a duplicate easy to see. The strings were reviewed too: typos ("Unkown error", "ugprade"), stray spaces before colons and inside brackets ("Settings : Passed", "# of Contacts :", "( If no optional text ... )"), double spaces, "other Super User user accounts", "non-latin", "writeable" (now "writable", as the other strings have it), "login" as a verb ("Please log in first", "log in to the site") and US spellings in British English ("centred", "centre", "cancelling"); the administrator's e-mail about a user awaiting approval lists Name, Email and Username in step. The template copies of Protostar's and Isis' strings, which differed from the site-wide files Joomla loads in stock Joomla 3, are identical again.
- Language files: strings nothing uses any more were removed: the MySQL driver's (removed in 3.16), the Languages and Templates managers' FTP login, System Information's Mcrypt, the Statistics modules' MySQL and Web Links, and five of Hammond's and Finch's. Global Configuration's form lost the empty Memcache section left when the Memcache options were removed. Strings still built in code from parts (cache and session handlers, module positions, layouts, category and field titles, help topics) were kept.
- Articles: the front-end article form no longer asks users who are logged in to solve the CAPTCHA (stock Joomla 3 asked everyone, Super Users included, whenever a CAPTCHA was set): it's meant to keep guests' submissions out. A new option, Articles: Options > Editing Layout > "CAPTCHA for Logged-in Users" (default No), asks them again. The field is left out of the form itself too, so its check doesn't fail.
- Administrator-only extensions keep their scripts and styles in their own folders, not in `media/` (which is for files both the site and the administrator load): Multilingual Associations, Joomla Update, Smart Search's indexer, Content History's Versions button, the Housekeeping and Sample Data modules, Install from Web, the quick icons and System - Statistics (`administrator/components/com_x/js`, `css`; `administrator/modules/mod_x/...`; `plugins/<group>/<name>/...`). A site whose `/administrator` is behind a password prompt no longer has any administrator file under `/media`, and files only the site needs stay where visitors can reach them: the editor buttons' pickers (Article, Contact, Menu, Module, Fields, Page Break) and Content History's comparison keep theirs in `media/`, since front-end editing opens them on the site. Asking for a moved file by its old name (`JHtml::_('script', 'com_contenthistory/admin-history-versions.js', ...)`) loads it from its new place. Unused files were removed: Joomla Update's `json2.js` (a JSON polyfill for Internet Explorer 7), Smart Search's `indexer.css`, System - Highlight's `highlight.css` and two-factor authentication's `qrcode_SJIS.js` and `qrcode_UTF8.js`.
- `media/` folders are named after the extension they belong to, and shared files sit in `media/system`: Contacts' icons moved from `media/contacts/images` to `media/com_contact/images`, the Media Manager's files from `media/media` to `media/com_media`, the e-mail form's close icon from `media/mailto` to `media/system/images`, and the debug console's script and styles from `media/cms` to `media/system` (the error pages of the core templates load them from there). Language overrides' search script and styles (`media/overrider`, administrator only) moved into Languages' own `css` and `js` folders. Old names keep working: a file asked for through `JHtml` by its old relative name (`JHtml::_('image', 'contacts/con_tel.png', ...)`, `JHtml::_('script', 'media/mediafield.min.js', ...)`) or by its old path from the site's root (`media/media/js/...`) is loaded from the new folder when nothing is found under the old one, so a template's own copy under the old name (`images/contacts/...` in the template) still wins. `media/jui` (Bootstrap 2, jQuery, jQuery Migrate, jQuery UI, Chosen, MiniColors, Simple Colors, the IcoMoon font, the sortable list, search tools and Bootstrap's Less sources) merged into `media/system` (no file names clashed), its images (`img`, and the duplicate `images/ajax-loader.gif`) into `media/system/images`; the stylesheets' image paths and every core reference (the templates' IcoMoon font, the error pages) were changed. `JHtml` names starting `jui/` and paths `media/jui/...` still load the files, and a template's own copy under the old name (`js/jui/bootstrap.min.js`, how templates replace Bootstrap's script) still wins over core's. Old names of files merged into `global.js` or removed (`jui/chosen.jquery.min.js`, `jui/jquery.searchtools.min.js`, `media/jui/js/html5.js`...) load what replaced them, or nothing, as their new names do. The date field's scripts, locales and stylesheets (`media/system/js/fields/`, `media/system/css/fields/`) are gone with the calendar itself (see the date field) and Chosen's two images from `media/system/css/` to `media/system/images/`. Addresses written into pages or stylesheets by hand (`/media/media/...`, `/media/jui/...`) aren't covered: templates and extensions linking to `media/jui` files directly need their paths changed. Empty folders left in `media/` were removed.
- Administrator-only scripts and styles named after their extension too: `associations.js`/`.css`, `config.js` (Send Test Mail), `contenthistory.js` (the Versions button), `finder.js` (the indexer), `languages.js`/`.css` (language overrides), the Sample Data module's `sampledata.js`, Install from Web's `webinstaller.js`/`.css`, the quick icons' `extensionupdate.js` and `joomlaupdate.js`. Joomla Update's three scripts are one, `joomlaupdate.js`, loaded by its main page and by the update run (the pre-update check starts only on the main page). The Media Manager's video player no longer ships its Flash and Silverlight fallbacks, which no current browser can run.
- Every stylesheet of Joomla's and this distribution's own is written in one style, one rule per line (`.a,.b {property:value;property:value;}`, rules inside `@media` indented), checked rule by rule against the files before: Bootstrap's Joomla-made extensions and right-to-left styles, the icon font's, the search tools', the sortable lists', the calendars', the system template's, the installer's, Install from Web's, the TinyMCE options' builder and others. Third-party stylesheets stay as their releases ship them.
- Internet Explorer hacks removed from those stylesheets (Isis, Prothero and Protostar, Bootstrap's Joomla-made extensions and right-to-left styles, the icon font's, the sortable lists', Install from Web's): `*property` and `_property` declarations, `-ms-` properties, values and placeholder rules, `filter:alpha()` and `progid:` filters, `zoom:1`, `@-ms-viewport`. Other browsers ignored all of them: the rules Chrome reads from each file are the same before and after, but for `zoom:1` (which changes nothing) and rules left empty.
- System Information's Third-party Software list names jQuery.PrettyTextDiff 1.0.2 (Content History's comparison, patched by Joomla; with Google's diff-match-patch in the same file), which it left out.
- The editors and CAPTCHA plugins keep all their files in their own folders, as the other plugins do: TinyMCE (both versions), CodeMirror, EasyMDE, Blocks (Editor.js) and None in `plugins/editors/<name>` (from `media/editors/<name>`, with the same folders inside: TinyMCE's `langs`, `skins`, `plugins` and `templates`, CodeMirror's `mode` and `theme`...), and the Google reCAPTCHA, Turnstile, hCaptcha and IconCaptcha scripts and styles in `plugins/captcha/<name>/js` and `css` (from `media/plg_captcha_<name>`). Uninstalling one removes everything it brought. CodeMirror options saved with the old folder (its hidden Base Path and Mode Path) are read as the new one. The update deletes the old folders, with any file added to them by hand (your own TinyMCE templates or skins, CodeMirror themes): copy such files into the new folders. The two plugins' manifests listed files they don't have (CodeMirror's `styles.css`, TinyMCE's `fields` folder) and left out others (CodeMirror's `layouts`): corrected.
- Each extension's folder in `media/` has one stylesheet and one script, named after it: `media/com_x/css/x.css` and `media/com_x/js/x.js` (`.min` versions beside them), `mod_x` likewise. Merged: Smart Search's date filter styles into `com_finder/css/finder.css`, Content History's comparison libraries (diff-match-patch and jQuery.PrettyTextDiff) into `com_contenthistory/js/contenthistory.js` (its styles `contenthistory.css`), the Article, Page Break and Read More buttons' scripts into `com_content/js/content.js`, and the Media Manager's: one `com_media/css/media.css` for the image picker, its list and the drop upload (each part applies to its own window only), one `com_media/js/media.js` for the drop upload, the media field, the Media Manager and the image picker (each starts only on its own page; `Function.prototype.bind`'s polyfill dropped). Renamed: Contacts' `contact.js`, Fields' `fields.js`, Menus' `menus.js`, Modules' `modules.js`, Wrapper's `wrapper.js` (also the Wrapper module's), the Language Switcher's `languages.css`. The Media Manager's stylesheets no page loaded (left from the removed Hathor template: `mediamanager.css`, `medialist-details.css`, `medialist-thumbs.css` and their right-to-left versions, `mejs-skins.css` and its images) and the picker's styles for markup it hasn't used since Joomla 2.5 were removed. Two-factor authentication's QR code script moved into its plugin (`plugins/twofactorauth/totp/js`), as the CAPTCHA plugins'. Unused images of MooTools' colour picker (`media/system/images/mooRainbow`) and of SqueezeBox's old shadows (`media/system/images/modal/bg_*.png`) removed.
- The headers of our scripts and stylesheets (and Joomla's in the distribution) hold only their copyright and licence lines: descriptions of what a file does, how it works or what it replaced were removed from 81 files (and from the headers kept in their `.min` versions), as they told visitors more than they needed. Software ported into a file keeps its credit as a copyright line (SqueezeBox, MooTree and Form.PasswordStrength in `global.js`/`global.css`, Bootstrap 2.3.2 in Isis', Prothero's and Protostar's `template.css`); third-party notices inside files (AES, Akeeba's update script, diff-match-patch, jQuery UI) are unchanged.
- Vendor prefixes only Chrome still reads and no standard covers are all that's left in our stylesheets (19 files): prefixed declarations Chrome treats as another name for the standard property (`-webkit-border-radius`, `-webkit-box-shadow`, `-webkit-box-sizing`, `-webkit-appearance`, `-webkit-mask`, `-webkit-user-select`, `-webkit-backdrop-filter`, `-webkit-background-clip`, `-webkit-transform`) became the standard property; `-moz-` and `-o-` properties, values and gradients, `-webkit-overflow-scrolling`, `-webkit-margin-top-collapse`, rules for `-moz-` pseudo-classes and elements (`::-moz-focus-inner`, `:-moz-placeholder`) and Isis' and Prothero's `@-moz-document` block were removed; `::-webkit-input-placeholder` is `::placeholder`. Kept: `-webkit-font-smoothing`, `-webkit-text-size-adjust`, `-webkit-line-clamp` (with `display:-webkit-box` and `-webkit-box-orient`), `-webkit-focus-ring-color` and the `::-webkit-scrollbar`, search field, calendar, details and colour swatch pseudo-elements. Checked by comparing the rules Chrome keeps from every file before and after: the same, apart from the placeholder selectors' new name and rules left empty. The tool is `docs/format/css_prefixes.py` (a dry run unless `--write`).
- Prothero's login page: the Log in button's hover is now visible (a lighter shade with a soft ring, in light and dark), as it barely changed before.

### Security fixes
- The Media Manager previews videos in the browser's own player: MediaElement.js 2.23.0 (2016, with jQuery, Flash and Silverlight fallbacks) was removed, as every browser plays the formats it lists (MP4, WebM, MOV where the browser can, OGV) with its own controls. The video lists start and pause it through `MediaManager.playVideo()`/`pauseVideo()` in `media.js`.
- The form validator's email check turns international domains into their ASCII form (`xn--`) with the browser's own IDNA, and `punycode.js` was removed (asking for it loads nothing): only that check used it, and its version 2 can't be loaded as a plain script. One address the old library let through is now refused: one with two `@` (`a@b@c.com`, which punycode.js cut at the second `@`); the server refused it anyway.
- **jQuery UI 1.14.2** (from 1.9.2 of 2013, which Joomla had patched for CVE-2021-41184 only): the later releases fix the rest of jQuery UI's known XSS issues and work with current browsers. Joomla's file (`jquery.ui.core.js` with widget, mouse and position) is cut unchanged from the official build and restores the 1.9 helpers removed since (`$.ui.ie`, `$.ui.hasScroll`, `$.fn.zIndex()`, `.focus(delay)` and others), so extensions written for 1.9 keep working. Tested: the API compared with 1.9.2's. Its sortable plugin (`jquery.ui.sortable.js`) was removed, as nothing in core uses it any longer (lists, subforms and both TinyMCE toolbar builders reorder in plain JavaScript): `JHtml::_('jquery.ui', array('sortable'))` loads jQuery UI's core and a stand-in for it (see `jquery.compat.js`).
- **TinyMCE 8.10.0** (from 8.9.2): TinyMCE 8.9.3 fixed a stored XSS through event handler attributes in its media plugin; 8.10.0 adds editing fixes (list styles, selection and scrolling in Chrome, image resizing, the editor iframe's `title`). Only TinyMCE's own files changed (30 of the 134 bundled); Joomla's integration, translations and templates are unchanged.
- Hammond, Finch and Rookwood showed the full text of articles a visitor had no access to: with "Show Unauthorised Links" on, Joomla shows a restricted article's page to guests (with its intro text and a "Register to read more" link) and leaves it to the layout to hold the rest back, which the templates' article layouts didn't do (stock Joomla's own layout does). They now show such visitors the intro text, guests with the link to log in, and logged-in users without access nothing of the text, as Joomla's layout does (`$item->params->get('access-view')`). Verified with each template for a Registered-only article: a guest sees the intro and the login link, a Registered user the whole article; public articles unchanged.
- The head wrote `<link>` addresses and attribute values exactly as given (stock Joomla 3), so an extension passing a value with a quote, e.g. from the request, let it add attributes (`href="/feed"onload="..."`). Addresses, relations and attribute values are now escaped, keeping entities already in them (`&amp;` from `JRoute::_()` or callers that escape stays as it is); attribute names that aren't valid names are left out. Defence in depth: core's own links were escaped already. The same for scripts and stylesheets: their URLs were written raw too (`addScript('/x.js?a=1"onload="...')` added an attribute), and an attribute name with a space could add attributes; URLs are escaped the same way and invalid names left out. Core's pages come out identical (94 script and link tags on eight site and administrator pages compared).
- Media uploads (stock Joomla 3): the check for executable extensions inside a file's name (`photo.php.jpg`) compared them case-sensitively, so `x.SHTML.png` or `x.ASP.jpg` passed it (Apache's `mod_mime` applies handlers for every extension of a name, in any case, e.g. server-side includes for `.shtml`; PHP extensions were still caught by the file input's own check). Names are now compared in lower case. Also, the "Illegal MIME Types" option (default `text/html`) was never checked; it now refuses those types. Verified with the real `canUpload()`: a valid PNG named `x.PHP.png` passed the old check and is refused by the new one.

### Bug fixes
- Media Manager: after browsing into a folder, the upload form still uploaded to the folder the page had opened with (its folder followed only the list's first load); it now follows every folder shown. The image picker's Insert threw for editors using the editors API (it named a variable that doesn't exist) and inserted nothing.
- Popovers given content by a script (`content` option) showed the element's `data-content` text instead, as the image field's preview did once the picker had set an image; the option comes first now, as in Bootstrap.
- The subcategories' expand buttons (Articles', Contacts' and News Feeds' Categories, the subcategories of Articles' category views) did nothing with templates that don't load Bootstrap (Hammond, Finch, Rookwood), and the Categories pages stopped on a script error (an inline jQuery script). They use the CMS's collapse now (`behavior.cms`; Bootstrap's where the template loads it), their label and plus/minus icon follow the state (`data-expand-label`, `data-collapse-label`), with `aria-expanded`. Smart Search's search filter's Invert Selection buttons on the site no longer need jQuery; Protostar's content history button and its unused stock `js/application.js` (Bootstrap's sample calls) neither.
- Banners: Tracks' export window's Cancel closed nothing (it named a modal that doesn't exist, `#modal-download`).
- Updates deleted Smart Search's PDF icon (`media/com_finder/images/file-pdf.svg`, new in 3.18): the cleanup list named the whole folder. Its old files are listed one by one there already.
- Extensions: Install: installing from the upload, URL or folder tab ignored the page's return address (stock Joomla 3: a second hidden field named `return` sent the form token instead).
- Smart Search's Content Maps never asked before deleting (stock Joomla 3: the page's script waited for a task named `map.delete`, the toolbar sends `maps.delete`).
- Two-factor authentication with Google Authenticator enabled: the new user form threw a script error (the QR code's script ran where the two-factor tab isn't shown), and a username with a quote broke the QR code's script (it was written into it unescaped).
- Redirects (stock Joomla 3): the component's manifest left out `access.xml` (its permissions file, so a package install lacked it); users who may not change states could still click the list's status buttons; the HTTP code filter's label and description showed as language keys.
- An error without an HTTP code (PHP's own errors, database exceptions) showed "Error: 0" as the page's title, and Hammond's and Finch's error pages showed "0", while the page was sent as a 500: titles and the three templates' error pages now say 500 too (the title was a stock Joomla 3 bug).
- The site's Site Configuration and Template Options addressed without their `controller` parameter (`index.php?option=com_config&view=config`) ended in a fatal error (no form was loaded); they now open as with it (stock Joomla 3 bug).
- Smart Search's indexer showed nothing useful when a request failed: its error handler called MooTools' `Json.evaluate()` (not loaded), and its error texts were registered on the JSON request instead of the page, so they showed as keys (stock Joomla 3 bugs).
- Language overrides' search lost everything after an `&` or `+` in the search text (it wasn't encoded), and the search and cache refresh requests had no form token check (stock Joomla 3 bugs); the Privacy Requests quick icon threw on an answer that wasn't JSON instead of saying "error".
- LESS compiling (`JLess`, lessphp, and FOF's copy) warned on modern PHP: hundreds of thousands of "Passing null to parameter #4 ($flags)" deprecations for one Bootstrap compile on PHP 8.1+, "Creation of dynamic property" on 8.2+ (every compile, also from setting a formatter), a float-to-int loss in colour arithmetic on 8.1+ and "array offset on false" for mixins without arguments; FOF's older copy also raised notices on PHP 7.4. Fixed without changing the CSS it writes (compared byte for byte over Bootstrap 2, its responsive part and IcoMoon, in every output format, on PHP 7.4 and 8.5).
- Menu Assignment's tree: collapsing a menu left its sub-items' toggles showing "-", and Select All/None missed checkboxes the user had clicked before; in a Menu Item Container, Show All and None didn't update which sub-items were disabled (stock Joomla 3 bugs).
- A media field in a new subform row kept the template row's id for its modal (`imageModal_..._optionsX_...`), the same in every new row (stock Joomla 3 bug).
- Module forms' Ordering list cut a module's title at its first comma and showed characters such as `&` as `&amp;`; and the ordering field's `linked` attribute was cast to a number, so it named no field (the list followed `jform_position` whatever the form said) (stock Joomla 3 bugs).
- Associations' Propagate filled the other languages' fields with an "id:alias" value and no title on SQLite and PostgreSQL (MySQL compared the slug as a number): the five components' `fetchAssociations` answer with numeric IDs (stock Joomla 3 bug).
- An item's form opened in a modal (Create or Edit of the Article, Category, Contact, Menu Item and News Feed fields) threw a JavaScript error on load: the Versions button's script moved its dialog to the end of the page without checking it was there (stock Joomla 3 bug).
- Media Manager (stock Joomla 3): in the list's details layout, videos in a subfolder didn't play, as their links named the file without its folder; and every time the list was loaded (opening a folder), the video preview's window got another handler for pausing it when closed. The links now use the file's path, as the image links do, and the handler is set once.
- **Smart Search suggestions were always empty on SQLite sites** (the search page and the Smart Search module): the suggestions query selected `DISTINCT(t.term)` without a name, which SQLite calls `(t.term)`, so every suggestion came back `null`. It's now `DISTINCT t.term AS term` on every database.
- **Invisible reCAPTCHA refused forms filled in slowly:** the stock "reCAPTCHA Invisible" plugin asked Google for its token as soon as the page loaded, and tokens expire after two minutes, so anyone taking longer to fill in a registration or contact form was refused ("timeout-or-duplicate"). The check now runs when the form is sent.
- **Modals on phones and small tablets ran off the screen** (stock bug; SqueezeBox, `media/system/js/modal.js`, which the editors' buttons and image fields open): below 980 pixels the window started 20 pixels down but was as tall as the screen plus its margins, and the page inside it was as large as the whole screen, wider and taller than the window, so its bottom edge and scrollbar were cut off and a second set of scrollbars was always drawn around it (`overflow: scroll`). The window now sits 20 pixels from the top and the bottom, the page inside fits it exactly, and scrollbars only show when needed. The image picker's file field no longer makes its page wider than a phone.
- **Media Manager uploads refused for their file type said "Bad Request":** Joomla's input filter drops executable uploads (`shell.php`, `shell.php.jpg`) before the upload sees them, and the JSON upload (the editors' and drag and drop's) then reported a bad request; it now says "This file type is not supported."
- **Notes without a label in administrator forms** (Isis, Prothero): their text flowed around an empty, floated label column, so its first line started indented; empty label columns are hidden now.
- **A damaged cache entry served an empty page until it expired** (stock Joomla 3 bug): the file handler writes an entry in place, and readers don't wait for it, so an interrupted or concurrent write (a request stopped by its time limit, a full disk, a visitor reading at that moment) left a cut-off entry. The page, view, module (callback) and output caches unserialized it without checking: PHP warned, and the page cache sent an empty page (status 200, 0 bytes) for the rest of the cache time. Entries that can't be decoded are now removed and count as a miss, so the page is made and stored again (`CacheController::getStored()`, used by every controller).
- **Browser caching of cached pages never worked** (stock Joomla 3 bug): with the page cache's "Use Browser Caching", a browser asking whether its copy is still current (`If-None-Match`) should get a 304. The ETag is sent in quotes, as HTTP requires, and browsers send it back so, but it was compared without them. Quoted and weak (`W/`) tags and lists of tags now match.
- **Cached pages gave every visitor another visitor's form token in Joomla's script options** (stock Joomla 3 bug): the cache replaced the token in hidden form fields, but not `csrf.token` in the script options, which `Joomla.request()` sends with its POST requests (`X-CSRF-Token`). On cached pages (page cache) and cached views and modules (whose document head is restored) those requests carried the token of the visit that stored the page, so they failed, and that token was handed to everyone. Forms whose token field ends in `>` rather than ` />` (HTML5-style, common in extensions and template overrides) kept the stale token too and failed their check. Both are replaced now.
- **Redis, APCu and WinCache: cleaning a cache group left its mobile copies, and could delete data that isn't Joomla's** (stock Joomla 3 bugs): with "Platform Specific Caching", mobile copies' keys start with `M-`, which the group match didn't allow for, so they stayed after Clear Cache, saving or Global Check-in (Memcached, which marks them at the end, was right). Cleaning everything but a group ("notgroup", also what Redis and APCu did when no mode was given) deleted every key of the store not in that group: other sites' cache entries, sessions and other applications' data in a shared Redis database or APCu. Only this site's cache keys are touched now, and no mode means "group", as in the file handler. Redis finds them with `SCAN` instead of `KEYS *`, which walked the whole database in one command and blocked the server meanwhile. APCu's and WinCache's expired-entry clean-up (`gc()`) only ever reached the mobile copies (a `strpos()` truthiness test), and Redis warned of an undefined `$connection` when it couldn't connect.
- **Log entries without a category raised a PHP 8.1+ deprecation** (`strtolower()` of null in `Log::findLoggers()`), e.g. when the Redis cache couldn't connect.
- **The page cache stored the headers of plugins which run before it, and sent them again on every cache hit** (stock Joomla 3 bug): it stored every response header with a page, also those set before it looked for the page, which those plugins set again on every request, cached or not. Headers that may repeat (e.g. `Vary`) went out twice, and a header such a plugin stopped sending (an option turned off) was still sent with cached pages until they expired. The page cache now stores only the headers set after it looked for the page (by the component, the template and the plugins after it), which a cache hit doesn't run.
- **`setHeader()` dropped a header whose value was `0`** (stock Joomla 3 bug): it treated `0` as no value, so `X-XSS-Protection: 0` or `Content-Length: 0` were never sent. Only an empty value now sets nothing, and `0` still means no status (exception codes are passed as statuses).
- **View caching kept the page but lost its HTTP headers** (stock Joomla 3 bug): with Global Configuration's caching on, a cache hit doesn't run the view, and the view cache (`JCacheControllerView`, used by `JControllerLegacy::display()` with `$cachable`) stored its output, document head, pathway and modules, but not the response headers the view set nor its `allowCache(true)`. So cached pages lost headers such as `Last-Modified` and `ETag` and went out as `no-cache`. Extensions worked around it (K2 passes its headers through a `<script type="application/x-k2-headers">` in the head and sets them again in its system plugin). The view cache now keeps the headers the view added while it ran (only those, not the rest of the request's) and whether it allowed browser caching, and sets them again on a hit.
- Updating from a version before 3.12 removed Beez 3 even from sites using it: its files were deleted and Protostar became the default template. A site using it (a Beez 3 style is the default template, also a language's, or assigned to a published menu item) now keeps it, as a third-party template its admin may uninstall later; an unused Beez 3 is removed as before, and so is Hathor (Isis becomes the default) and the two quick icon plugins. Sites past 3.12 keep an installed Beez 3, while rows whose files are already gone are cleaned up (a Beez 3 default then switches to Protostar). The decision moved from the 3.12 migration, which runs on every update, to the update script (`keepOrRemoveBeez3()`, with the version updated from), and the old-files cleanup leaves Beez 3's files alone while it's installed (also from Extensions: Database and `update:joomla:remove-old-files`). Sites which crossed 3.12 with an earlier version already lost Beez 3.
- PHP 8.2+ deprecation warnings whenever Joomla wrote a text log, e.g. Joomla Update's: the text logger set `clientIP`, `datetime` and `time` on log entries as dynamic properties. They're declared now, and `LogEntry` allows other fields custom log formats add.
- Joomla Update's extraction script (`restore.php`, stock Joomla 3) flooded the server's error log on PHP 8.1+: every request logged several "Implicit conversion from float to int loses precision" deprecations per 16 bytes of its encrypted messages (around 300,000 lines for a 300 KB message, from the AES counter's block numbers), and "substr(): Passing null" (a dry run, PHP 8.1+) and "Creation of dynamic property `$hasRun`" (PHP 8.2+, a misspelt declaration in Akeeba Kickstart's code) ones. The conversion is explicit now, with the same values: verified that the old and new code decrypt each other's messages, on PHP 7.4 and 8.5.
- Two-factor authentication on PHP 8 (stock Joomla 3): a user whose two-factor settings were still stored encrypted, the format of Joomla 3.2 to 3.6 (they're rewritten in the current format on first use, so users who hadn't logged in since), couldn't log in, and their profile and their page in Users: Manage stopped with "Undefined constant MCRYPT_RIJNDAEL_128". Reading the old format creates FOF's mcrypt encryption class, which falls back to OpenSSL when PHP has no mcrypt (removed in PHP 7.2), but its settings named mcrypt's constants, which PHP 8 refuses as soon as the class is created. They're now the constants' values. Verified on PHP 8.5 with settings stored in the old format: the profile failed before; now it opens, the settings are rewritten in the current format with the same key, and logging in with the code works (a wrong code is refused). Joomla Update's extraction script had the same class (used only without OpenSSL), fixed the same way.
- FOF layouts (`FOFLayoutHelper::render()`, used by extensions built on FOF 2) stopped with "Call to undefined function each()" on PHP 8: `each()` was removed in PHP 8.0. Now a `foreach`, with the same order (the template's override first). Verified by rendering a layout with the old and new code on PHP 7.4 and 8.5.
- PHP 8.1+ deprecation warnings when database iterators were used (`$db->getIterator()`, Joomla's and FOF's: `current()`, `key()`, `next()`, `rewind()`, `valid()`, `count()`) and when extensions loaded the bundled Joomla Framework event and session packages (`Joomla\Event\Event`, which also gets `__serialize()`/`__unserialize()` alongside its deprecated `Serializable` methods, `ListenersPriorityQueue`, `Joomla\Session\Session`): their methods are marked `#[\ReturnTypeWillChange]`. Found by loading every FOF class (and checking the rest of the codebase's iterator, array access and countable classes) on PHP 8.5 with all errors reported; none remain.
- When the site's template files were missing (e.g. a template folder deleted by hand), the site stopped with an error instead of falling back to another template (stock Joomla 3 fell back to Beez 3, which this distribution no longer ships). It now falls back to the default template style, else any installed template whose files are there, and shows Joomla's "no template" notice.
- The removed Beez 3 and Hathor templates and "Quick Icon - PHP Version Check" and "Quick Icon - Joomla 3.10 End of Support" plugins are no longer treated as core extensions (`ExtensionHelper::getCoreExtensions()`), and the Template Manager and Redirect no longer check for Hathor as the administrator template. Only the update script's cleanup of their files and rows, the old database migrations and the release notes still name them.
- Articles with "Link Author" on failed with a 500 error ("Table '#__contact_details' doesn't exist") when Contacts had been uninstalled but its "Content - Contact" plugin was left (stock Joomla 3). The plugin, and "Privacy - Contacts" for data exports, now do nothing while Contacts isn't installed and enabled: the author just isn't linked.
- Smart Search: a full reindex stopped with "The table '#__finder_tokens' is full" on MySQL/MariaDB with default settings ([#23](https://github.com/joomlaworks/joomla-3.x/issues/23), stock Joomla 3). The indexer works in two MEMORY tables, which store their text columns at full width (about 620 bytes a row with utf8mb4), so the default `max_heap_table_size` of 16 MB holds about 27,000 rows, fewer than the 30,000 at which the indexer moves them to disk (the "memory_table_limit" option; the token insert checked only its own rows, not the table's). One long article stopped the run and lost its index. The limit is now capped by what `max_heap_table_size` holds (a kilobyte a row), and should a table still fill up, the indexer moves both tables to disk and runs the insert again. Verified on MySQL 8.4 with a 40,000-word article: the old code stopped, the new one indexed all 119,458 terms (PHP 7.4 and 8.5).
- Smart Search from the command line (`finder:index`, and `cli/finder_indexer.php`, which runs it): indexing stopped with "Call to a member function getTag() on null" when a content plugin rendered a module into the content being indexed (as Modules Anywhere-style plugins do) ([#21](https://github.com/joomlaworks/joomla-3.x/issues/21), stock Joomla 3): the command indexes with the site application, whose language was never loaded, and modules' `getParams()` reads its tag. The site application now gets the language, Smart Search as its active component (modules read the component's options; PHP 8.5 also warned about a null array offset) and the `JPATH_COMPONENT*` constants, which plugins read and PHP 8 makes fatal when undefined.
- Smart Search's Advanced Search: setting only a start date ended the range today, and setting only an end date found nothing at all, as the empty date became "now" (`JFactory::getDate('')`) ([#9](https://github.com/joomlaworks/joomla-3.x/issues/9), stock Joomla 3, still in Joomla 5). An empty date now sets no limit. How filters combine taxonomy branches is unchanged.
- Uninstalling a core component left the modules and plugins which only serve it installed and enabled, e.g. "Smart Search - Contacts" after Contacts, which then stopped every Smart Search indexing run with "Table '#__contact_details' doesn't exist", or the Search module and plugins after Search (stock Joomla 3). Every core component's manifest now names its companions (`<companions>`: `<module>` and `<plugin>` entries, e.g. Contacts' content, editors-xtd, privacy, Smart Search, search and user plugins), and uninstalling the component uninstalls them too (`ComponentAdapter`). A companion two components share (System - Highlight, for Search and Smart Search) stays while the other one is installed; one that needs both (Smart Search - Contacts) goes with either. On update, sites which uninstalled a removable core component before get its leftover companions removed; "Restore uninstalled core extensions" brings each component back with all of them (System - Highlight joined Smart Search's group, and an extension in two groups is restored once). The protected components list theirs too, for removals to come.
- Uninstalling some core extensions left files behind, as their manifests didn't list them (stock Joomla 3): the media folders of Multilingual Associations, Contacts (also `media/contacts`), Articles, Content History, Fields, Menus, Modules, Wrapper, the Language Switcher module and seven plugins (reCAPTCHA, reCAPTCHA Invisible, the three update/privacy quick icons, System - Highlight, System - Stats, Two Factor - Google Authenticator), and the administrator Statistics module listed the site module's language files instead of its own. Their manifests now list them, and updates delete the media of the removable ones again when they're uninstalled.
- PHP 8.2+/8.5 deprecations ([#20](https://github.com/joomlaworks/joomla-3.x/issues/20)): the administrator's toolbar title was set on the application as a dynamic property (`JComponentTitle`, now declared), and iterating a `Registry`, a session or a `Joomla\Data` object built an `ArrayIterator` on an object (an array now, with the same keys and values). The bundled `joomla/data` package also lacked `#[\ReturnTypeWillChange]` on its interface methods and used `SplObjectStorage::attach()` (deprecated in PHP 8.5). The issue's other findings were fixed in 3.17.
- PHP 8.2+ deprecation in forms with an SQL list field using the `header` attribute (`SqlField::$header` was set as a dynamic property).
- Users' and Contacts' Options failed with a 500 error once Fields had been uninstalled (possible since 3.16): their "fieldgroups" option, a field type of Fields, fell back to a text field, which can't show its list of values ("htmlspecialchars(): Argument #1 must be of type string, array given"). A field whose type can't be loaded is now left out when its value is a list.
- Languages' Multilingual Status failed with a 500 error once Contacts had been uninstalled (possible since 3.16): its check of contacts per language ran without the table. It's skipped without Contacts or Articles.
- Tags' manifest listed `newsfeeds.php` instead of `tags.php` among its site files (stock Joomla 3), so installing Tags from a package left out its entry file.
- "User - Joomla!" could be uninstalled, after which nobody could log in, on the site or in the administrator (stock Joomla 3): it's the user plugin which accepts logins. It's a protected core extension now, on new sites and, through `3.18.0-2026-10-11.sql`, on existing ones.
- Batch copying or moving articles failed with "Class 'FieldsHelper' not found" once Fields had been uninstalled (possible since 3.16), and so did Privacy's data exports ("Privacy - Content", "- Contacts", "- User Accounts" add each item's custom fields): both now skip custom fields without Fields.
- PostgreSQL ("PostgreSQL" driver, PHP 8.1+): with Debug System on, every page failed with "PostgreSQL result has already been closed". Before each query, debug mode frees the previous result, which the `load*()` methods had usually freed already; PHP 8.1 throws on a second free where earlier versions warned (which was silenced). The driver now forgets a result it freed, as the MySQL drivers do, and ignores one freed elsewhere (also in FOF's copy).
- PostgreSQL ("PostgreSQL" driver, PHP 8.1+): with caching on, code passing an object which holds the database to a cache callback failed with "Serialization of 'PgSql\Connection' is not allowed" (PHP 8.1 made connections and results objects, which can't be serialized). The driver leaves them out when serialized and connects again when used, as the PDO drivers do (also in FOF's copy).
- PostgreSQL (both drivers): saving a new item whose form posted an empty ID failed with "null value in column "id" ... violates not-null constraint": `insertObject()` left out the primary key only when it was `0`, and sent an empty one as NULL. An empty key is left out too now, as on SQL Server (also in FOF's copy).
- SQLite: tables were recorded with the wrong collation (stock MySQL-on-SQLite layer, unfixed upstream): a table got the first collation in its CREATE TABLE statement, often that of a column (Joomla's `alias` columns are `utf8mb4_bin`), and columns without a collation of their own `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci` instead of the table's. SQLite compares text the same way regardless, but `SHOW CREATE TABLE` also left out each column's collation, so a site moved to MySQL with `database:convert` (or a `database:export` imported there) had tables such as `#__content`, `#__categories`, `#__menu` and `#__tags` in `utf8mb4_bin`, titles included: searches and other text comparisons became case-sensitive there. Tables now take their own `DEFAULT COLLATE`/`DEFAULT CHARSET`, columns the table's unless they have one (also when added or changed later), and `SHOW CREATE TABLE` writes the columns' own, as MySQL does. What existing SQLite sites recorded is repaired by updating (`JDatabaseDriverMysqlonsqlite::repairCollations()`, from the update's utf8mb4 step), by Extensions: Database and `maintenance:database`, which report it as a problem and repair it with Fix/`--fix` (for sites whose files were updated by copying), and by `database:convert` before it copies an SQLite database: a table whose collation came from a column gets `utf8mb4_unicode_ci`, and its columns without one the table's. Verified: a new site, and one installed with the old layer and then updated, both convert to MySQL with exactly the collations of `installation/sql/mysql/joomla.sql` (53 tables `utf8mb4_unicode_ci`, 25 `utf8mb4_general_ci`, the 9 alias-type columns `utf8mb4_bin`), and back to SQLite with the same.
- PostgreSQL ("PostgreSQL" driver): `insertid()` after an INSERT run as a string (`setQuery("INSERT INTO ...")`, common in third-party code) failed with "Call to a member function getElements() on null", and after an INSERT built as a query object for another table it could return that table's last ID: it read the table from the last query object built, not the query run. It returns the session's last sequence value (`lastval()`) now, as the PDO driver's `lastInsertId()` and MySQL's `LAST_INSERT_ID()` do (also in FOF's copy).
- MySQL ("MySQLi" driver, PHP 8.4+): a "mysqli_ping() is deprecated" warning whenever the driver checked its connection (e.g. the command line, after a lost connection). It asks the server with `mysqli_stat()` on PHP 8.4+ (`mysqli_ping()` no longer reconnects since PHP 8.2 anyway); a dropped connection is still detected and reconnected (also in FOF's copy).
- `database:import` (and so `database:convert`) into MariaDB or MySQL 5.7 refused tables of a MySQL 8 export with "Unknown collation": MySQL 8's `utf8mb4_0900_*` collations and `utf8mb3` name don't exist there (SQLite databases record `utf8mb4_0900_ai_ci` for tables created without a collation too). A collation or character set the server doesn't know is replaced in the table's statement: `utf8mb3` by `utf8` (`utf8mb3_general_ci` by `utf8_general_ci`), another collation by its character set's `_unicode_ci`, else that character set's default.
- SQLite: `LIKE` ignored the case of ASCII letters only (stock MySQL-on-SQLite layer, SQLite's own `LIKE`), so searches in Greek, Cyrillic or accented Latin text found nothing unless typed with the same case: the administrator lists' search, the site's search, tag lookups and extensions' searches. `LIKE` is now the driver's own function (`JDatabaseMysqlonsqliteFunctions::like()`), matching MySQL's `utf8mb4_unicode_ci`: case and accents are ignored in every script (`αθηνα` finds `Αθήνα`, `ecole` finds `École`), characters are compared one by one as MySQL's `LIKE` does (`ß` isn't `ss`), trailing spaces count, and a pattern ending in the escape character matches it. Accents are removed with PHP's intl extension, or without it with a table of the Latin, Greek and Cyrillic letters (`foldmap.php`, generated from the same rules by `docs/sqlite/build-fold-map.php`); "is this username taken" checks use the same table without intl now, where they only folded case. Plain ASCII text takes a quicker way. Verified against MySQL 8.4: 961 value/pattern pairs (Greek with and without tonos, Cyrillic, French, German, Vietnamese, full-width, escapes, `%`/`_`) give MySQL's results, with and without intl, on PHP 8.5 and 7.4. Cost, on 5,221 articles of about 2,600 characters: the administrator's article search 98 → 145 ms, the site's search 223 → 270 ms for English words and 214 → 354 ms for Greek ones (which found nothing before).
- Search (com_search, every database): results found by the database were dropped when the search word was typed without the accents of a non-Latin script, e.g. `αθηνα` for `Αθήνα` (stock Joomla 3). `SearchHelper::remove_accents()`, which checks the results again in PHP and places the highlighting, only removed the accents of Latin letters; it removes those of every script now (Greek tonos, Vietnamese, Cyrillic `ё`...), with PHP's intl extension, else Greek's with a table. The results' text is shown as it is, accents included, and highlighted in place.
- Smart Search on PostgreSQL: looking a link up by its URL, which the indexer does for every item it indexes, saves or removes, scanned the whole `#__finder_links` table (stock Joomla 3): its URL index was on `substr(url,0,76)`, which `url = ...` queries can't use (3.7 ms against 0.14 ms per lookup with 50,000 links, measured with EXPLAIN ANALYZE). It's a plain index on `url` now (`#__finder_links_idx_url_full`; new sites, Smart Search's own install SQL, and `3.18.0-2026-10-17.sql` for existing sites, which drops the old index; sites converted with `database:convert` already had a usable one, which it replaces). The migration's statements are written so that Extensions: Database can check them (its PostgreSQL check skips statements of fewer than six words, e.g. most single-column `CREATE INDEX` statements of earlier migrations). Verified on a converted site with each kind of index: through the update's schema step and through `maintenance:database --fix`, then a clean check, and the lookup using the new index.
- Sample Data (the Control Panel's Sample Data module): installing a set after an earlier install of a set had stopped partway (e.g. a failed step, then another try) removed the menu assignments of every module shown on all pages, site and administrator, so they all disappeared (also in 3.17). Removing the previous set deleted the assignments of its menu items with `menuid IN (...)`, and an empty list is written as `IN (0)`: menu item 0 is "on all pages". The assignments are only removed for the menu items and modules the set added, and a safeguard in core now makes this impossible: `JModuleHelper::removeMenuAssignments()` (`Joomla\CMS\Helper\ModuleHelper`) removes the module assignments of deleted menu items, takes only positive menu item IDs (so an empty, unset or zero list never reaches "on all pages") and is used wherever menu items' assignments are removed: deleting a menu item, uninstalling a component (its site menu items) and the Sample Data plugin.
- PHP 8.1+ deprecation when a category was saved without a request, e.g. by the Sample Data plugin (`explode(): Passing null`, in the category model's association check): with errors displayed, it also broke the Sample Data module's steps, whose answer then wasn't valid JSON.
- Deleting a menu item: a module shown "on all pages except" it alone was then shown on no pages (stock Joomla 3 left its `-id` assignment row; uninstalling a component with menu items, which since 3.18 removes its site menu items, removed those rows and hid such modules). Its exception is removed now, and a module left with no exceptions is on all pages again. A menu item table's `delete()` called without an ID (after `load()`, as the API allows) also failed on an empty `menuid = ` condition after the item was already deleted, leaving its assignments; it uses the loaded item's ID.
- Inline scripts and styles added more than once, e.g. from a loop or a module shown twice, were written as many times (stock Joomla 3: `addScriptDeclaration()`/`addStyleDeclaration()` appended every call). The same declaration for the same type is now kept once; only a whole declaration counts, never part of another. `addCustomTag()` also keeps a tag once when it's added (the head already wrote duplicates once). Verified with the real head renderer: three loops of the same calls wrote each declaration three times before, once now.
- Scripts and stylesheets: a boolean attribute set to `false` was written with an empty value, which turns it on (stock Joomla 3 handled `false` for `async` and `defer` only, so `'nomodule' => false` wrote `nomodule=""`), and `true` wrote `="1"`. Every attribute now works as `async` and `defer` do: `true` writes it without a value (`name="name"` outside HTML5), `false` or `null` leaves it out. A stylesheet given its own `rel` (e.g. `alternate stylesheet`) had it written twice. Documented in API-GUIDE.md, with what `addScript()`/`addStyleSheet()` accept (`type="module"`, `id`, `integrity`, `data-*`, ...).
- CodeMirror's ASN.1 mode never loaded outside debug mode (stock Joomla 3): its minified file was named `asn.min.js`, but the editor loads `mode/asn.1/asn.1.min.js`. The file now has the right name, and updates delete the old one.
- Media Manager (stock Joomla 3): files of several types the default options allow couldn't be uploaded: ICO, every OpenDocument format, `.xls` and `.ppt` were refused as invalid MIME types, because the MIME types list didn't have their types (and named Excel and PowerPoint files `application/excel` and `application/powerpoint`, which PHP never reports). With PHP 7.4, `.xlsx` files were refused because its file detection reports their type twice in a row; that's now read as one type. Listing a folder with GIMP or Draw files (or images PHP can't read) warned on PHP 8 ("Trying to access array offset on false"); they're listed as documents.
- Studio sample data: nine articles showed a stray full stop on a line of its own after their quote (the generator added the quote's closing full stop after `</blockquote>`). Fixed in the generator and the content, and the set rebuilt.
- Front-end article form (stock Joomla 3): its fallback for sites whose Articles options were never saved (hide Images and Links) read and set a property of the options object instead of an option, so it never worked, and PHP 8.2+ warned about a dynamic property on every page with the form. It uses the options' `exists()` and `set()` now.
- Media Manager (stock Joomla 3): the Create Folder row showed an empty grey box before the new folder's name. It's meant to show the folder the new one goes in (`images`, `images/news`), but the script set `.value` on a jQuery object, which did nothing, so the box kept only the folder relative to `images` (empty at the top). It also gave the page the server's absolute path to the images folder (`basepath`); it now gets the folder relative to the site. Both templates: the box shows `images` or `images/sampledata/news`.
- Command line: `article:update` (new in 3.17) ignored `--text`/`--text-file` and removed the HTML from the article's text, also when only another field (e.g. `--title`) was changed: "<p>Some <strong>bold</strong> text</p>" was saved as "Some bold text". The command loaded the article with its `introtext` and `fulltext` columns, which the article form filters as plain text, and the table saved those over the text it splits from `articletext`. It now gives the text as `articletext` only, as the edit form posts it.
- Hammond, Finch and Rookwood: buttons in article text (links with the `btn` class, such as Joomla's "Register to read more" for restricted articles, or an author's own) took the text's link styles, which outrank the buttons': in Hammond and Finch the label was the colour of the button (unreadable until hovered), underlined and in the text's serif font; in Rookwood the hover turned the label the accent colour, and a primary button (`btnPrimary`) lost its text colour. They look like the templates' other buttons now, and buttons styled as links (`btn-link`) stay links. Checked in all three, light and dark.
- Installer (stock Joomla 3): a step that worked but had a warning to show (e.g. while installing sample data) started the installation over: the installer's script took any message as a failure and went to the view the step named, which for those steps is the installation itself, so the steps ran again and again, the warnings piling up. The warnings now stay on the page and the installation goes on; steps that fail still go back to the step to correct.
- CodeMirror (stock Joomla 3): with its stock theme ("default"), every page with the editor asked for `media/editors/codemirror/theme/default.css`, which doesn't exist (a 404 each time). CodeMirror's default look is in its main stylesheet; that theme no longer loads a file.
- Language strings that were missing, so their keys showed instead of text: Language Overrides' message for an override with quotes, which only Super Users may save (since 3.16: `COM_LANGUAGES_ERROR_QUOTES_IN_TEXT` was never added), and, in stock Joomla 3, the Extend Consent page's default heading (Privacy), the logout image's `alt` text (the layout asked for `COM_USER_...` instead of `COM_USERS_...`), the extension installer's errors for a module that already exists and for uninstalling a module of an unknown client, the deprecated `JMail::sendAdminMail()`'s subject and message, the deprecated `JHtmlRules`' inherited permission tooltips and the plugins field's warning when it has no folder. Front-end Template and Module options: a failed save showed `JERROR_SAVE_FAILED` (an administrator string, with a placeholder nothing filled); it now shows the reason the save failed, and a form that can't be loaded says so.
- Google reCAPTCHA on pages that load MooTools (e.g. the front-end article form, through Joomla's modal behaviour): Google's script never called back, so with the invisible type (and possibly the checkbox and v3) the form never sent. MooTools 1.4.5 replaces the browser's `Array.from()` with one that wraps anything without a `length` (a Set, a Map's keys) in an array and ignores a mapping function, while Google's script relies on the standard behaviour. MooTools' `Array.from()` is patched: the browser's own for those calls, MooTools' unchanged otherwise. hCaptcha and Turnstile weren't affected.
- The Smart Search module's field could not be emptied: leaving it empty put the previous text back (a leftover of the time its prompt was a value, not a placeholder).
- Modules dragged to a new place in their position (Modules list sorted by ordering) went back to where they were on the next page load: modules created in the administrator or from the command line all had ordering 0 (stock Joomla 3), the list gave those same values out again after a drag, and the save, seeing nothing changed, stored nothing. Dragging now gives the rows distinct values in their new order (repairing such positions, in any list), new modules go last in their position, and the sample data and the Control Panel's stock modules have distinct orderings.
- Smart Search's Advanced Search button did nothing with Hammond, Finch or Rookwood (it relied on Bootstrap's script, which they don't load); with Rookwood the advanced search couldn't be opened at all. It's plain JavaScript now, and the panel is hidden until opened in every template.
- PHP warnings "Undefined array key 1" in `Joomla\CMS\Environment\Browser` (stock Joomla 3) for user agents whose version has no minor part, e.g. Safari's `Version/18` or an in-app browser's `Chrome/141`: the major and minor version were split with `list() = explode('.')`. They're read by one method now, the minor version 0 when there's none; `isViewable()` likewise for a MIME type without a `/`.
- Edit forms rendered the item's ID field twice, as two hidden inputs both named `jform[id]` with the ID `jform_id` (stock Joomla 3): the sidebar's layout (`joomla.edit.global`) made the field hidden and the Publishing tab's (`joomla.edit.publishingdata`) rendered it again. Each of the two layouts now skips a field the other already rendered. Checked on the edit forms of articles, categories, menu items, menus, modules, users, groups, access levels, tags, fields, plugins, template styles and redirects: no duplicate IDs left, and saving keeps the item's ID.

## Version 3.17 - released October 10th, 2026

### New features
- **AI Assistants & the Command Line** (`AI-GUIDE.md`): a guide to connecting AI assistants to a site through the MCP server (Claude Code, Claude Desktop, Devin Desktop (formerly Windsurf), Cursor, VS Code, Gemini CLI, Codex CLI and any stdio client, locally or over SSH), choosing what they may do (`--allow-write`, `--allow-code`, `--allow`/`--deny`, `--as`, with example setups), how the tools behave (names, dry runs, answers, errors), safety and traceability, and the command line itself: global options, exit codes, recipes for every area, scripting with JSON, cron jobs, troubleshooting and a command reference. Linked from the README. The MCP server's instructions and the help of `template:file:set`/`template:info` now name Rookwood next to Hammond and Finch as loading `css/custom.css`.
- **Housekeeping** (`mod_housekeeping`), an administrator module in the status bar, published on new sites and added to updated ones (also from older Joomla 3 versions; it can be uninstalled and restored like the other removable core extensions). **Clean Cache** cleans every group of the site's and the administrator's cache in one click, whatever the cache handler (file, Memcached, Redis, APCu, WinCache); everyone who can log in to the administrator has it. For those who manage the cache and Global Check-in (Administrators and Super Users), a menu opening above it adds **Clean Everything** (the cache, its expired entries, and everything in the site's and the administrator's cache folders and the temporary folder but their `index.html`, `.htaccess` and `web.config`; a folder outside the site's folder, e.g. a temporary folder set to the server's `/tmp`, or one of Joomla's own folders is left alone and named) and **Global Check-in** (unlocks every item open for editing), at once (no confirmation: they are for administrators). Requests go through com_ajax as POST only, with the form token in the request's body (never in the URL, which ends up in logs), and the server checks each action's permission, and that the module is published for the user (com_ajax itself only checks that it's installed); Clean Everything leaves the temporary folder alone while a Joomla update is being extracted from it; the result shows in a small green or red message above the status bar, announced to screen readers, which hides after a few seconds. Plain JavaScript and CSS of its own, with SVG icons (no jQuery, Bootstrap or icon font).
- TinyMCE 8 (8.9.2, the current release) as a new editor plugin, "Editor - TinyMCE" (`plg_editors_tinymce_latest`), next to the TinyMCE 4.5.12 one, which is renamed "Editor - TinyMCE (legacy)" and otherwise unchanged. TinyMCE is GPL 2 or later since version 7, so it's bundled (with `license_key: 'gpl'`, which keeps it from going read-only, and without TinyMCE's branding and promotions), with its 61 translations (from the `tinymce-i18n` package, under TinyMCE's licence). New sites use it (the installer, `core:install` and the Global Configuration default); existing sites keep their editor and switch in Global Configuration, or per user, and can switch back. A separate plugin, rather than an upgrade of the old one, because TinyMCE 4 → 8 changes the toolbar button names (saved in the old plugin's sets), drops or moves several plugins (templates is a paid plugin now), can't run TinyMCE 4 plugins of third-party extensions, and handles content differently (iframes are sandboxed, `<object>`/`<embed>` converted). Joomla never patched TinyMCE itself (every file Joomla 3 shares with TinyMCE 4.5.12 is identical), so only the Joomla integration needed writing, in plain JavaScript:
  - **Joomla's editor API:** `Joomla.editors.instances` (getValue, setValue, getSelection, replaceSelection), `jInsertEditorText()`, the Toggle Editor button, the template's `editor.css` for the content, read-only fields, editors in subforms (also after sorting rows: an editor whose iframe the browser empties is started again).
  - **Editor buttons** (Article, Image, Menu, Module, Contact, Page Break, Read More and those of extensions) below the editor, as with Joomla 3's other editors, with their usual windows; they insert through Joomla's editor API (`Joomla.editors.instances`, `jInsertEditorText()`).
  - **Large images and embeds** stay within the editing area instead of making it scroll sideways (a `max-width` rule for the editor only; the saved content and the site are unchanged).
  - **Images** dropped or pasted into the editor, or chosen in the image dialog's Upload tab, are uploaded to the images folder (or the set's folder) through the Media Manager, for users who may create files there (TinyMCE's own upload handling, which replaces the old drag-and-drop plugin; pasted images get unique names).
  - **Templates:** TinyMCE removed its template plugin from the open source edition, so "Insert template" is the plugin's own dialog, with a preview, for the HTML files of `media/editors/tinymce_latest/templates`.
  - **Toolbar builder** (the plugin's options): sets of menus and buttons for user groups, as before, with TinyMCE 8's buttons and icons, the presets of Joomla 4+ and per-set options (skins, toolbar overflow, image uploads and folder, entity encoding, language, template CSS, URLs, new lines, paste as text, text filters or valid/prohibited elements, iframe sandboxing with extra trusted domains, resizing, element path, word count, advanced image and list, autosave, context menu, custom plugins and buttons). Drag and drop is native (no jQuery UI), with a remove button and the Delete key as alternatives to dragging out.
  - Verified in Chrome on PHP 7.4 and 8.5: a new site with the blog sample data (editing, every editor button tried with Article and Read More, a template, an uploaded image in the backend and on the frontend, toggling, saving with the content in the database); the toolbar builder (dragging in, reordering, dragging out, presets, a user group in one set only, saving, the editor using the saved set); switching the site between the two editors; an update of a 3.17 site keeping its editor; uninstalling and restoring; the install SQL and migration on MySQL 8.4, PostgreSQL 18 and SQLite.
- The "Install from Web" installer plugin (`plg_installer_webinstaller`, the Joomla! Extensions Directory browser in Extensions: Install) is bundled and enabled, in its latest version (2.1.2), with its tab first: Install from Web, Upload Package File, Install from Folder, Install from URL. It had to be added with the "Add Install from Web tab" button, which installs it from the JED's feed; that feed offers no version for Joomla 3.11 and later (its versions are limited to 3.2–3.10 and 4.x), so on this distribution the button could not work. Sites updating from an earlier version get it once, enabled; a copy installed from the JED is kept (with its settings), its leftover files from 1.x and the JED update site are removed. Admins can uninstall it (it then stays uninstalled on updates, and "Restore uninstalled core extensions" brings it back); while it's disabled, the JED message's button now leads to it in the Plugin Manager. Changes to the 2.1.2 package: the Hathor parts are removed (Hathor isn't in this distribution), and its scripts only load on Extensions: Install (they were added to every page which loads the installer plugins, e.g. Privacy: Capabilities); the fixes are listed under bug fixes. Its JavaScript is rewritten in plain JavaScript, without jQuery or Bootstrap: the directory is loaded with `fetch()` instead of JSONP script tags, and its buttons use data attributes instead of inline handlers (the names the directory's own markup calls are kept). Verified on PHP 7.4 and 8.5, with fresh installs on MySQL 8.4, PostgreSQL 18 and SQLite, and updates from a site with the JED's 1.1.1: the JED's list for Joomla 3.17 is the same as for 3.10.
- PostgreSQL in the command line database tools. `database:export` and `database:import` work on PostgreSQL (with either driver), keeping each table's columns, defaults, keys and sequences, and moving each sequence past the imported rows; a PostgreSQL site exported and imported again has identical rows, column defaults and indexes. `database:import` also reads files of the other kind of database, translating the structure. `database:convert` moves a site between MySQL/MariaDB, PostgreSQL and SQLite in any direction (`--to=postgresql` or `--to=pgsql`, with `--host`, `--user`, `--password` and `--database`). Between MySQL and PostgreSQL it translates column types (e.g. unsigned integers become wider ones, MySQL's `char` becomes `character varying`, as PostgreSQL pads `character` values with spaces; PostgreSQL's unlimited `text` becomes `mediumtext`), auto-increment columns (`serial`/`bigserial`), keys (prefix lengths where MySQL needs them; no fulltext indexes on PostgreSQL), Joomla's "no date" values (`0000-00-00 00:00:00` on MySQL, `1970-01-01 00:00:00` on PostgreSQL), time zone offsets of PostgreSQL's "with time zone" columns (dropped, keeping the time Joomla stored), booleans and binary data. NOT NULL columns without a default get the empty value MySQL would use, as code written for MySQL leaves such columns out of inserts. The site is taken offline while its database is copied (unless it already was), and put back online with the switch, or with its current database if the conversion fails (verified by forcing a failure part way). After copying and comparing every table's rows, it fixes the new database's structure as Extensions: Database > Fix does (column types and indexes of the core tables, the utf8mb4 conversion record), so the site starts with "Database table structure is up to date". Verified in every direction on copies of real installations: PostgreSQL to MySQL and back (identical rows), PostgreSQL to SQLite, SQLite to PostgreSQL, and a MySQL installation to PostgreSQL, each followed by the database check, front end and administrator pages, and saving content.
- PostgreSQL install and uninstall scripts for Banners, Contacts and News Feeds, which only shipped MySQL ones, so reinstalling them after an uninstall (also through "Restore uninstalled core extensions") failed on PostgreSQL. They're the definitions of the main PostgreSQL installation file. Verified on PostgreSQL 18 and MySQL 8.4: uninstall (tables, files and menu items removed), restore through a Joomla update, the components' pages, and uninstalling again.
- New SQLite database driver ("SQLite (experimental)"), for small to medium sites, development and testing: the whole site in one file, with no database server. It runs Joomla's and extensions' MySQL SQL unchanged, including raw SQL which doesn't use Joomla's query builder and extensions' MySQL install and update scripts, through the bundled "MySQL on SQLite" emulation layer (`libraries/vendor/wordpress/mysql-on-sqlite`, from WordPress's SQLite Database Integration project, GPL-2.0-or-later). The driver reports itself as MySQL 8 to the code, so no SQLite-specific SQL files are needed; it emulates `SHOW`/`DESCRIBE`/`INFORMATION_SCHEMA`, MySQL's case-insensitive comparisons and `ALTER TABLE ... MODIFY` (by rebuilding the table). Needs PHP 7.4 or newer and the PDO SQLite extension with SQLite 3.37.0 or newer; it's only offered where those are available. Tested on SQLite 3.37.2 (Ubuntu 22.04's, the oldest supported) and 3.46.1. Verified on a copy of a site with K2, VirtueMart and other extensions (front end, administrator, saving content, installing and updating extensions, Joomla updates) and with a fresh installation.
  - The installer offers it, asking for the database file instead of a server, user and password; it suggests `database/joomla-<random>.sqlite`. A folder inside the site gets `.htaccess` and `web.config` rules which block web access to it, and the random name protects it on servers which ignore those (e.g. nginx); a folder outside the web root is safest. A database file directly in the site's root folder is refused. As SQLite needs no database user or password, the installer always asks to confirm the site is yours (by deleting a file it creates), as it does for remote databases; otherwise anyone who found a newly uploaded, not yet installed site could install it.
