The database's folder is only created, and the location only checked, after that confirmation, so a request from anyone else writes nothing else and learns nothing about the server's folders. The file name must end in `.sqlite`, `.sqlite3`, `.db` or `.db3` (a name a web server would run, e.g. `.php`, is refused, since the file holds content anyone can write), and inside the site the folder must hold nothing else (e.g. not `images/`, whose web access the protection rules would block); Global Configuration and `database:convert` apply the same rules.
  - The driver adds MySQL functions the emulation lacks or gets wrong (`IF()`, which always returned its "else" value; Unicode `LOWER()`/`UPPER()`; `FIND_IN_SET()`, `SUBSTRING_INDEX()`, `RIGHT()`, `LPAD()`/`RPAD()`, `UUID()`, `LAST_INSERT_ID()`). The vendored emulation carries five translator fixes, listed in its `PATCHES.md` (`STRAIGHT_JOIN` and other SELECT hints, `GROUP_CONCAT(... SEPARATOR ...)`, parenthesized `UNION` operands, `INSERT ... SELECT` with unaliased values).
  - Usernames and email addresses are checked for being taken as MySQL compares them: SQLite tells apart only ASCII letter case, so `ａｄｍｉｎ` (full-width), `àdmin`, `ÀDMİN` or `Foldtést` could have been registered next to `admin` or `foldtest`, look-alikes MySQL refuses. The checks (saving a user, the registration and user forms) fold trailing spaces, accents, case (`ß` as `ss`) and compatibility forms such as full-width letters (accents and compatibility forms need PHP's intl extension). Logging in and password resets still look names up exactly. Matches MySQL 8.4's verdict on 22 test names; verified from the command line, the user form and the registration form's rules.
  - Joomla's ordering code (`Table::reorder()`) used MySQL user variables, which the emulation can't assign inside expressions; the driver's query class uses the standard `ROW_NUMBER()` window function instead. The update script skips the MySQL-only storage engine check and records the utf8mb4 conversion as done (SQLite stores all text as UTF-8).
  - Global Configuration shows a "Path to Database Folder" option for SQLite, instead of the server, user, password and database name, which SQLite doesn't use. Changing it moves the database: Joomla writes a consistent copy into the new folder (SQLite's `VACUUM INTO`, safe while other requests use the database), checks it, switches the running request to it, saves `configuration.php`, and only then deletes the old file (and the old folder inside the site, when only its protection files are left). If any step fails, the site keeps using the database where it was. Folders inside the site get the same web-access rules as the installer's, and a database there is stored relative to the site's root, so the site can still be moved as a whole; the site's root folder itself and a folder which already has a file of that name are refused. `config:set db=...` on the command line moves the database the same way.
  - System Information (and its text/JSON downloads), the Statistics modules (administrator and site) and the `site:info` command show the database as SQLite with its real version, e.g. "3.46.1 (MySQL 8.0.38 emulation)", instead of the MySQL server the driver reports to the code. The driver's internal name stays `mysqlonsqlite`, as Joomla 3 already has a basic `sqlite` driver with different behaviour.
  - Sessions: the "Database" session handler writes to the database on every page view, which for SQLite means rewriting the database file all the time, even while only browsing. The installer therefore sets SQLite sites to PHP sessions (the "PHP" session handler), and `database:convert --to=sqlite` switches a site from "Database" to PHP sessions, keeping Memcached, Redis or APCu (preferable for performance where available) as they are. Measured on a site's copy: with database sessions, every administrator and front-end page view changed the session table; with PHP sessions, browsing the administrator wrote nothing, and the front end wrote once per new visitor (the session's who's-online record).
  - Known gaps, unused by Joomla core: `DIV`, `TIMESTAMPDIFF()`, `LENGTH()` counting characters instead of bytes, `ADD COLUMN ... AFTER` adding the column at the end. SQLite allows one write at a time, so busy sites with many simultaneous writers should stay on MySQL/MariaDB.
- New `database:convert` command line command, which moves a site's database between MySQL/MariaDB and SQLite, in both directions (`--to=sqlite`, or `--to=mysqli`/`--to=pdomysql` with `--user`, `--password`, `--database`). It copies every table with the site's prefix into the new database, compares each table's number of rows, and only then changes `configuration.php`; the old database is left as it is. A round trip of a whole site's database (MySQL to SQLite and back to MySQL) gave identical rows in every table. Global Configuration refuses to switch between SQLite and the other database types, which would leave the site's data in the old database, and points to this command.
- New command line interface, `cli/joomla.php`, with the command names, arguments and options of the Joomla 4 and later CLI, built on Joomla 3 code with no new dependencies. Run `php cli/joomla.php list` for the commands and `php cli/joomla.php help <command>` for the details of one. Every command also accepts `--format=json`, which prints one JSON document (`command`, `success`, `exitCode`, `data`, `messages`) for scripts and AI agents; `list --format=json` describes every command, argument and option in one call. Exit codes are 0 for success, 1 for failure, 2 for invalid input, 3 when what was asked for doesn't exist and 4 when it's refused (e.g. the last Super User, a protected extension, missing permissions); in JSON, `error` gives the same as a `code` (`failed`, `invalid`, `not_found`, `refused`) with the `message`. PHP notices go to stderr so they never mix with the output. Extensions can add commands through plugins of the new `console` group, by returning command objects (`Joomla\CMS\Console\AbstractCommand`) from the `onGetConsoleCommands` event.
  - Site and configuration: `site:info` (an overview of the site and its environment; not in Joomla 4+), `site:down`, `site:up`, `config:get` (passwords and keys are masked unless `--show-secrets` is given; the option name can hold the wildcards `*` and `?`, e.g. `config:get 'memcached_*'`, which Joomla 4+ doesn't support), `config:list` (all options, or a `--group`; not in Joomla 4+) and `config:set` (which saves through the Global Configuration model, so its checks apply, e.g. the database settings must connect).
  - Users: `user:list` (optionally only users whose username, name or email matches a name filter, e.g. `user:list '*@example.com'`; not in Joomla 4+), `user:add`, `user:reset-password`, `user:addtogroup`, `user:removefromgroup`, `user:delete`, and `user:block`/`user:unblock` (like Users: Manage > Block/Unblock; blocking also ends the user's sessions; not in Joomla 4+). Missing values are asked for at the terminal (passwords without echo); without interaction the options are required. The last active Super User can't be deleted, blocked or lose Super User rights, and a user can't be removed from their last group.
  - Extensions: `extension:list` (optionally only extensions whose name or element matches a name filter, e.g. `extension:list '*k2*'`, also with `--type`; not in Joomla 4+), `extension:install` (`--path` to a package file or folder, or `--url`), `extension:remove`, `extension:enable`, `extension:disable`, `extension:discover`, `extension:discover:list` (with the same name filter), `extension:discover:install`, and `extension:update` (updates one or more extensions by ID, like Extensions: Update, with the updates found by `update:extensions:check`; not in Joomla 4+). Protected core extensions can't be disabled or removed, nor templates used by a default template style.
  - Updates and maintenance: `core:update:check`, `core:update:channel`, `update:extensions:check` (with a `--use-cache` option, not in Joomla 4+, for frequent cron jobs; the two checks replace the cached update information, as the update screens do, so with `--dry-run` they list what the last check found without fetching anything), `update:joomla:remove-old-files`, `cache:clean`, `session:gc`, `session:metadata:gc` and `finder:index`.
  - Updating Joomla: `core:update` downloads the latest version of the update channel, verifies its checksum when the update feed gives one, copies the files over the site (leaving out `installation/`) and then finalises the update (database changes, update script, removal of obsolete files) in a new PHP process, so the new code does it, like the separate requests of Joomla Update. It logs to `joomla_update.php` and records the update in the User Actions Log, like Joomla Update. `--restore-core` reinstalls uninstalled core extensions, like Joomla Update's "Restore uninstalled core extensions" option, `--reinstall` installs the current version's files again when there is no newer one, and `--file` installs a local package (`.zip`, `.tar`, `.tar.gz`/`.tgz` or `.tar.bz2`, also with all files in one top folder, like a GitHub download) instead of the update channel's, e.g. to test a newer or customised build, also with `--restore-core`; all three are additions over Joomla 4+. Packages older than the installed version are refused, as database changes can't be undone. Unlike Joomla 4+, a site with database structure problems is warned about them but still updated, as Joomla Update does, since updates often fix them. Exits with 0 when there is nothing to update.
  - Database: `database:export` writes each table (or `--table`) to an XML file, optionally in a ZIP archive (`--zip`, or `--zip=name.zip` for a custom name, an addition over Joomla 4+), and `database:import` replaces tables from such files (MySQL and MariaDB only). Files are in the format of Joomla 4+'s commands, so files can be moved between the two in both directions; exports also carry each table's exact `CREATE TABLE` statement, which Joomla 4+ ignores and the import uses, so tables come back identical (verified with `CHECKSUM TABLE` across a whole site's database). Rows are read in batches and written to the file as they're read, and the import reads files as a stream, so large tables need little memory. Unlike Joomla 4+'s, the export keeps `FLOAT` values exactly (MySQL prints them with 6 significant digits), keeps carriage returns, base64-encodes binary values, and writes NULLs as valid XML; the import also reads the Joomla 4+ files whose NULLs aren't valid XML. The import asks for confirmation when interactive. It only takes tables of the site (names with the `#__` prefix, letters, digits and underscores) and checks every statement before any table is replaced: a file's `CREATE TABLE` must create the table it describes, each statement must be one statement, and the column types, defaults (PostgreSQL), `Extra` (MySQL) and index statements the SQL is built from must have the forms an export has (e.g. no generated columns or subqueries), so a crafted file can't run other SQL (PostgreSQL and PDO run several statements given at once); a file which fails changes nothing. `--dry-run` lists the tables named inside the files and applies the same checks (checked against complete exports of MySQL and PostgreSQL sites, and crafted ones).
  - `maintenance:database` lists the problems of Extensions: Database, and fixes them with `--fix`.
  - Commands which change things act as a Super User, since whoever runs the command line can already change `configuration.php`. That user exists only in memory, only for the command's duration, and is never stored in a session. The content commands (below) act as a real account instead.
  - Content: `article:list`, `article:get`, `article:create`, `article:update`, `article:publish`, `article:unpublish`, `article:archive`, `article:trash` and `article:delete`; the same for categories (`category:*`, also of other components with `--extension`, e.g. `com_contact`), modules (`module:*`, with their options as JSON, the pages they show on, and the content of Custom modules) and menu items (`menu:list`, `menu:item:*`, linking to an article, a category blog or list, the featured articles, any component page or URL, an alias, a heading or a separator), and tags (`tag:*`: nested with `--parent`, found by ID, path, alias or title; `tag:list` counts each tag's items, and deleting a tag removes it from them; like categories, a new tag is published unless `--state` says otherwise). All additions over Joomla 4+. They save through Joomla's own administrator models and forms, so the text filters of Global Configuration, content plugins, versions, tags, Smart Search and the "same alias" checks apply as in the administrator. They act as a real account: the first active Super User, or the one given with `--as`, whose permissions are checked (create, edit, edit own, edit state, delete), so e.g. an Editor account can write and change articles but not publish them. New items are unpublished unless `--state=published` is given, so they can be checked first. An item someone has open for editing is refused unless `--force` is given, and only trashed items can be deleted, as in the managers. Long texts can be read from a file or the standard input (`--text-file`).
  - Templates: `template:list` (the styles, which is the default), `template:info` (a style's module positions with their descriptions and modules, its options with their current values, the CSS custom properties of its main stylesheet, its CSS files and overrides, and the file for the site's own CSS), `template:style:update` (options merged as JSON, title, default; menu item assignments are kept), and the files of a template's folder: `template:file:list`, `template:file:get` (binary files in base64 in JSON) and `template:file:set` (whole contents, `--append`, or an exact `--find`/`--replace` which must match once unless `--all`; a new file needs `--create`), and `template:file:delete` (never `index.php` or `templateDetails.xml`). Paths stay inside the template's folder: absolute paths, `..` and links are refused. PHP is checked for syntax errors and XML for well-formedness before it's written, and a file that fails isn't written. Every change keeps the file's previous version, which `--restore` puts back (again to redo; a file the change created is removed), `--dry-run` shows a change as a unified diff, and PHP files are dropped from OPcache. `template:backup` saves a template's folder in a ZIP file, `template:backup:list` lists the backups, and `template:restore` makes the folder what it was in one (changed files, files removed since and files added since), after backing up the current state so the restore can be undone. Backups and previous versions are kept in `backup/<a folder with a name nobody can guess>/` in the site's root, which updates never touch, with rules that block web access on Apache and IIS. All additions over Joomla 4+. Tested with PHP 7.4 and 8.5: every command, path escapes (`..`, absolute paths, links to `/etc` and to `configuration.php`), syntax and XML refusals, undo and redo, backup and restore (including Joomla's ZIP code for servers without `ZipArchive`), and the diffs against GNU `patch` (300 random edits).
  - `--dry-run`, a new global option: a command reports what it would change (as "Would: ..." lines, and a `plan` list in JSON) and changes nothing. Every command which changes something supports it (also `config:set`, `core:update`, `database:convert`, `extension:install` and the others); commands which only read ignore it, and any command (e.g. from an extension) which doesn't support it refuses to run rather than run for real. `list --format=json` and `help` say which commands only read and which support it.
  - `core:install`: installs a new site on SQLite from the package's files (no `configuration.php` yet), with the installer of its `installation` folder, so the result is the same as a web installation with those choices: a database file with an unguessable name in `database/` (protected from web access), a random table prefix, PHP sessions, the Super User. Only the site's name, the administrator's email address and username are needed (`--site-name`, `--admin-email`, `--admin-username`); when one isn't given, it's asked for ("What is the site's name?"), and asked again when the answer isn't valid (the web installer's rules); without a terminal it stops with an error instead. The administrator's password is generated (16 letters, both cases, and digits, with at least one of each) and shown at the end, or returned with `--format=json`. The optional `--admin-password` sets it instead (never asked for; handy for a development site): taken as given, like the web installer's field (any non-empty value, spaces kept), and not repeated at the end (JSON returns it, with `adminPasswordGenerated` false). The optional `--sample-data=<set>` also installs one of the installer's sample data sets, named after their files: `blog`, `brochure`, `default` (the installer's default set) or `learn` (`none` for none; without a name it asks which, in a terminal); it's installed where the web installer does it, after the tables and before the configuration. Without the option there's no sample data and no question. Like the web installer, it checks that whoever installs can change the site's files: the installer writes a file in the `installation` folder, which the command deletes. Afterwards it removes the `installation` folder, as the web installer does. It warns when run as root, or as another user than the one owning the site's files (the web server must be able to write to the database). `--dry-run` shows what it would do. Before installation, `cli/joomla.php` only runs this command (others say how to install), without loading plugins. Not in Joomla 4+. Tested with PHP 7.4 and 8.5, from another folder, with prompts (including an invalid answer), JSON, a refusal on an installed site, an `installation` folder it can't write to, and a backend login with the generated password; and each sample data set, with its home page, the pages it links to and a backend login checked over HTTP.
  - `database:optimize`: rebuilds the site's tables without their unused space and updates the statistics queries are planned with: `OPTIMIZE TABLE` on MySQL/MariaDB (InnoDB tables are rebuilt and analysed), `VACUUM ANALYZE` on PostgreSQL (`--full` for `VACUUM FULL`, which gives the space back to the system but locks each table meanwhile) and `VACUUM` and `ANALYZE` on SQLite (the whole file; the write-ahead log is checkpointed afterwards, as `VACUUM` otherwise leaves a second copy of the database in it). `--analyze` only updates the statistics, `--table` limits it to one table; it shows the sizes before and after. Not in Joomla 4+. Verified on MySQL 8.4, PostgreSQL 18 and SQLite.
  - `extension:reinstall`: installs an extension again over itself, so that every file of its package is written again, e.g. to make sure an extension's files match the originals on a site which may have been hacked. By default the package comes from the extension's update site, in the version installed (following collection feeds, with the site's download key, and checked against the SHA-256/384/512 checksum the feed gives); `--file` (a package or an extracted folder) or `--url` give it instead, with `--sha256` to check it against a known checksum. The package must be the same extension (type, element, client and plugin group, as the installer works them out) and the same version unless `--allow-version-change` is given (from the update site, the latest compatible version when the installed one isn't offered any more). Afterwards it lists the files in the extension's folders (administrator, site and media for components, packages' extensions) which the package doesn't have, i.e. files the installation didn't write, such as an added backdoor, and `--remove-extra` deletes them. Core extensions are refused (`core:update --reinstall` restores those). Not in Joomla 4+. Verified with a test module and component on their own update feed: a tampered file restored and an added file found and removed, the installed version chosen over a newer one, refusals of another extension's package, another version, a wrong checksum and a core extension, and download keys kept out of the User Actions Log.
  - `site:health`: one report of the site's health (PHP version and its security support, PHP extensions, database version, database structure, pending Joomla and extension updates as last checked, debug and error reporting, the installation folder, `configuration.php` permissions, the temporary and logs folders, where a SQLite database is, session and cache handlers, Force HTTPS and Little WAF), each check `ok`, `info`, `warning` or `error` with what to do; `--strict` fails on errors (for monitoring).
  - Logs: `log:list` lists the log files (Global Configuration's logs folder, and PHP's error log when it's a file), and `log:tail` shows the last entries of one, split into their fields, filtered by `--priority`, `--category`, `--grep` and `--since`. Only those files, by name, can be read. `actionlog:list` shows the User Actions Log (who did what and when), filtered by `--user`, `--extension` and `--since`.
  - The User Actions Log records every command run which may have changed the site (not dry runs, nor commands which only read, nor the cache, session and indexing housekeeping of cron jobs), with its arguments and secret values (passwords, keys) left out, as the user `cli`: e.g. "Command line: config:set caching=1", or "MCP: article:create ..." for changes made through the MCP server.
  - `mcp:serve`: an MCP (Model Context Protocol) server on the standard input and output, so AI assistants (e.g. Claude Code or Claude Desktop, also over SSH) can use the commands as tools, each with a JSON Schema of its arguments and options, read-only/destructive hints and the JSON results. It's read-only by default: the assistant can use the commands which only read, and dry runs of the others, but can't change the site. `--allow-write` offers every command; `--allow`/`--deny` narrow them down (e.g. `--allow="article:*,category:*,site:*"`), and `--as` fixes the account the content commands act as, so its permissions apply whatever the assistant asks. Options which name files or folders on the server (`--text-file`, `extension:install --path`, `core:update --file`, `database:export --folder` etc.) or reveal secrets (`--show-secrets`) aren't offered; through MCP, `database:export` writes to, and `database:import` reads from, the site's protected backup folder. Anything which puts code on the server needs `--allow-code` too: installing, updating or reinstalling extensions, updating Joomla, writing `configuration.php` (`config:set`, `database:convert`), writing a template's code (PHP and other server scripts, XML and dot files such as `.htaccess`, in any spelling: double extensions such as `file.php.txt`, names ending in a dot or space, which are refused anyway) or restoring a template backup. With `--allow-write` alone, an assistant can change content, template options and a template's CSS, JavaScript and images, and only dry-run the rest. Dry runs through MCP never download anything (`extension:install --url`, `extension:reinstall` plan the download instead), and `database:convert` through MCP leaves no copy of the database behind. Speaks the protocol versions 2024-11-05 to 2025-11-25. Tested with a scripted client: the handshake, the tool list, calls, refusals in read-only mode, dry runs, unknown tools and methods, `--allow`, `--as` (an Editor account), on PHP 7.4 and 8.5.
  - The content commands, `--dry-run` and the MCP server were tested on SQLite and PostgreSQL 18 sites, on PHP 7.4 and 8.5 with every PHP message reported: a suite of 47 command runs (creating, changing, listing, publishing, trashing and deleting each kind of item, permissions of an Editor account, refusals and missing items), with the site's front end showing the results.
  - Name filters work the same in every command which has one: the wildcards `*` (any characters) and `?` (one character) make a pattern, otherwise the name must match exactly; case is ignored. `list` explains them and ends with practical examples, and `help <group>` (or just the group, e.g. `php cli/joomla.php user`) lists the commands of a group, e.g. `user:*`.
  - Third-party extensions only run in the commands that need them (e.g. content and finder plugins in `finder:index`, user plugins in `user:*`). Their PHP notices go to stderr, so they never mix into a command's output or JSON, and their exceptions and errors make only that command fail, with exit code 1. A broken console plugin can't stop the core commands from working. `finder:index` presents its work as a request for the site's home page (based on the configured Live Site, if any), since content plugins often read the request's server variables, which the command line doesn't have; the old script left them unset, and the URL paths pointing at `/cli`.
  - The command line uses an in-memory session, so commands never touch the session storage. They work with an empty or broken database, e.g. to restore one with `database:import`, and the old `cli/` scripts no longer leave a session row behind on every cron run. `finder:index` (and `finder_indexer.php`) also removes the session row its site application registers, which showed up as a guest in Who's Online.
  - The existing scripts in `cli/` (`finder_indexer.php`, `update_cron.php`, `garbagecron.php`, `sessionGc.php`, `sessionMetadataGc.php`, `deletefiles.php`) still exist and take the same options, so cron jobs keep working, but now run the matching new command. They now exit with a non-zero code when the command fails. `finder_indexer.php --pause` with no value now uses the documented default (dynamic pausing) instead of a fixed 1-second pause, and `garbagecron.php` now also removes the administrator's expired cache, not only the site's.

---
- **Hammond**, a news and magazine template (`templates/hammond`), the default template of new sites without sample data or with the News sample data. Existing sites get it installed, not as their default. Plain CSS (Grid and Flexbox, organised by section) and plain JavaScript, system font stacks (no web fonts, so any language works), inline SVG icons, light design; made for Core Web Vitals (sized images, lazy loading below the fold, the main story's image fetched first, nothing that shifts the layout):
  - **Frontpage made of modules:** a 12-column grid (`frontpage` position; module classes `grid-col-span-N` and `grid-row-span-N`, 1432px wide: 90px columns, 32px gutters) of Articles - Category modules with Hammond's layouts: main story, latest news, cards (optionally with a large first card), opinion columns with the authors' initials, a section with a lead story, and a ranked "most read" list; `skip-N` skips articles another module shows. Between them, ad placeholders. It replaces the home page's component output; without modules there the home page shows the featured articles.
  - **Header:** a sticky header which shrinks to the navigation bar with a small logo, trending tags, the date, social links, and a "Menu / Search" button opening a large panel with the search form and the menus over the page (it closes with its button, Escape or a click outside).
  - **Views:** category, archive, featured and tag lists in one layout, the article (category, title, standfirst, byline with reading time, share links which open a centered popup, image, text with styled quotes, tables and embedded videos and X posts, tags), info pages centered without the sidebar (the menu set as "Info Pages Menu"), search results and the error page. On phones, lists show their lead article above its text and the others as a thumbnail beside the title.
  - **Readability:** titles of at least 1rem, dates and details of at least 0.8rem, also in crowded blocks.
- **News sample data:** a news site for Hammond, in the installer (first after "None"), `core:install --sample-data=news` and the Control Panel's Sample Data module. 227 articles (placeholder text, fictional authors) in ten sections and an info pages section, with tags, two menus and the frontpage, sidebar, header and footer modules. Some articles have a table, a list, a quote, an image, a YouTube video or an X post matching their section. Its 240 images (CC0, credited in `docs/sample-data/news/CREDITS.md`) are bundled, as WebP, in `images/sampledata/news` (about 11 MB). The articles keep their timing: on installation the dates move forward so that the latest article is 25 minutes old, instead of all getting the installation's time. From the Sample Data module, the set is added next to the site's content (its own menus, `News Menu` and `News Company Menu`) and a new "Hammond - News" template style becomes the site's default template. The SQL files and the module's data are generated by the scripts in `docs/sample-data/build/`.
- **Finch**, a blog template (`templates/finch`), the default template of new sites with the Blog sample data. Existing sites get it installed, not as their default. Built like Hammond (plain CSS and JavaScript, system font stacks, inline SVG icons, CSS organised by section), with a colourful journal design: plum ink on white, very large type and square shapes, a finch on a yellow disc for a logo.
  - **Topic colours:** each topic (category) has one of five tones (lavender, sunflower, coral, mint, sky), on its page's header band, its posts' labels, the coloured block each post's image sits on, their links and their quotes. Site owners choose it with a class in the topic menu item's Page Class (`tone-lavender`, `tone-sunflower`, `tone-coral`, `tone-mint`, `tone-sky`; the template's options explain it); topics without one get a tone picked by the category's ID. The class also colours the header band of other pages (e.g. a search menu item).
  - **Colour Scheme** (template option): Automatic (the visitor's device setting, the default), Light or Dark; the browser's colour bar and native controls follow it.
  - **Greeting** (template option): the words the home page opens with, set across the page (e.g. "Hi, I'm Finch."), with the tagline under it; the blog's name when empty.
  - **Printing** (both templates): an article (or any page) prints on A4 with the site's logo and the content only: no menus, modules, sidebar, sharing links, tags, author box, post navigation, footer or embedded videos; the text full width at print sizes, paragraphs, list items, quotes, images and tables never split between pages, headings kept with what follows, at least three lines of a paragraph at the end or start of a page, and the address of each link of the text printed after it.
  - **Lean page files** (both templates): `index.php`, `component.php` and the new `offline.php` start with one call to the template's helper (`HammondHelper::prepare($this)`, `FinchHelper::prepare($this)`), which sets up the page's head and tells the markup what kind of page it is; the logo, the social links and the icons come from the helper too, so the files are mostly markup and easy to start a new template from. The print and popup view now gets the viewport and theme colour meta tags and the icon like the other pages. The error page keeps writing its head itself (every value in it escaped). The layout overrides load the helper from their own template's folder, and only when no page file loaded it already, so a copy of the template (Templates: Copy Template) works, also with modules whose layout names the original template (as the sample data's do): the copy's pages failed with "Cannot redeclare class". Checked by copying each template and viewing its pages.
  - **Offline page** (both templates): `offline.php` in the template's style, with the logo, the offline message (Global Configuration's, the standard one or none, as set there), the offline image when set, and the administrators' login form (with the two-factor field when a method is enabled). Joomla's plain system offline page was shown before.
  - **Lazy images** (both templates): every image and embedded frame (videos, posts) loads lazily, also those in article texts, category descriptions and modules, except the top of each view: the frontpage's main story, the first item of every list page (the first row of Finch's grid; page 2 and later, the archive and tag pages too, where it had been lazy), the article's main image, or the first image of its text when it has none; these load at once with high priority. The all-tags page's image too. The attributes are added by reading the HTML as browsers do (tags and their quoted or unquoted attribute values, comments, and the contents of `script`, `style`, `textarea`, `iframe` and similar elements are skipped as a whole), never by a pattern over the text: markup inside an attribute value, e.g. a link's `title`, can't be turned into a real tag (a first version during 3.17's development could, which an author could have used for a script). Checked in Chrome against 23,000 generated pieces of HTML: the page's structure is the same before and after, but for the added attributes.
  - **Social links** (both templates): the social profile options default to `#`, so the icons show on a new site (Finch's were all empty, Hammond's had `#` only with the News sample data); replace them with the real addresses in the template's options. RSS stays empty: then it links to the site's own feed. Only web addresses (`http`, `https`) and paths become links: a `javascript:` or `data:` address (in any spelling browsers accept) is left out.
  - **The site's own CSS** (both templates): when `css/custom.css` exists in the template's folder, the template loads it after `css/template.css` (on every page, the print/popup view and the error page, with the same cache busting), so its rules can change anything. The file isn't part of the templates, so no kind of update (Joomla Update, uploading the files, the command line) ever touches it; the template's options page explains it, and it can be created in Templates: Templates, by FTP or with `template:file:set`.
  - **No jQuery, Bootstrap or MooTools** (both templates): their pages load only the template's own script. Joomla 3 had added jQuery 1.12 with Migrate and noConflict and Bootstrap's JavaScript (about 137 KB, 46 KB compressed, render-blocking in the head) to every list and article page, for Bootstrap tooltips: the page links (`Pagination::getPagesLinks()`, hard-coded in the core class) and the Page Navigation plugin's Prev/Next links. The templates render the page links themselves (`HammondHelper::pagination()`/`FinchHelper::pagination()`, from the pagination's data, with the current page marked `active` and `aria-current`, which the core markup lacked), and override the plugin's layout with nothing (they show previous/next from its data). Nothing is blocked: an extension that asks for jQuery or Bootstrap still gets them, on its pages. Pages that still need them in core, none linked from the sample sets: the login, password reset and username reminder forms (form validation, Bootstrap popovers on labels). Verified by crawling a News site (479 pages) and a Blog site (71 pages).
  - **All tags** (`com_tags` tags view, both templates): the list of tags as cards in the template's style (two columns on phones), with the component's options (page description and image, tag descriptions, images and hits, the title filter and pagination). The stock layout loaded jQuery for the old captions script (`behavior.caption`, which turns `img.caption` titles into captions) and used inline scripts and Bootstrap markup; the filter is now a plain form.
  - **Joomla's other pages** (both templates): a "Joomla core" section of the CSS styles the stock layouts of the components and modules the templates don't override (Joomla 3's Bootstrap 2 markup, without Bootstrap's CSS), so that they fit in when a site uses them: the user pages (login, logout, registration, password reset and username reminder, profile and its form), contacts, news feeds, the content categories and category list, Smart Search, privacy requests, front-end article editing and site configuration, and the stock modules (login, latest, popular, newsflash, categories, archive, related, statistics, who's online, latest users, feed, Smart Search, similar and popular tags, syndication, footer, random image, menu, wrapper, banners). Forms get the templates' fields and buttons (side by side labels on wide screens), fieldsets a title instead of a frame, the profile and contact details a two-column list, lists and tables the templates' rules, and Chosen's select boxes the look of the other fields. Bootstrap's scripted parts work when an extension loads Bootstrap's JavaScript: tabs (the article form showed all its tabs' fields at once), collapsible panels, dropdowns, modals, tooltips and popovers. Nothing else changes: the pages the templates style themselves were compared before and after, pixel by pixel.
  - **Lists** (the home page, topics, tags, archive): one post after another, each with a large date stamp, its topic, title, excerpt and reading time, and its image; the newest (on a list's first page) on a band of its topic's colour. Topic and tag pages open with a band and their name set large. The `sidebar` position's modules (e.g. an about box, popular posts in order, a tag cloud) sit side by side on a plum band under the posts.
  - **Posts:** a header band in the topic's colour (topic, a very large title, the standfirst, byline with reading time), the image overlapping its lower edge, the text in a 680px reading column (serif), quotes on a block of the topic's colour, lists, tables and embeds, then tags, share links (centered popups), an author box (the template's "Author Bio") and links to the previous and next posts, from the "Content - Page Navigation" plugin. Under them, the `below-content` position (e.g. "Read next" cards).
  - **Pages:** an article with a menu item of its own (e.g. About) is shown as a page: title and text only.
  - A search panel which opens over the page from the navigation bar (no layout shift), search results, the error page; on phones, the menu scrolls sideways.
- **Blog sample data:** a personal blog for Finch, replacing the stock Blog set, in the installer, `core:install --sample-data=blog` and the Control Panel's Sample Data module. 20 essays (placeholder text, made from the News set's opinion articles) by one fictional author, Finch Hartley ("Hi, I'm Finch."), in four topics, each in its own colour, a post every few days, with tags, about, contact and privacy pages, a main and a footer menu, and the sidebar and "Read next" modules. It reuses the News set's images (no images of its own). From the module, a new "Finch - Blog" template style becomes the site's default template. Both sets are generated by `docs/sample-data/build/build.sh`. No sample data set uses Protostar any more; it stays, for backwards compatibility.
- **Rookwood**, a template for studios, agencies and companies (`templates/rookwood`), the default template of new sites with the Studio sample data. Existing sites get it installed, not as their default. Built like Hammond and Finch (a helper with `prepare()`, lean page files, `css/custom.css`, lazy images, its own pagination, no jQuery, Bootstrap or MooTools), with a bold design: a dark and a light theme (dark by default, an option of the template; a switch in the header lets each visitor choose, remembered in the browser and set before the page is drawn, so it never flashes the other theme), vivid colours, large type from system font stacks, solid-edged buttons and cards with hard offset shadows, organic shapes and curved section edges.
  - **Full-width sections:** every module in the `frontpage`, `above-content` and `below-content` positions is a band as wide as the window, its content centred at most 1400px wide, unless its class suffix holds `section-full` (heroes and image bands draw their own width). Further suffix classes colour it (`tone-alt`, `tone-accent`, `tone-lime`) or give it a curved top edge (`divider-wave`). A module showing one category gets a "View all" link to it.
  - **Two category layouts of the template** (menu item type Category Blog, or the menu item's Layout option): **Showcase** (case studies, products, services: one panel per article with its image as the canvas of the text, or a grid of large cards with more columns) and **Mosaic** (a journal: groups of seven on a four-column grid, a large article with its image as the canvas taking three quarters, two cards beside it and four below, every other group mirrored; best with 14 articles a page). The other lists (category blog, featured, archive, tags) are grids of cards.
  - **Articles:** labels (category and tags), a large title, the intro as a standfirst, a byline with reading time, a wide image, the text in a readable column with styled quotes, a case study's results as large figures (`ul.figures`) and its client and services (`dl.facts`), then previous and next. An article with a menu item of its own (e.g. Studio, Contact) is a page: its image full width with the title over it, then the text, whose `.wide` blocks (team, values, contact cards) may be wider than the column.
  - **The header's button** is a menu in the `header-action` position (e.g. a Menu Item Alias of the contact page with the Link CSS Style `btn btnPrimary`), shown in the toolbar and in the menu on phones: no menu item IDs in the template's options. Menus of buttons can also be placed in Custom modules with `{loadposition ...}`, as the Studio set's hero does.
  - **Menu items hidden in the header only:** a link with the Link CSS Style `hideInNav` isn't shown in the main navigation but stays in other menus, e.g. Home (the logo leads home) in the footer's menu showing the same items. Joomla's "Display in Menu" option would hide it everywhere.
  - **Search:** a search button in the header opens a panel under it (over the page, so nothing moves) with the modules of the new `search` position, e.g. Search (`mod_search`); Escape, the button or a click outside it closes it, and on phones opening it closes the menu. The search page has the template's look: the list header with the words searched for and the number of results, a large search field, the search options folded away, and the results numbered one under another, each with its section, title, text and date, the searched words marked. The template's own `.highlight` (gradient text in headings) no longer turns the words com_search marks with that class into gradient text there.
  - A footer with a headline and an email address (template options), menus, the social links and back to top; the error and offline pages in the same style.
- **Studio sample data:** a design and technology studio for Rookwood ("Rookwood Studio"), in the installer, `core:install --sample-data=studio` and the Control Panel's Sample Data module. 9 case studies (with real copy) and 16 journal posts (placeholder text) by four fictional authors, studio, contact, privacy and accessibility pages, a Search module in the header, and a home page of sections (hero, clients, services, selected work, the studio, figures, a quote, the latest posts). Ten CC0 images at 1920px, with 960px copies for phones (`images/sampledata/studio`, credited in `docs/sample-data/studio/CREDITS.md`); the journal reuses the News set's images. The home page's buttons are menus of Menu Item Aliases loaded with `{loadposition}`, so they keep pointing at the right pages when the set is added to a site whose menu items have other IDs: the Sample Data plugin now adds Menu Item Aliases, pointing them at the set's own menu items (or, for the set's Home, at the site's own home page: the set's menu gets a Home link without a second default page), and the build script exports menu links to a template's layouts (`layout=rookwood:showcase`) with the category's alias, which it had left with the build site's ID.
- **Footer text** (Hammond, Finch, Rookwood): the option stays plain text, printed escaped, and "Joomla 3.x UTD" in it becomes a link to the distribution's site, https://j3xutd.joomlaworks.net (`<Helper>::footerText()`, which links the phrase in the escaped text). The sample sets now say they were made for demonstrating Joomla 3.x UTD, not Joomla, in their footers and on the Studio set's contact page (a link there too), since this is a distribution, not an official Joomla release.
- New sites without sample data show the Breadcrumbs module without its title, as Protostar's position did; Hammond and Finch style the breadcrumb trail.

### Improvements
- Options in the administrator (Isis): the "spacer" fields which extensions use to organise their options stand out. A spacer with text (a heading, e.g. the User - Profile plugin's two groups, or a menu item's "If a field is left blank, global settings will be used.") is a band across the form, a pale blue background with a navy edge, as the TinyMCE plugins' headings; a spacer drawn as a line is a full-width 2px rule instead of a faint 380px one. Both were squeezed into the labels' column. Right-to-left languages get the band's edge on the right.
- The editor is taller by default: 800px instead of 550px, for TinyMCE 8 and, on new sites, the legacy TinyMCE 4. Both keep their "HTML Height" option (pixels such as `800px`, or any CSS length such as `80vh`); sites which saved the legacy plugin's settings keep the height they have. In both plugins' settings, the options which were on a separate "Advanced" tab (height, width, number of sets), easy to miss, are now on the main tab, under the headings "Editor Size" and "Toolbars and Options" (the sets' builder follows). Each set's options, a list of about 30 fields, are grouped too, each group led by a band in the administrator's colours (a pale blue background with a navy edge) holding its heading and a line saying what it covers: Who Uses This Set, Look, Editing Area, Content Styles, Language, Images, Writing and Pasting, HTML Output, Allowed HTML, Extending the Editor. "Template CSS Classes" and "Custom CSS Classes" are now "Template Styles" and "Custom Stylesheet", as they choose the editor's stylesheet. Saved settings are unchanged.
- **The editor shows the text as the site does** (Hammond, Finch, Rookwood): each template has a `css/editor.css`, which the editors load into the editing area for the site's default template (TinyMCE 8, the legacy TinyMCE 4 and JCE all look for it; without one, Joomla's `templates/system/css/editor.css` gave every article Tahoma at 12px). It holds the template's article text rules, written for the editor's page: its reading column, fonts, headings, links, quotes, lists, tables, figures, embeds and, for Rookwood, the results figures, client and services, values, team and contact cards, with the wide blocks across the editor. Rookwood's follows the device's light or dark setting. The Read More and page break markers stay visible as dashed lines. Their plain class selectors (e.g. `ul.figures`, `ul.team`) also appear in the editor's Formats menu. TinyMCE 8 now links the stylesheet with its modification time (`editor.css?t=…`), so browsers don't keep an old copy after an update.
- New administrator login page (Isis): the site's name in large type on a gradient with a "Visit the site" button (same tab), and a clean form with visible labels, "Forgot your username?"/"Forgot your password?" links next to the fields (instead of tooltip-only icons), a show/hide password button, the language list as a native select, and messages that can be closed. The keyboard starts in the username field and goes to the password, then the log in button, and only then to the other fields (secret key, language), the help links and the link to the site, so a login takes just Tab and Enter. It follows the system's dark mode, reflows for phones and tablets (the gradient over the top half and the form in the middle of the screen), and respects reduced motion. Plain CSS and vanilla JavaScript: the page no longer loads jQuery, Bootstrap or Chosen, nor scripts for old Internet Explorer versions. The template's Login Logo and Login Background Colour settings still apply. The form keeps mod_login's field names and IDs; its new layout is an Isis override (`html/mod_login/default.php`), so other administrator templates keep theirs. The link back to the site keeps HTTPS: it switched to `http` unless the whole site forced HTTPS.
- Post-installation Messages (System > Post-installation Messages, linked from the Control Panel) now show what's new in this distribution: a "What's New in Joomla 3.x UTD" message for every release from 3.11 to 3.17 (newest on top), summarising it with its release date and links to the new features, for new sites and for existing sites updating to 3.17 (once; hiding one keeps it hidden). Stock Joomla's messages from 3.2 to 3.10 (two-factor authentication, beginners' welcome, statistics, update cache time, Joomla 4 pre-checks, reCAPTCHA v1, User Actions Log, privacy, .htaccess changes and others), which described features as new that have been part of Joomla 3 for years, are gone: new sites don't get them, and updating to 3.17 removes them (messages of other extensions stay). Each future release will add its own message. The Control Panel's notice says so in friendlier words ("You have post-installation messages – see what's new in this release!" instead of "important post-installation messages that require your attention"), the messages page's sidebar is titled "News from Joomla!", and its heading is just "Post-installation Messages" (the extension is chosen below it).
- SQL Server and Azure SQL tested for the first time by this project: SQL Server 2022 with both drivers ("sqlsrv" and "sqlazure"), on PHP 8.4 with Microsoft's PHP drivers 5.13 and ODBC Driver 18. A fresh installation with the News sample data, every administrator page, creating, editing, copying and deleting content, categories, tags, fields, users, groups, access levels, menus, modules and template styles, Global Configuration, Smart Search and search, a crawl of the front end, and the command line commands were checked (the fixes above came out of that). Unlike the other drivers, SQL Server runs every statement of a query string (T-SQL needs no `;` between statements, so a driver can't prevent it). The command line's `database:export`, `database:import`, `database:convert` and `database:optimize` don't support SQL Server.
- New sites show the site's name after every page title (Global Configuration's "Site Name in Page Titles" set to "After"), so the home page is "Home - My Site" instead of a bare "Home" in browser tabs, bookmarks and search results. Set by the installer and `core:install`; existing sites keep their setting.
- New sites list 50 items per page (Global Configuration's "Default List Limit", used by the administrator's managers and as the default of lists elsewhere) instead of 20. Set by the installer and `core:install` (and `configuration.php-dist`); existing sites keep their setting.
- The "System - Joomla! Statistics" plugin is installed disabled on new sites, and turned off on every update and reinstall (also when restoring uninstalled core extensions): joomla.org's statistics don't cover this distribution, so its request for permission to send them was pointless. The plugin stays, for statistics of this distribution in the future.
- The installer (for new sites) removes the `installation` folder by itself (see also the security fixes above): the final page says it will, and following its "Site" or "Administrator" link (also at the end of the extra languages steps) removes the folder, shows that it was removed and then opens the page. The manual "Remove installation folder" button is gone. Until the folder is removed, anyone who can reach the site can run the installer, which has no login, against it, so it no longer depends on the site admin remembering. The removal is refused unless it's a POST with the form's token, Joomla is installed (a `configuration.php` exists), and the folder is the installer's own, a real folder (not a link) directly in the site's root; nothing in the request chooses what's deleted. PHP's opcache is cleared for the removed scripts. If the removal fails, the page says so and asks to delete the folder manually; the links then work as normal links. Verified in Chrome on PHP 7.4 and 8.5: the folder, `joomla.xml` and `robots.txt.dist` handled, the redirect to the administrator login, and refusals before installation, without the token, with GET, for a symlinked folder and when a file can't be deleted.
- The installer no longer has FTP options. Its FTP step (shown only when `configuration.php` couldn't be written), FTP settings and the FTP layer used to write `configuration.php` are removed; when the file can't be written, the final page shows its contents to save by hand, as it already did when the FTP write failed. New sites get Joomla's FTP settings empty and disabled; Global Configuration still has them for sites which need them.
- The installer (for new sites) has a new design: an app-style layout with a deep blue sidebar (the Joomla! logo as inline SVG, the install steps as a progress track, and the Joomla!/GPL notice at its bottom) and the content beside it, panels, gradient buttons, pill-shaped switches, sample data as cards, a large percentage while installing and a celebratory finish; on smaller screens the sidebar becomes a header and the steps a strip. The installer no longer shows Joomla's version (one less detail for fingerprinting). The HTML is XHTML-style HTML5 (void elements closed, boolean attributes with values), properly nested and indented, including what Joomla's form field layouts output (normalised by the installer: whitespace, named entities as characters); every page and step of an installation parses as well-formed XML. It no longer loads anything from the rest of Joomla: no Bootstrap, jQuery, jQuery Migrate, Chosen or Joomla core scripts (18 files before; now its own stylesheet and script only). Same steps and the same requests as before (apart from the FTP step, removed later in this version). Its stylesheet uses CSS Grid and Flexbox, a light and a dark colour scheme (following the system's), logical properties for right-to-left languages (replacing a separate RTL stylesheet), and works from phone to desktop widths. Its script is plain JavaScript (fetch, data attributes instead of inline handlers, the browser's own form validation on the fields shown), and the template writes its own page head. Sample data choices show their descriptions, the installation shows each task's progress, and messages appear inside the page. Verified in Chrome (headless, through its DevTools protocol) with complete installations on SQLite and PostgreSQL on PHP 7.4 and 8.5: no requests outside the installation folder, no JavaScript errors and no PHP notices with all errors reported; validation, error messages (wrong database password, mismatched passwords), the SQLite fields, the extra languages steps, a right-to-left language (Arabic) and a phone-sized screen.
- The backend Site Information module and the frontend Statistics module show the web server, after the operating system: its name and version in the backend (e.g. "Apache 2.4.58", "nginx 1.24.0", "IIS 10.0"; servers set to hide their version give only the name), only the name on the frontend. It's read from `SERVER_SOFTWARE` without the details servers add (operating system, modules), by the new `JUtility::getWebServer()`.
- The installer offers three sample data sets, after "None": **News**, **Blog** and **Studio** (all new, see below). The Brochure, Default and Learn sets are removed: they add little to a new site (the Learn set's "Fruit Shop" and "Parks" sections were built for the Beez3 template, removed from this distribution), and their files had fallen behind `joomla.sql`, e.g. without the Control Panel's newer modules, and with styles of the removed Beez3 and Hathor templates. Their images (`images/sampledata/fruitshop`, `images/sampledata/parks`) are no longer in the package; updated sites keep theirs, which their content may use.
- The "Sample Data - Blog" plugin is now "Sample Data", and installs both sets from the Control Panel's Sample Data module: News first, then Blog. Its stock blog content (six articles in language strings) gives way to the new Blog set, from the same data as the installer's. The module shows each set with an "Install" button (a plugin can offer several sets now; one which offers one works as before) and its script is plain JavaScript (`fetch()`, no jQuery), which also ignores the empty answers of other sample data plugins instead of failing on them.
- The Sample Data module replaces the installed set instead of adding a second one: installing a set after another one (or the same one again) had added all of its content next to the first, with two sets of menus, modules and sections. The plugin now records what a set adds (the IDs of its articles, categories, tags it created, menus, menu items, modules and template style; the installer and `core:install` record their set too), and a set's first step removes the recorded set: its articles (with their featured, rating and tag rows, and from Smart Search's index), menu items and the module assignments to them, menus once empty, modules, and its template style (the site's previous default template comes back). The site's own content stays: a category or tag that other content still uses is kept, and so are tags that existed before the set. Before replacing a set, the module warns, naming it, that everything it added is deleted for good, changes included. The installed set is marked "Installed", its button says "Reinstall". The record is kept in the plugin's parameters, in a hidden field so that saving the plugin keeps it.
- The Sample Data module's requests are POST only, with the form token in the request's body instead of the URL (where it ended up in server and proxy logs, and a leaked token would have let a plain link replace the installed sample data). Sample data plugins written for stock Joomla 3, which look for the token in the URL, keep working: the module adds it there too, but only when such a plugin is enabled. A refused step of the News or Blog set now says why (token, permission) instead of "invalid response". A set's description is shown with only a few inline tags (`em`, `strong` and similar, without attributes): it comes from language strings, which language overrides can change, and was output as it was.
- The Sample Data module and the plugin's install steps are for Super Users only: the module was only hidden from others by its access level, and the steps only checked permission to create items in each component, though they now delete a set and always change the site's template.
- The administrator's footer and status bar name the distribution: "Joomla 3.x UTD v3.17.0" instead of "Joomla! 3.17.0" (the Joomla Version module, its "Show Name" option; the version is read from `Version` as before). The name comes from a new constant, `Version::DISTRIBUTION`; `Version::PRODUCT` stays "Joomla!", since update checks match it against the update feeds' target platform ("joomla") and the user agent and Install from Web send it. To make room, the status bar shows Visitors and Messages as icons with their counts (the words stay for screen readers, and the tooltips give e.g. "2 Visitors"; SVG files in Isis' new `images/svg` folder, painted in the bar's text colour), and "Administrators" is now "Admins": the bar stays on one line down to 768px wide, where it used to wrap below 820px.
- `config:set` names what it set: its success message lists each option with its new and previous value (e.g. `[OK] Configuration set: caching = 2 (was 0); cachetime = 30 (was 15).`), or "(unchanged)", so consecutive runs can be told apart; it only said "Configuration set.". Secret options (passwords, keys) and values too long or with markup for one line of text (e.g. an HTML offline message) are named without their value ("smtppass changed"). With `--format=json`, `data.options` lists every option with `value`, `previous` and `changed` (secrets as `***`).

### Security fixes
- Installer (for new sites): the check which makes a remote database prove the site is yours (delete a file the installer creates) could be skipped. The database step stored the posted settings before checking them, and the step which writes `configuration.php` and creates the Super User never checked them again, so a request could go straight to it: an exposed, not yet installed site could be installed against a database someone else controls, giving them a Super User account. The step now applies the same checks as the database step before writing anything. Confirmed against the code before the fix (stock Joomla 3 behaves the same).
- Installer: once Joomla was installed, anyone could run the installer again while the `installation` folder was still there, from a new browser, overwriting the live `configuration.php` and creating another Super User (stock Joomla 3 behaviour; Joomla relies on the folder being removed). Now only the browser which installed the site can go on (for the extra languages steps); anyone else gets a page saying Joomla is installed, whose links remove the installer, and every other request is refused. Verified: every view and step from another browser, the installing browser's languages step, and the links removing the folder.
- Module ordering list (`task=module.orderPosition`, also reachable from the front end): with `module_id=0` its access check let anyone through, so any visitor, including a guest with the token of a front-end form, could list the titles and order of the modules in any position, administrator and unpublished modules included (stock Joomla 3). It now requires the permissions of the edit form the list belongs to: editing (or changing the state of) that module, or creating modules for a new one. Confirmed against the code before the fix.
- Installer: fixed the Super User's group being set with an `UPDATE` without a condition when the user's group mapping already existed, which put every user of the site into the Super Users group. The mapping is now replaced for that user only.
- `{loadmoduleid}` (Content - Load Modules) showed any module, whatever its access level, publishing dates, client or language, since 3.12: the change which let it show modules not assigned to the current menu item (`ModuleHelper::getModuleById()`) dropped those checks too. An author could put `{loadmoduleid 123}` in an article and show guests a Registered module, an unpublished-by-date one or an administrator module. The module must again be a site module the visitor's access levels include, within its publishing dates and in the page's language (with the language filter on); menu assignments are still ignored, as intended. `{loadmodule}` and `{loadposition}` were not affected. Verified with modules of each kind, as a guest and as a registered user.
- Joomla Update's extraction script (`restore.php`, stock Joomla 3) wrote each entry of the update package where its name pointed: a package entry named `../file.php` or with an absolute name was written outside the site's folder, and link entries could point anywhere. Entries with `..` parts, absolute names (also Windows drive letters) or control characters are now skipped, and so are links (Joomla's packages have none). The package comes from the update server or from a Super User's upload, so this is a defence in depth. Verified with the real extraction code: a crafted ZIP wrote three files outside the site and a link to `/etc` before, and nothing outside it after; a real Joomla package extracts to identical files (6,530) with the old and new script. Joomla Update now also checks the whole package before extracting it (it must be a ZIP file, without such entries or links), so a crafted or wrong package is refused with a message and nothing is changed, instead of being applied with entries left out; verified by uploading a crafted package, a file which isn't a ZIP, and a real package (updated to the end).
- Extension installer (stock Joomla 3): file and folder names, language tags, the media `destination` and `folder`, `<sql>` and `<schemapath>` paths, and the target folders of file extensions were joined to the site's folders as the manifest gave them, so a package could write files outside its own folders (e.g. `<filename>../../../file.php</filename>` into the site's root, or a `files` extension's `target="../.."` outside the site), and uninstalling a package whose manifest named `<media destination="../..">` deleted that whole folder with everything in it. Such paths (with `..` parts) are now refused when installing (the extension's own files, or the whole package for files and package extensions) and skipped when uninstalling. Verified with crafted packages for each way, before and after, and with an ordinary plugin with folders, media and language files, which installs and uninstalls as before. Installing an extension needs a Super User, so this keeps a package to its own folders rather than closing an escalation. A refused path stops the whole installation, which is rolled back with the reason shown: a package is never installed with some of its files quietly left out. Uninstalling skips (and reports) such paths, so a package like that can still be removed.
- Archive extraction (`JArchiveZip`, `JArchiveTar` and the framework's `Zip`/`Tar`, stock Joomla 3): the check that an entry stays inside the destination compared the paths without a separator, so an entry `../tmp_evil/file.php` passed for a destination `.../tmp`. Fixed in all four; verified with ZIP (both readers of each class) and TAR archives.
- The PHP configuration writer (`Joomla\Registry\Format\Php`, which writes `configuration.php`) escaped values but wrote array keys and property names as given, and values inside arrays in double quotes (where PHP expands `{$...}`), so a crafted key or value ended up as PHP code in `configuration.php`. The installer's form accepted an array for a single field (e.g. `jform[site_name][...]`), which reached the writer: whoever completes an installation could run code, which they can as its Super User anyway, so this is defence in depth. Keys and values inside arrays are now written as single-quoted, escaped strings (or numbers), property names that aren't valid PHP names are skipped, and the installer refuses a value posted as a list (a required field then fails validation). `configuration.php` comes out the same for normal settings (which have no arrays), and arrays read back the same values (checked on PHP 7.4 and 8.5).
- Joomla Update (stock Joomla 3): the version name, download address and information link of the update feed were written into the page unescaped, so a feed (a custom update channel, or a compromised server) could add markup and scripts to the screen of the Super User updating, or a `javascript:` link. They're now escaped, and only `http`/`https` addresses become links. Verified with a crafted feed.
- Extensions: Install from URL (stock Joomla 3): the address checked was the one entered, but an update XML file given there could name a download address of any scheme (e.g. `file://`), which was fetched. The download address now has to be `http` or `https` too, and an XML file without a download address no longer causes a PHP 8 warning. Both checks now accept the scheme in any case (`HTTPS://` was refused, also in stock Joomla).
- Install from Web: the address passed to it to install (`installfrom`) was checked with the URL rule's default list of schemes, which includes `file`, `gopher` and others. Only `http` and `https` are now accepted, also for the download address of an XML file.
- FOF's download class (`FOFDownload`, used by FOF 2 extensions; stock Joomla 3) fetched any address its cURL adapter accepts, including `file://` (a file of the server copied into the temporary folder), and wrote a staggered download under the file name the request gave, `../` included. It now only fetches `http`, `https`, `ftp` and `ftps` addresses, and the file stays in the temporary folder under the name's last part. Verified before and after (the old code copied `/etc/hostname` and wrote outside the temporary folder).
- Database queries (all drivers, also FOF's; stock Joomla 3): a limit or offset given to `setQuery()` was written into the SQL as it was passed, so an extension passing request values unchecked would have let them into the query (under PHP 8's comparison rules, `"10 UNION ..."` counts as a positive number). They're now converted to numbers, as in Joomla 4.
- MySQL (PDO) driver ("pdomysql", and FOF's copy; stock Joomla 3): PDO's MySQL driver runs every statement of a query string, so an injection anywhere (core or extension) could have added statements of its own (`SELECT 1; INSERT ...` ran both, on MySQL and MariaDB). It now connects with multi-statements off, like the "mysqli" driver, which never ran them. Verified on MySQL 8.4, MariaDB 11.8 and 10.11, PHP 7.4 and 8.5.
- PostgreSQL (native driver, "postgresql", and FOF's copy; stock Joomla 3): queries ran through `pg_query()`, which runs every statement of a string, so an injection anywhere (core or extension) could have added statements of its own (`SELECT 1; INSERT ...` ran both). Queries now run through `pg_query_params()` without parameters, which runs one statement only, like the MySQL drivers and the PDO PostgreSQL driver; the install SQL (including its `soundex()` function), sample data, database checks and `VACUUM` run as before.
- PostgreSQL (native driver and FOF's copy; stock Joomla 3): the connection string was built from the database name, user and password as they were, so a password with a space or a quote couldn't connect, and one such as `x dbname=other` connected to another database, or `x user=other` as another user. The values are now quoted (the host trimmed, so one with a stray space still connects).
- Usernames on PostgreSQL were compared with their case when checking that one isn't taken (stock Joomla 3), so `Admin` could be registered next to `admin`; the user and form checks now compare them (and email addresses) case-insensitively there.
- Little WAF only checked the request's address as one string, for four exact spellings of the tags: a tag in form data (POST), with a single encoded character (`{%73ource}`), with one brace encoded and the other not, encoded twice, or with an option (`{source 0}`) went through, as did every request on servers which don't give `REQUEST_URI` or give it without the query string. It now checks the address (rebuilt as Joomla does when the server doesn't give it), the query string and path, the names and values of GET and POST data and uploaded file names, as they are and percent-decoded up to twice, matching the tags themselves. A filter now only acts while the extension it protects is installed and enabled (its plugin in any group, so a vendor's choice of group can't switch the filter off), as its description always said, and the editor's text posted by logged-in users who may write articles (in the component or any category; a module's content for users who may edit modules) isn't checked, so editing articles with these tags in the front end keeps working; everything else they post is checked. Blocked requests are now logged to their own file (`littlewaf.php` in the administrator's logs folder, with the request as rebuilt where the server doesn't give `REQUEST_URI`, and no longer written once it reaches 10 MB, as blocked requests never reach the log rotation; before, nothing was written unless the Debug plugin logged everything), and a log which can't be written no longer turns the block into an error page. The descriptions were corrected. Verified against the bypasses (all blocked now), false positives (`sourcecode`, `{sources}`, `{modules}`), an unwritable logs folder, and requests without `REQUEST_URI`.
- `database:convert` wrote its copy of the database (password hashes included) into a folder of `tmp/` named after the time (`uniqid()`), with no protection files, inside the site, so the web server could serve it while the conversion ran. It now uses a randomly named folder of the protected backup folder (`backup/<random>/database/`). `database:export` without `--folder`, run from a folder inside the site, now writes there too (outside the site, the current folder as before).
- Database drivers built on PDO ("MySQL (PDO)", "PostgreSQL (PDO)"; stock Joomla 3): the host, port and database name went into the connection string as they were, where `;` separates its settings, so a database name such as `site;dbname=other` connected to another database. Such values are now refused (and the port must be a number).
- Backup folders (`backup/<random>/`): only the last level of a nested folder got its protection files (`.htaccess`, `web.config`, `index.html`), so on a server which lists folders and ignores `.htaccess` the levels between them (e.g. `template-files/site/` and a template's own folders, where `template:file:set` keeps previous versions) could be listed. Every level gets them now.

### Bug fixes
- User Actions Log: the dashboard's Latest Actions module (and a privacy request's list of actions) showed the command line's entries as `COM_ACTIONLOGS_CLI_COMMAND`: only the Actions Log's own pages loaded the language files of an entry's extension. Every place showing log messages now does (`ActionlogsHelper::getHumanReadableLogMessage()`), which also helps extensions logging messages of their own. `actionlog:list` showed Joomla's own entries (logins, saved articles) as language keys: it now loads the Action Log plugins' strings.
- SQLite driver on SQLite older than 3.44 (e.g. Ubuntu 22.04's 3.37.2, the oldest the driver supports): category pages failed with "no such function: CONCAT_WS" (an error 500), and article links came out as `/?id=3:alias&catid=10:category` instead of their address, as the "id:alias" slugs Joomla builds with `CONCAT_WS()` failed. SQLite has `concat_ws()` only from 3.44.0, so it worked on newer servers. The driver now provides it (MySQL's: NULL values skipped). It also provides `SOUNDEX()` (Smart Search's indexer and its "Did you mean") where SQLite was built without it, with SQLite's own codes, and `RIGHT()` works before SQLite 3.39 (where `RIGHT` was only a join keyword, a syntax error). Checked on SQLite 3.37.2 and 3.46.1: the three sample sites (339 pages), 50 administrator screens each, Smart Search indexing and search, the database check, export and optimize.
- TinyMCE plugins' settings (stock Joomla 3): the toolbar builder, and with it every set's options, was indented by the labels' column and ran past the right edge of the page, as it was rendered like a field with a hidden label; the legacy plugin hid it with a negative margin, which pushed it off the screen on phones, where that column isn't there. Both builders now render as a block as wide as the form, on desktops and phones.
- Page 2 and later of a list shown with a template's layout (`layout=template:name`, e.g. Rookwood's Mosaic) linked to the layout without its colon (`templatename`), which doesn't exist: the pagination read the layout with the `CMD` filter. A list reached through its own menu item still showed the right layout, one reached otherwise fell back to the default (stock Joomla 3). Values of the form `template:layout` are now kept as they are.
- Administrator lists (stock Joomla 3): a link with an array as list length or start (`limit[]=1`, `limitstart[]=1`, `list[limit][]=5`) failed with "Unsupported operand types", and the array was kept in the session, so every list failed until logging out (the link needs no form token, so someone else could send it). Such values now fall back to the default length and the first page; front-end lists no longer warn about them either.
- Smart Search on PostgreSQL: with the native driver ("postgresql") no search result could be read (PostgreSQL returns the stored data as hex text, which wasn't decoded), and the indexer stored its results through `pg_escape_bytea()` without a connection (deprecated in PHP 8.1, and depending on the last connection opened) and escaped them twice. Results are now written as hex the server decodes, and read with both drivers, also from indexes written by earlier versions. On sites moved to PostgreSQL with `database:convert`, indexing failed (`ROUND()` on the weight columns, which are `real` there, and the `soundex()` function PostgreSQL's installation adds); the indexer casts the weights, and `database:convert` now adds the function.
- FOF's PostgreSQL driver: a failing query ended in a PHP error ("TypeError") on PHP 8.1+ instead of the exception extensions catch, and it ignored the port (`host:port`, or a separate port), so FOF-based extensions couldn't connect to a server on another port. FOF's MySQL drivers now also give MariaDB's real version, as Joomla's do.
- Users whose usernames or email addresses differ only in case (possible on PostgreSQL and SQLite sites before 3.17) couldn't be saved anymore, as each matched the other; a user's own unchanged username and email address are now never "in use". On SQL Server, usernames are compared ignoring case whatever the database's collation.
- Queries with an offset but no limit on the SQLite driver and FOF's MySQL and SQLite query classes returned no rows (`LIMIT n, 0`); the offset is now ignored, as in Joomla's MySQL drivers. The PostgreSQL driver's row count no longer warns on PHP 8.1+.
- MariaDB 10 reported its version as "5.5.5-10.x.y-MariaDB" through PHP 7 (an old compatibility prefix), so System Information showed it that way; both MySQL drivers now give the real version, as Joomla 4 does. `site:health` names MariaDB as such.
- PostgreSQL (stock Joomla 3): batch copying articles, categories or modules failed with "column "t" of relation "#__assets" does not exist" (the copy's permissions were written with MySQL's `UPDATE ... JOIN` form); they're now read and written in two plain queries, which also works on MySQL and SQLite.
- PostgreSQL native driver (stock Joomla 3): saving Global Configuration failed with "PostgreSQL connection has already been closed": its database check opened a second connection with the same settings, which `pg_connect()` hands out as the site's own, and closed it. Every driver instance now opens a connection of its own. FOF's copy also passed no connection to `pg_query()` (deprecated since PHP 8.1).
- Installer on PostgreSQL (stock Joomla 3): "Backup" for old tables failed on the index named without its table (`#__uc_ItemnameTagid`), and on mixed-case index names, so installing over an earlier installation stopped. Indexes are renamed with quoted names, and one without its table's name gets the backup prefix.
- Modules of the same position and ordering came out in whatever order the database returned them: on PostgreSQL, the frontpage grid of Hammond's News sample data was shuffled (the main story wasn't first). The module ID now breaks ties, giving the order MySQL and SQLite already gave.
- SQLite driver: `REGEXP` ended its pattern at an escaped slash (`\/`) and printed PHP warnings (with the server's paths, where errors are displayed) for a pattern PHP can't compile; the Banners module matches the keywords of the page being viewed this way, which authors write.
